Of course. Here are 250 WASSCE-standard Multiple Choice Questions, with answers and explanations, derived directly from the provided textbook content (Chapters 1-16).

---

### **Chapter 1: Introduction to Economics and Basic Concepts**

**1. According to the widely accepted definition by Lionel Robbins, economics is the science which studies human behavior as a relationship between**
A. wealth and production.
B. ends and scarce means which have alternative uses.
C. production and consumption.
D. government and markets.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Lionel Robbins' definition emphasizes that economics is about making choices (human behavior) regarding unlimited wants (ends) with limited resources (scarce means) that can be used in different ways (alternative uses).

**2. The fundamental economic problem that exists in all societies is**
A. poverty.
B. choice.
C. scarcity.
D. inflation.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Scarcity, the condition where resources are limited relative to unlimited human wants, is the fundamental problem from which all other economic problems, including the need for choice, arise.

**3. The branch of economics that focuses on the economy as a whole, dealing with variables like inflation and unemployment, is called**
A. Normative economics.
B. Microeconomics.
C. Social economics.
D. Macroeconomics.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Macroeconomics studies the aggregate behavior of the entire economy, including national income, inflation, unemployment, and economic growth.

**4. Which of the following is NOT a characteristic of human wants?**
A. They are unlimited.
B. They are always fixed and do not change.
C. They are competitive.
D. They can be complementary.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Human wants are dynamic and vary with time, place, and person. They are not fixed.

**5. A luxury good is best described as**
A. a good essential for survival.
B. a good that makes life more comfortable.
C. a non-essential good desired for status or extreme comfort.
D. a good with a perfectly inelastic demand.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Luxuries are non-essential items that are typically expensive and sought after for status, pleasure, or a very high level of comfort, beyond basic needs and comforts.

**6. The basic economic question of "For whom to produce?" relates to the issue of**
A. production methods.
B. the combination of goods to produce.
C. resource allocation.
D. distribution of income and wealth.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The "For whom to produce?" question addresses how the final output of goods and services is divided among the members of society, which is fundamentally a problem of distribution.

**7. In economics, the term "land" as a factor of production includes**
A. only agricultural fields.
B. only buildings and factory sites.
C. all natural resources provided by nature.
D. the monetary value of property.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** "Land" in economics is a broad term that encompasses all natural resources, including physical land, mineral deposits, water, forests, sunlight, and air.

**8. The reward or income for enterprise as a factor of production is**
A. rent.
B. wages.
C. interest.
D. profit.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Profit is the residual income that accrues to the entrepreneur for organizing other factors of production and bearing the risks of the business venture.

**9. Opportunity cost is defined as the**
A. monetary price paid for a good.
B. value of all alternatives given up.
C. value of the next best alternative foregone.
D. cost of producing one more unit of output.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Opportunity cost represents the real cost of making a choice, which is the benefit lost from the single best alternative that was not chosen.

**10. A point inside the Production Possibility Curve (PPC) represents**
A. an unattainable level of production.
B. allocative efficiency.
C. economic growth.
D. inefficient production or unemployment of resources.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Any point within the PPC indicates that the economy is producing less than its potential, meaning some resources are either idle (unemployed) or not being used efficiently.

**11. An outward shift of the Production Possibility Curve is caused by**
A. a decrease in unemployment.
B. an improvement in technology.
C. producing more of one good and less of another.
D. government setting a price ceiling.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** An outward shift of the PPC signifies economic growth or an increase in productive capacity. This is caused by an increase in the quantity or quality of resources or an improvement in technology.

**12. In a command economic system, the three fundamental economic questions are primarily answered by**
A. individual consumers and producers.
B. a central planning authority.
C. customs and traditions.
D. the forces of supply and demand.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A command economy is characterized by centralized decision-making, where the government or a state agency determines what, how, and for whom to produce.

**13. A key feature of a market economic system is**
A. state ownership of all resources.
B. the dominance of customs.
C. the price mechanism.
D. central planning.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In a market economy, the price mechanism (the forces of supply and demand) is the primary means by which resources are allocated and economic decisions are coordinated.

**14. Most modern economies are described as mixed economies because they**
A. trade with many other countries.
B. have both an agricultural and industrial sector.
C. combine elements of market and command systems.
D. have both public and private limited companies.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A mixed economy combines private ownership and market forces with government intervention and regulation to address market failures and achieve social goals.

**15. The "law of diminishing returns" applies to which factor of production in the short run?**
A. Enterprise
B. Capital
C. Labour
D. Land
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The law states that as more units of a variable factor (like labor) are applied to a fixed factor (like land), the marginal output will eventually decrease. Land is a classic example of a fixed factor.

### **Chapter 2: Demand, Supply, and Market Equilibrium**

**16. The law of demand states that, ceteris paribus, there is**
A. a direct relationship between price and quantity demanded.
B. an inverse relationship between price and quantity supplied.
C. an inverse relationship between price and quantity demanded.
D. a direct relationship between price and quantity supplied.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The law of demand states that as the price of a good rises, the quantity demanded falls, and vice versa, holding all other factors constant.

**17. A change in the price of a good itself will cause**
A. a shift of the demand curve.
B. a shift of the supply curve.
C. a movement along the demand curve.
D. no change in quantity demanded.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A change in the good's own price causes a change in quantity demanded, which is represented as a movement along the existing demand curve. A shift of the curve is caused by a change in a non-price determinant.

**18. If tea and coffee are substitutes, an increase in the price of coffee will lead to**
A. a decrease in the demand for tea.
B. an increase in the demand for tea.
C. an increase in the quantity demanded of coffee.
D. a decrease in the supply of tea.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** If the price of coffee rises, consumers will switch to its substitute, tea, causing the demand for tea to increase (shift to the right).

**19. For an inferior good, an increase in consumer income will cause the demand curve to**
A. shift to the right.
B. shift to the left.
C. become vertical.
D. become horizontal.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** An inferior good is one for which demand decreases as consumer income rises, as consumers switch to better quality alternatives. This is shown as a leftward shift of the demand curve.

**20. A government subsidy to the producers of a good will**
A. shift the supply curve to the left.
B. shift the supply curve to the right.
C. shift the demand curve to the left.
D. shift the demand curve to the right.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A subsidy reduces the cost of production for firms, encouraging them to produce and supply more at every price level. This is represented by a rightward shift of the supply curve.

**21. A price elasticity of demand (PED) of 1.5 indicates that demand is**
A. perfectly inelastic.
B. inelastic.
C. unit elastic.
D. elastic.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** When the absolute value of PED is greater than 1, demand is classified as elastic, meaning quantity demanded is highly responsive to changes in price.

**22. If a 10% increase in the price of a good leads to a 5% decrease in the quantity demanded, the price elasticity of demand is**
A. 0.5 and inelastic.
B. 2.0 and elastic.
C. 0.5 and elastic.
D. 2.0 and inelastic.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** PED = % Change in Quantity Demanded / % Change in Price = 5% / 10% = 0.5. Since 0.5 is less than 1, demand is inelastic.

**23. For a good with inelastic demand, an increase in its price will cause the total revenue of the seller to**
A. increase.
B. decrease.
C. remain unchanged.
D. fluctuate.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** When demand is inelastic, the percentage increase in price is greater than the percentage decrease in quantity demanded, leading to an overall increase in total revenue (P x Q).

**24. If the cross-price elasticity of demand (XED) between two goods is negative, the goods are**
A. substitutes.
B. complements.
C. inferior goods.
D. luxury goods.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A negative XED means that an increase in the price of one good leads to a decrease in the demand for the other, which is the definition of complementary goods (goods used together).

**25. A price floor set above the equilibrium price will result in a**
A. shortage of the good.
B. surplus of the good.
C. black market.
D. new equilibrium.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A price floor is a minimum price. If set above the market-clearing equilibrium price, quantity supplied will exceed quantity demanded, creating a surplus (excess supply).

**26. Consumer surplus is the difference between**
A. what consumers are willing to pay and what they actually pay.
B. the cost of production and the price.
C. quantity demanded and quantity supplied.
D. total revenue and total cost.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Consumer surplus measures the net benefit to consumers, which is the amount they were prepared to pay for a good minus the amount they actually paid (the market price).

**27. Deadweight loss represents**
A. the tax revenue collected by the government.
B. the surplus enjoyed by consumers.
C. the profit earned by producers.
D. the loss of total surplus due to market inefficiency.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Deadweight loss is the reduction in total economic surplus (consumer surplus + producer surplus) that results from a market distortion like a tax, price ceiling, or monopoly.

**28. An improvement in technology used to produce a good will lead to**
A. a lower equilibrium price and a higher equilibrium quantity.
B. a higher equilibrium price and a lower equilibrium quantity.
C. a lower equilibrium price and a lower equilibrium quantity.
D. a higher equilibrium price and a higher equilibrium quantity.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Improved technology shifts the supply curve to the right. Given a downward sloping demand curve, this intersection will occur at a lower price and a higher quantity.

**29. The time horizon is a key determinant of the price elasticity of**
A. supply only.
B. demand only.
C. both supply and demand.
D. neither supply nor demand.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Both demand and supply tend to be more elastic in the long run. Consumers have more time to find substitutes, and producers have more time to adjust their production capacity.

**30. The income elasticity of demand for a necessity is typically**
A. negative.
B. zero.
C. greater than 1.
D. positive and less than 1.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** For a normal good, YED is positive. For a necessity, demand increases as income rises, but less than proportionally, so the YED is between 0 and 1.

### **Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly**

**31. Which of the following is a characteristic of a perfectly competitive market?**
A. A single seller.
B. Significant barriers to entry.
C. A homogeneous product.
D. Product differentiation.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In perfect competition, all firms produce an identical (homogeneous) product, so consumers perceive no difference between the products of different firms.

**32. The demand curve facing an individual firm in a perfectly competitive market is**
A. downward sloping.
B. upward sloping.
C. perfectly inelastic (vertical).
D. perfectly elastic (horizontal).
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** A perfectly competitive firm is a price taker. It can sell as much as it wants at the prevailing market price but nothing if it charges more. This makes its demand curve a horizontal line at the market price.

**33. For a perfectly competitive firm, profit is maximized at the output level where**
A. Price equals Average Total Cost.
B. Marginal Revenue equals Marginal Cost.
C. Total Revenue is at its maximum.
D. Average Total Cost is at its minimum.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The universal profit maximization rule for any firm is to produce at the quantity where MR = MC. For a competitive firm, this is also where P = MC.

**34. In the long run, a firm in perfect competition will earn**
A. supernormal profit.
B. a loss.
C. normal profit.
D. economic rent.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Due to free entry and exit, if firms make supernormal profits, new firms will enter, driving down the price. If they make losses, firms will exit, driving up the price. This process ensures that in the long run, firms only earn normal profit (zero economic profit), where P = min ATC.

**35. A monopolist is a price**
A. taker.
B. maker.
C. leader.
D. follower.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** As the sole seller in the market, a monopolist has significant control over the price and can choose a price-quantity combination along the market demand curve.

**36. For a single-price monopolist, marginal revenue is**
A. equal to price.
B. greater than price.
C. less than price.
D. constant.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** To sell one more unit, a monopolist must lower the price on all units sold (not just the last one), so the additional revenue (MR) from selling that unit is less than its price (AR).

**37. Which of the following is a barrier to entry in a monopolistic market?**
A. A large number of firms.
B. A homogeneous product.
C. Economies of scale.
D. Perfect information.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Significant economies of scale can lead to a natural monopoly, where a single large firm can produce at a lower average cost than multiple smaller firms, making it difficult for new firms to enter and compete.

**3.8 The short-run supply curve of a perfectly competitive firm is its**
A. Average Total Cost curve above Average Variable Cost.
B. entire Marginal Cost curve.
C. Marginal Cost curve above the shut-down point.
D. Average Variable Cost curve.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A firm will only supply output in the short run if the price covers its average variable costs. Therefore, its supply curve is its marginal cost curve above the minimum point of the AVC curve (the shutdown point).

**39. Compared to perfect competition, a monopoly typically produces**
A. a higher output and charges a lower price.
B. a lower output and charges a higher price.
C. the same output but charges a higher price.
D. a lower output and charges a lower price.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A monopolist maximizes profit where MR=MC, which leads to a quantity that is lower and a price that is higher than the socially efficient outcome seen in perfect competition (where P=MC).

**40. Allocative efficiency occurs when**
A. Price = Marginal Cost.
B. Price = Average Total Cost.
C. Marginal Revenue = Marginal Cost.
D. production occurs at minimum Average Total Cost.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Allocative efficiency means resources are allocated to produce the 'right' mix of goods that society values most. This occurs when the value consumers place on the last unit (Price) equals the cost of producing it (Marginal Cost).

**41. The social cost of monopoly refers to the**
A. tax paid by the monopolist.
B. high salaries paid to its managers.
C. deadweight loss due to underproduction.
D. cost of advertising.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The primary social cost of monopoly is the deadweight loss, which is the loss of total surplus because the monopolist restricts output below the socially efficient level (where P=MC).

**42. Productive efficiency is achieved when**
A. P = MC.
B. MR = MC.
C. P = minimum ATC.
D. a firm earns supernormal profits.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Productive efficiency means producing goods at the lowest possible average cost. In perfect competition, this occurs in the long run where firms operate at the minimum point of their ATC curve.

**43. A natural monopoly exists when**
A. the government grants an exclusive license.
B. a firm controls a key natural resource.
C. a single firm can supply the entire market at a lower average cost than two or more firms.
D. a firm has a patent for its product.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A natural monopoly arises from significant economies of scale, where the long-run average cost curve is declining over the entire range of market demand, making one firm more efficient than many.

**44. A perfectly competitive firm should shut down in the short run if the price falls below**
A. Average Total Cost.
B. Average Fixed Cost.
C. Marginal Cost.
D. Average Variable Cost.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** If the price is below the average variable cost, the firm cannot even cover its variable costs of production per unit. It minimizes its loss (which will be its fixed costs) by shutting down.

**45. "X-inefficiency" is most likely to occur in which market structure?**
A. Perfect competition
B. Monopolistic competition
C. Monopoly
D. Oligopoly
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** X-inefficiency refers to organizational slack and higher-than-necessary costs due to a lack of competitive pressure. Monopolies, being sheltered from competition, are most prone to this.

### **Chapter 4: Monopolistic Competition and Oligopoly**

**46. The key feature that distinguishes monopolistic competition from perfect competition is**
A. the number of firms.
B. product differentiation.
C. barriers to entry.
D. the profit maximization rule.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The hallmark of monopolistic competition is that many firms sell products that are close substitutes but are differentiated by branding, quality, design, or location.

**47. Non-price competition, such as advertising, is a key feature of**
A. perfect competition and monopoly.
B. monopoly only.
C. monopolistic competition and oligopoly.
D. perfect competition only.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Firms in both monopolistic competition (to differentiate their products) and oligopoly (to avoid price wars and build brand loyalty) heavily engage in non-price competition like advertising.

**48. In the long run, a firm in monopolistic competition earns normal profit because of**
A. significant barriers to entry.
B. a standardized product.
C. interdependence among firms.
D. free entry and exit.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Similar to perfect competition, the free entry of new firms (attracted by short-run supernormal profits) erodes those profits, leading to a long-run equilibrium where firms earn only normal profit.

**49. A key characteristic of an oligopolistic market is**
A. a single firm.
B. interdependence among firms.
C. zero barriers to entry.
D. a perfectly elastic demand curve.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** In an oligopoly, there are only a few large firms, so each firm's decisions on price and output directly affect its rivals, and they must anticipate and react to each other's actions. This strategic interdependence is crucial.

**50. The Kinked Demand Curve model of oligopoly is used to explain**
A. how prices are initially determined.
B. price rigidity or stickiness.
C. output determination in a cartel.
D. the benefits of price wars.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The model assumes rivals will match price cuts but not price increases. This creates a kink in the demand curve and a discontinuity in the MR curve, leading to stable prices even if costs change slightly.

**51. A formal agreement among oligopolistic firms to fix prices and limit output is known as a**
A. gentleman's agreement.
B. cartel.
C. price leadership.
D. kinked demand curve.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A cartel is a formal, explicit collusive agreement among firms to act like a single monopolist to maximize joint profits.

**52. The Prisoner's Dilemma illustrates that**
A. cooperation is always the best strategy for individuals.
B. firms always have an incentive to collude.
C. individually rational actions can lead to a collectively worse outcome.
D. competition leads to the best outcome for all players.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In the Prisoner's Dilemma, each player's dominant strategy is to confess (or cheat), leading to a Nash equilibrium that is worse for both than if they had cooperated (stayed silent).

**53. A monopolistically competitive firm is considered inefficient in the long run because**
A. it earns supernormal profits.
B. Price is less than Marginal Cost.
C. it operates with excess capacity.
D. it produces a standardized product.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In long-run equilibrium, a monopolistically competitive firm produces at an output level to the left of the minimum point on its ATC curve (where P=ATC on the downward-sloping part). This means it has excess capacity—it could produce more at a lower average cost.

**54. The Cournot model of duopoly assumes that firms compete by choosing their**
A. price level.
B. advertising budget.
C. output level.
D. technology level.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In the Cournot model, each firm decides on its quantity (output) to produce, assuming the other firm's quantity is fixed.

**55. Which of the following is an example of non-price competition?**
A. A 10% discount sale.
B. Branding and packaging.
C. A price war.
D. Lowering the listed price of a product.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Non-price competition involves attracting customers through means other than changing the price, such as product differentiation, branding, advertising, and packaging.

**56. Cartels are often unstable in the long run because**
A. it is illegal to form them.
B. members have an incentive to cheat on the agreement.
C. production costs are too high.
D. consumer demand is too low.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** While illegality is a factor, the primary internal reason for cartel instability is that each member firm has a powerful individual incentive to secretly increase its own output or lower its price to capture a larger market share and profits.

**57. Price leadership is a form of**
A. explicit collusion.
B. predatory pricing.
C. a price war.
D. tacit collusion.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Price leadership is an informal, unwritten understanding where one firm (often the largest or most efficient) sets a price and other firms follow, avoiding direct price competition without a formal agreement. This is a form of tacit collusion.

**58. Compared to monopoly, the output of a Cournot duopoly is typically**
A. lower and the price is higher.
B. the same and the price is the same.
C. higher and the price is lower.
D. higher and the price is higher.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The Cournot equilibrium results in a total market output that is greater than a single monopolist's output but less than the perfectly competitive output. Consequently, the price is lower than the monopoly price but higher than the competitive price.

**59. In the Bertrand model of duopoly with a homogeneous product, firms will end up setting price equal to**
A. Average Total Cost.
B. Marginal Revenue.
C. the monopoly price.
D. Marginal Cost.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The Bertrand model assumes price competition. With a homogeneous product, firms will keep undercutting each other until the price is driven down to the level of marginal cost, similar to the perfectly competitive outcome.

**60. The long-run equilibrium for both perfect competition and monopolistic competition is characterized by**
A. supernormal profits.
B. a single firm.
C. normal profits.
D. price equal to marginal cost.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Due to free entry and exit in both market structures, any short-run supernormal profits are competed away, leading to a long-run equilibrium where firms earn only normal profits (zero economic profit).

### **Chapter 5: Consumer Theory**

**61. The law of diminishing marginal utility states that**
A. total utility eventually falls as more of a good is consumed.
B. the satisfaction from consuming each additional unit of a good eventually decreases.
C. average utility decreases as more of a good is consumed.
D. people prefer more of a good to less.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This law states that as a person consumes more and more units of a specific commodity, the additional (marginal) satisfaction derived from each successive unit will eventually decline.

**62. When total utility is at its maximum, marginal utility is**
A. at its maximum.
B. negative.
C. zero.
D. positive and rising.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Total utility stops increasing and reaches its peak (the satiation point) when the last unit consumed adds zero additional satisfaction. After this point, if consumption continues, MU becomes negative and TU falls.

**63. The ordinal utility approach assumes that utility can be**
A. measured in cardinal units called utils.
B. ranked or ordered.
C. added across individuals.
D. held constant.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Ordinal utility, used in indifference curve analysis, rejects the idea of measuring satisfaction in units. Instead, it assumes consumers can rank bundles of goods as preferred, less preferred, or indifferent.

**64. An indifference curve shows all combinations of two goods that**
A. a consumer can afford to buy.
B. yield the same level of total utility.
C. can be produced with given resources.
D. have the same price ratio.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** By definition, a consumer is indifferent between any combination of goods lying on a single indifference curve because each combination provides the same level of satisfaction.

**65. Indifference curves are convex to the origin because of the**
A. law of diminishing marginal utility.
B. assumption of non-satiation.
C. assumption of transitivity.
D. principle of diminishing marginal rate of substitution (MRS).
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The convex shape reflects that as a consumer has more of Good X and less of Good Y, they are willing to give up fewer units of Y to get one more unit of X, because Y is becoming scarcer and X more abundant to them.

**66. The slope of a consumer's budget line is determined by the**
A. consumer's income.
B. consumer's preferences.
C. ratio of the prices of the two goods.
D. marginal rate of substitution.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The slope of the budget line is given by -(Px/Py), representing the rate at which the market allows the consumer to trade one good for the other.

**67. Consumer equilibrium is achieved when**
A. the budget line intersects the highest possible indifference curve.
B. marginal utility is zero.
C. the budget line is tangent to the highest attainable indifference curve.
D. the consumer spends all of their income.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Equilibrium represents the point of maximum satisfaction given a budget constraint. This occurs at the point of tangency, where the slope of the indifference curve (MRS) equals the slope of the budget line (price ratio).

**68. An increase in a consumer's income, with prices held constant, will cause the budget line to**
A. pivot inwards.
B. pivot outwards.
C. shift inwards in a parallel manner.
D. shift outwards in a parallel manner.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** An increase in income increases the consumer's purchasing power, allowing them to buy more of both goods. This is represented by a parallel outward shift of the budget line.

**69. The substitution effect of a price fall for a good is always**
A. negative.
B. positive.
C. zero.
D. dependent on whether the good is normal or inferior.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** When the price of a good falls, it becomes relatively cheaper. The substitution effect captures the change in consumption due solely to this change in relative prices, which always leads to an increase in consumption of the cheaper good.

**70. A Giffen good is a special type of inferior good where the**
A. substitution effect outweighs the income effect.
B. income effect is positive.
C. negative income effect outweighs the substitution effect.
D. demand curve is downward sloping.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** For a Giffen good, a fall in price leads to a decrease in quantity demanded. This happens because the good is so strongly inferior that the negative income effect (the increase in real income causes a large drop in demand) is more powerful than the positive substitution effect.

**71. An Engel curve shows the relationship between**
A. price and quantity demanded.
B. income and quantity demanded.
C. price and quantity supplied.
D. income and price.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** An Engel curve illustrates how the quantity of a good consumed changes as consumer income changes, holding prices constant.

**72. Two indifference curves for the same individual cannot intersect because it would violate the assumption of**
A. diminishing MRS.
B. convexity.
C. transitivity.
D. rationality.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** If two curves intersected, it would imply that a consumer is indifferent between a bundle on the lower curve and a bundle on the higher curve, and also indifferent between that same bundle on the higher curve and another bundle on the higher curve, which creates a contradiction.

**73. Revealed preference theory attempts to derive consumer preferences from**
A. psychological surveys.
B. hypothetical choices.
C. observed purchasing behavior.
D. marginal utility calculations.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The theory of revealed preference, developed by Samuelson, works on the principle that if a consumer chooses one bundle of goods over another when both were affordable, they have "revealed" their preference for the chosen bundle.

**74. The condition for consumer equilibrium, MUx/Px = MUy/Py, is known as the principle of**
A. diminishing returns.
B. consumer sovereignty.
C. equi-marginal utility.
D. revealed preference.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This principle states that a consumer maximizes total utility by allocating their budget such that the marginal utility per dollar (or other currency unit) spent on each good is equal.

**75. Higher indifference curves represent**
A. higher levels of satisfaction.
B. lower levels of satisfaction.
C. unattainable combinations.
D. affordable combinations.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Due to the "more is better" (non-satiation) assumption, an indifference curve further from the origin represents bundles with more of at least one good and therefore a higher level of utility.

### **Chapter 6: Producer Theory**

**76. The key difference between economic costs and accounting costs is that economic costs include**
A. variable costs.
B. fixed costs.
C. explicit costs.
D. implicit costs.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Economic cost is the sum of explicit (out-of-pocket) costs and implicit costs (the opportunity cost of self-owned resources). Accountants typically only focus on explicit costs.

**77. In the short run, at least one factor of production is**
A. expensive.
B. variable.
C. fixed.
D. inefficient.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The short run is defined as a period where the firm cannot change all its inputs; typically, capital (plant size, heavy machinery) is considered fixed.

**78. Which of the following is an example of a fixed cost?**
A. Cost of raw materials.
B. Wages of production workers.
C. Rent on a factory building.
D. Fuel for machinery.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Rent on a factory is a cost that must be paid regardless of the level of output produced in the short run, making it a fixed cost.

**79. The Marginal Cost (MC) curve intersects the Average Total Cost (ATC) curve**
A. at the maximum point of the ATC curve.
B. at the minimum point of the ATC curve.
C. when the ATC curve is falling.
D. when the ATC curve is rising.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This is a key geometric relationship. When MC is below ATC, it pulls the average down. When MC is above ATC, it pulls the average up. Therefore, MC must cross ATC at its lowest point.

**80. Economies of scale occur when**
A. short-run average cost falls as output increases.
B. long-run average cost falls as output increases.
C. total cost falls as output increases.
D. short-run average cost rises as output increases.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Economies of scale is a long-run concept referring to the cost advantages a firm experiences as it increases its scale of operations (all inputs are variable), leading to a fall in long-run average costs.

**81. Diseconomies of scale are often caused by**
A. technological improvements.
B. bulk-buying discounts.
C. specialization of labor.
D. managerial and coordination problems.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** As a firm becomes too large and complex, communication, coordination, and control can become difficult and inefficient, leading to rising long-run average costs.

**82. The Average Revenue (AR) curve for a firm is the same as its**
A. Total Revenue curve.
B. Marginal Revenue curve.
C. Demand curve.
D. Marginal Cost curve.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Average Revenue is Total Revenue / Quantity = (Price x Quantity) / Quantity = Price. The demand curve shows the price consumers are willing to pay for each quantity, so the AR curve and demand curve are the same.

**83. A firm reaches its break-even point when**
A. Marginal Revenue equals Marginal Cost.
B. Total Revenue is maximized.
C. Total Revenue equals Total Cost.
D. Average Total Cost is minimized.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The break-even point is the level of output where the firm makes zero economic profit (i.e., earns a normal profit). This occurs where TR = TC.

**84. If a firm earns more than a normal profit, it is said to be earning**
A. supernormal profit.
B. accounting profit.
C. implicit profit.
D. a loss.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Supernormal profit (or economic profit) is any profit earned in excess of normal profit, which means TR > TC (where TC includes all explicit and implicit costs).

**85. An isoquant shows all combinations of two inputs that**
A. can be bought for a given total cost.
B. yield the same level of output.
C. have the same marginal rate of technical substitution.
D. can be used in the long run.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** An isoquant ("iso" meaning equal, "quant" meaning quantity) is a curve that represents all the different combinations of, for example, labor and capital that can be used to produce a specific, constant level of output.

**86. The slope of an isoquant is known as the**
A. marginal product of labor.
B. marginal rate of substitution.
C. input price ratio.
D. marginal rate of technical substitution (MRTS).
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The MRTS measures the rate at which one input (e.g., capital) can be substituted for another (e.g., labor) while keeping the level of output constant.

**87. A producer's least-cost combination of inputs for a given output is found where**
A. the isocost line is tangent to the highest possible isoquant.
B. the isocost line is tangent to the given isoquant.
C. the isoquant has the steepest slope.
D. the firm uses only one input.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** To minimize the cost of producing a specific level of output (represented by an isoquant), the firm finds the point where that isoquant is tangent to the lowest possible isocost line.

**88. If the Marginal Product per dollar of labor is greater than the Marginal Product per dollar of capital (MPL/PL > MPK/PK), the firm should**
A. use more capital and less labor.
B. use more labor and less capital.
C. increase its total costs.
D. decrease its total output.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This condition means the firm gets more "bang for its buck" from the last dollar spent on labor than on capital. To produce more efficiently, it should reallocate spending from capital to labor until the ratios are equal.

**89. The Cobb-Douglas production function Q = A * L^α * K^β exhibits increasing returns to scale if**
A. α + β < 1
B. α + β = 1
C. α + β > 1
D. A = 1
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In a Cobb-Douglas function, the sum of the output elasticities (α + β) indicates the returns to scale. If the sum is greater than 1, doubling all inputs will more than double the output.

**90. Average Fixed Cost (AFC) continuously falls as output increases because**
A. fixed costs are not important.
B. the firm becomes more efficient.
C. total fixed cost is being spread over more units of output.
D. variable costs are rising.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** AFC = TFC / Q. Since TFC is a constant, as the denominator (Q) increases, the value of the fraction must decrease.

### **Chapter 7: National Income Accounting**

**91. Gross Domestic Product (GDP) is the total market value of all**
A. goods and services produced by nationals of a country.
B. intermediate goods produced within a country.
C. final goods and services produced within the geographical boundaries of a country.
D. transactions in an economy, including second-hand goods.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** GDP's definition is specific: it includes *final* goods to avoid double counting, and measures production *within a country's borders*, regardless of who owns the factors of production.

**92. Gross National Product (GNP) is calculated as**
A. GDP - Depreciation.
B. GDP + Net Factor Income from Abroad.
C. NNP + Indirect Taxes.
D. PI - Personal Taxes.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** GNP measures the income of a country's nationals. It starts with GDP (what's produced domestically) and adds the net income that nationals have earned from overseas (NFIA).

**93. The difference between gross and net national product is**
A. indirect taxes.
B. subsidies.
C. depreciation.
D. net factor income from abroad.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** "Gross" measures do not account for the value of capital used up in production. "Net" measures subtract this value, which is known as depreciation or capital consumption allowance.

**94. Which of the following is excluded when calculating a country's GDP?**
A. The value of services provided by a teacher.
B. The value of a newly built house.
C. The sale of a used textbook.
D. The production of a new car that remains unsold.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** GDP measures current production. A used textbook was counted in the GDP of the year it was first produced. Its resale is a transfer of an existing asset.

**95. The expenditure approach to calculating GDP is given by the formula**
A. Y = C + I + G + M - X
B. Y = Wages + Rent + Interest + Profit
C. Y = GDP - Depreciation
D. Y = C + I + G + (X - M)
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The expenditure approach sums up spending on final goods and services by households (Consumption), firms (Investment), the government (Government Expenditure), and the net spending by foreigners (Net Exports = Exports - Imports).

**96. A transfer payment is**
A. a payment for a good or service.
B. included in the G component of GDP.
C. a payment for which no good or service is exchanged.
D. the same as a subsidy.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Transfer payments, such as pensions or unemployment benefits, are transfers of income from one group to another (usually via the government) and are not made in exchange for currently produced goods or services. They are excluded from the income and expenditure approaches to GDP.

**97. Real GDP is nominal GDP adjusted for**
A. population changes.
B. changes in the quality of goods.
C. exchange rate changes.
D. price changes (inflation).
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Real GDP measures the volume of output. To calculate it, the effects of price changes are removed from nominal GDP by using prices from a constant base year.

**98. If nominal GDP is GHS 500 billion and the GDP deflator is 125, what is the real GDP?**
A. GHS 400 billion.
B. GHS 625 billion.
C. GHS 375 billion.
D. GHS 500 billion.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Real GDP = (Nominal GDP / GDP Deflator) x 100 = (500 / 125) x 100 = 4 x 100 = 400 billion.

**99. A major difference between the GDP deflator and the Consumer Price Index (CPI) is that the CPI includes the prices of**
A. investment goods.
B. government purchases.
C. imported consumer goods.
D. exported goods.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The CPI measures the cost of a basket of goods consumed by a typical household, which can include imports. The GDP deflator measures the prices of all goods produced domestically, so it excludes imports but includes investment goods, etc.

**100. Disposable Personal Income (DI) is defined as**
A. National Income - Corporate Taxes.
B. Personal Income - Personal Direct Taxes.
C. GNP - Depreciation.
D. GDP - Indirect Taxes.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** DI is the income that households have left for spending (consumption) or saving after they have paid their direct personal taxes like income tax.

**101. Which of the following is a major limitation of GDP as a measure of economic welfare?**
A. It is difficult to calculate.
B. It is always revised.
C. It excludes the value of non-market activities and leisure.
D. It is always measured in current prices.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** GDP does not account for many things that contribute to well-being, such as unpaid household work, volunteer work, or the amount of leisure time people have.

**102. The value-added approach to calculating GDP is used to**
A. measure the profit of firms.
B. avoid double counting intermediate goods.
C. include the sale of used goods.
D. account for depreciation.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** By summing the value added at each stage of production (value of output minus cost of intermediate inputs), the value-added method ensures that the value of intermediate goods is not counted multiple times on the way to the final product.

**103. The Human Development Index (HDI) is a composite index that includes**
A. GDP per capita, inflation, and unemployment.
B. life expectancy, education, and GNI per capita.
C. infant mortality, literacy, and political freedom.
D. poverty rate, inequality, and environmental quality.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** HDI provides a broader measure of development by combining three key dimensions: a long and healthy life (life expectancy), knowledge (education levels), and a decent standard of living (Gross National Income per capita).

**104. Net Factor Income from Abroad is the difference between**
A. exports and imports of goods.
B. income earned by residents abroad and income earned by foreigners domestically.
C. foreign aid received and foreign aid given.
D. direct investment and portfolio investment.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** NFIA specifically measures the net flow of factor incomes (wages, profits, interest) between a country's residents and the rest of the world.

**105. An increase in a country's capital stock is known as**
A. consumption.
B. depreciation.
C. investment.
D. savings.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Investment, or gross capital formation, is the expenditure on new capital goods like machinery, equipment, and buildings, which adds to the country's capital stock.

### **Chapter 8: Money, Banking, and Fiscal Policy**

**106. An item is considered money if it is**
A. issued by the government.
B. widely accepted as a means of payment.
C. made of a precious metal.
D. easily divisible.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The primary characteristic that defines money is its general acceptability in exchange for goods and services.

**107. The function of money that allows us to compare the values of different goods is**
A. medium of exchange.
B. store of value.
C. unit of account.
D. standard of deferred payment.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** As a unit of account (or measure of value), money provides a common denominator to express and compare the prices of diverse items.

**108. Fiat money is money that**
A. is backed by a physical commodity like gold.
B. has intrinsic value equal to its face value.
C. is declared legal tender by government decree.
D. is created by commercial banks.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Fiat money has value not because of any intrinsic worth but because the government has declared it to be legal tender and it is accepted in faith.

**119. The M1 measure of the money supply typically includes**
A. currency in circulation and demand deposits.
B. savings deposits and small time deposits.
C. government bonds and treasury bills.
D. currency in circulation and commercial bank reserves.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** M1 is the narrowest measure of money readily available for transactions. It consists of physical currency held by the public and checkable (demand) deposits.

**110. The Central Bank's most frequently used tool for controlling the money supply is**
A. changing the reserve requirement.
B. changing the discount rate.
C. moral suasion.
D. open market operations.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Open Market Operations (buying and selling government securities) are flexible, precise, and can be used daily, making them the most common instrument of monetary policy.

**111. To combat inflation, a central bank would most likely**
A. buy government securities in the open market.
B. sell government securities in the open market.
C. lower the reserve requirement.
D. lower the discount rate.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Selling securities drains reserves from the banking system, reducing banks' ability to lend. This contracts the money supply and increases interest rates, which helps to curb inflation.

**112. The credit multiplier is calculated as**
A. 1 / (1 - MPC).
B. RRR x Total Deposits.
C. 1 / RRR (Required Reserve Ratio).
D. Change in Deposits / Change in Reserves.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The simple credit (or deposit) multiplier shows the maximum potential expansion of the money supply from a new deposit. It is the reciprocal of the required reserve ratio.

**113. A loan on a bank's books becomes a Non-Performing Asset (NPA) when**
A. it is fully repaid.
B. the borrower's business is making a loss.
C. the interest or principal payments are overdue for a specified period.
D. the interest rate on the loan is too high.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** An NPA is defined as a loan that has stopped generating income for the bank because the borrower has failed to make scheduled payments for a designated period (e.g., 90 days).

**114. Fiscal policy refers to the government's use of**
A. money supply and interest rates to influence the economy.
B. taxation and spending to influence the economy.
C. trade barriers to protect domestic industry.
D. regulations to control market behavior.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Fiscal policy is the government's tool for macroeconomic management, operating through its budget, specifically its powers of taxation and expenditure.

**115. A progressive tax is one where the tax rate**
A. is the same for all income levels.
B. decreases as income increases.
C. increases as income increases.
D. is levied on goods and services.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A progressive tax system aims to take a larger percentage of income from high-income earners than from low-income earners, based on the ability-to-pay principle.

**116. An example of an indirect tax is**
A. Personal Income Tax.
B. Corporate Income Tax.
C. Value Added Tax (VAT).
D. Property Tax.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** VAT is levied on the sale of goods and services. Its burden can be shifted from the seller to the final consumer, which is a characteristic of indirect taxes.

**117. A government budget is in deficit when**
A. total revenue is greater than total expenditure.
B. total expenditure is equal to total revenue.
C. total expenditure is greater than total revenue.
D. exports are greater than imports.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A budget deficit occurs when the government spends more than it collects in revenue (from taxes and non-tax sources) during a fiscal year.

**118. Government expenditure on building a new road is an example of**
A. recurrent expenditure.
B. capital expenditure.
C. a transfer payment.
D. a budget deficit.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This is capital expenditure because it creates a long-term asset (a road) that will provide benefits over many years. Recurrent expenditure is for day-to-day running costs.

**119. Public debt is the total accumulated amount of**
A. foreign aid received by the government.
B. taxes owed to the government.
C. money owed by the government from past borrowing.
D. non-performing assets of state-owned banks.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Public debt is the stock of outstanding government borrowing that has resulted from financing past budget deficits.

**120. During a recession, the appropriate expansionary fiscal policy would be to**
A. increase taxes and decrease government spending.
B. decrease taxes and decrease government spending.
C. decrease taxes and increase government spending.
D. increase taxes and increase government spending.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** To boost aggregate demand and combat a recession, the government can cut taxes (to encourage consumption and investment) and increase its own spending.

### **Chapter 9: Classical and Keynesian Models**

**121. Say's Law of Markets states that**
A. demand creates its own supply.
B. supply creates its own demand.
C. savings always equals investment.
D. government should manage aggregate demand.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Attributed to J-B Say, this core classical belief posits that the act of producing goods generates enough income to purchase those goods, so a general lack of demand is impossible.

**122. The Classical Dichotomy suggests that in the long run, a change in the money supply will only affect**
A. real variables like output and employment.
B. nominal variables like the price level.
C. the rate of economic growth.
D. the distribution of income.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The classical dichotomy separates real and nominal variables. The concept of "money neutrality" implies that money supply changes affect nominal variables (prices) but not real economic activity (output, employment) in the long run.

**123. The Keynesian model of the economy is primarily focused on the**
A. long run.
B. short run.
C. supply side.
D. behavior of individual firms.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Keynes's analysis was a response to the Great Depression, focusing on why economies can get stuck with high unemployment in the short run, a period in which wages and prices are "sticky".

**124. According to Keynes, the most important determinant of consumption expenditure is**
A. the interest rate.
B. disposable income.
C. future expectations.
D. the price level.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The Keynesian consumption function makes a direct link between consumption and current disposable income (income after tax).

**125. The Marginal Propensity to Consume (MPC) is the**
A. fraction of total income that is consumed.
B. fraction of an additional unit of income that is consumed.
C. ratio of consumption to savings.
D. total consumption divided by total income.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** MPC = ΔC / ΔYd. It measures how much consumption changes for a given change in disposable income.

**126. If the MPC is 0.75, the value of the simple Keynesian multiplier is**
A. 0.25
B. 1.33
C. 4
D. 0.75
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Multiplier (k) = 1 / (1 - MPC) = 1 / (1 - 0.75) = 1 / 0.25 = 4.

**127. The 'crowding out' effect refers to the tendency for expansionary fiscal policy to**
A. reduce interest rates and increase private investment.
B. increase the money supply.
C. reduce net exports.
D. increase interest rates and reduce private investment.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** When the government borrows to finance spending, it can increase the demand for loanable funds, pushing up interest rates and making it more expensive for private firms to borrow and invest, thereby "crowding them out".

**128. The Aggregate Demand (AD) curve is downward sloping because a lower price level**
A. increases the real value of money holdings, encouraging spending.
B. reduces the purchasing power of consumers.
C. causes the central bank to tighten monetary policy.
D. makes domestic goods more expensive for foreigners.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** This is the real balance effect (or wealth effect). A lower price level increases the real purchasing power of consumers' financial assets, making them feel wealthier and spend more.

**129. The Short-Run Aggregate Supply (SRAS) curve is upward sloping because**
A. all input prices are fully flexible.
B. some input prices, like nominal wages, are sticky in the short run.
C. firms have a fixed amount of output they can produce.
D. technology improves as prices rise.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** If the overall price level rises while nominal wages are fixed by contract, firms' output becomes more profitable, so they increase the quantity supplied.

**130. The Long-Run Aggregate Supply (LRAS) curve is vertical at the potential level of output because**
A. technology is fixed in the long run.
B. government policy fixes the output level.
C. in the long run, output depends on resource and technology levels, not the price level.
D. prices are sticky in the long run.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** In the long run, all prices (including input prices like wages) are fully flexible. Therefore, changes in the overall price level do not affect the real productive capacity of the economy, which is determined by its factors of production and technology.

**131. A recessionary gap occurs when**
A. actual output is greater than potential output.
B. actual output is equal to potential output.
C. actual output is less than potential output.
D. the aggregate supply curve shifts to the left.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A recessionary gap (or output gap) signifies that the economy is operating below its full potential, with high unemployment and idle resources.

**132. According to the Keynesian model, an underemployment equilibrium can exist because**
A. wages and prices are perfectly flexible.
B. supply creates its own demand.
C. aggregate demand can be insufficient to generate full employment.
D. interest rates automatically adjust to clear markets.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A central Keynesian insight is that if planned aggregate demand (C+I+G+X-M) is too low, the economy can reach an equilibrium at a level of output below full employment, and there is no automatic mechanism to fix this quickly.

**133. If autonomous consumption is 50 and the MPC is 0.8, the consumption function is**
A. C = 0.8 + 50Yd
B. C = 50 + 0.2Yd
C. C = 50 + 0.8Yd
D. C = -50 + 0.8Yd
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The linear consumption function is C = a + bYd, where 'a' is autonomous consumption and 'b' is the MPC.

**134. Which of the following would cause a leftward shift in the Aggregate Demand curve?**
A. An increase in government spending.
B. A decrease in taxes.
C. A fall in consumer confidence.
D. An increase in the money supply.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A fall in consumer confidence will cause households to spend less and save more at any given income level, reducing consumption (C) and thus shifting the AD curve to the left.

**135. An increase in oil prices, a key input for many industries, would cause**
A. a rightward shift of the SRAS curve.
B. a leftward shift of the SRAS curve.
C. a rightward shift of the AD curve.
D. a leftward shift of the AD curve.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A rise in the price of a major input like oil increases production costs for many firms, causing them to supply less at any given price level. This is a negative supply shock, shifting the SRAS curve to the left.

### **Chapter 10: Inflation, Unemployment, and Business Cycles**

**136. A sustained increase in the general price level is known as**
A. deflation.
B. disinflation.
C. inflation.
D. stagflation.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This is the standard definition of inflation.

**137. Inflation caused by "too much money chasing too few goods" is called**
A. cost-push inflation.
B. demand-pull inflation.
C. built-in inflation.
D. hyperinflation.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Demand-pull inflation occurs when aggregate demand grows faster than the economy's productive capacity, pulling prices up.

**138. The combination of rising prices and falling output (stagnation) is called**
A. reflation.
B. depression.
C. disinflation.
D. stagflation.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Stagflation, a major problem in the 1970s, is caused by a negative supply shock (e.g., an oil price increase) that shifts the SRAS curve left, leading to both higher inflation and higher unemployment.

**139. The short-run Phillips curve shows a trade-off between**
A. inflation and economic growth.
B. unemployment and economic growth.
C. inflation and unemployment.
D. wages and profits.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The original Phillips curve illustrated an inverse relationship: when unemployment was low, inflation tended to be high, and vice versa, suggesting a policy trade-off in the short run.

**140. The long-run Phillips curve is vertical at the**
A. zero rate of unemployment.
B. natural rate of unemployment.
C. actual rate of inflation.
D. expected rate of inflation.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** In the long run, after expectations have adjusted, there is no trade-off between inflation and unemployment. The unemployment rate will gravitate to its natural rate regardless of the rate of inflation.

**141. A university graduate searching for their first job is an example of**
A. cyclical unemployment.
B. structural unemployment.
C. seasonal unemployment.
D. frictional unemployment.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Frictional unemployment is the normal, short-term unemployment that occurs as people transition between jobs or enter the labor force.

**142. Unemployment caused by a mismatch between the skills of workers and the skills demanded by employers is called**
A. frictional unemployment.
B. cyclical unemployment.
C. structural unemployment.
D. demand-deficient unemployment.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Structural unemployment arises from long-term changes in the structure of the economy, such as technological shifts, that make certain skills obsolete.

**143. Unemployment that rises during an economic recession is called**
A. cyclical unemployment.
B. natural unemployment.
C. frictional unemployment.
D. structural unemployment.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Cyclical unemployment is caused by a lack of aggregate demand during the contraction phase of the business cycle.

**144. The peak of a business cycle is characterized by**
A. high unemployment and falling output.
B. the lowest point of economic activity.
C. rising employment and rising output.
D. low unemployment and the economy operating near full capacity.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The peak is the turning point at the top of an expansion, where economic activity is at its highest, unemployment is at its lowest, and inflationary pressures may be strong.

**145. An example of a leading economic indicator is**
A. the unemployment rate.
B. real GDP.
C. the stock market index.
D. the inflation rate.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Leading indicators tend to change direction before the broader economy. Stock market prices reflect investor expectations about future corporate profits and often fall before a recession and rise before a recovery.

**146. A feature of the fiscal system that automatically moderates the business cycle is called**
A. a multiplier.
B. an automatic stabilizer.
C. discretionary policy.
D. a balanced budget.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Automatic stabilizers, like a progressive income tax system or unemployment benefits, work without any new government action to dampen fluctuations by reducing spending power in a boom and supporting it in a recession.

**147. To whom is unanticipated inflation most likely to be beneficial?**
A. Lenders
B. Debtors
C. People on fixed incomes
D. Savers
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Debtors benefit because they repay their loans with money that is worth less than the money they originally borrowed, reducing the real value of their debt.

**148. Which of the following will shift the short-run Phillips curve upwards?**
A. A decrease in expected inflation.
B. An increase in expected inflation.
C. An increase in unemployment benefits.
D. A successful government training program.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** When workers and firms expect higher inflation, they build it into their wage and price-setting decisions. This means that for any given rate of unemployment, the corresponding rate of inflation will be higher, shifting the entire SRPC up.

**149. The natural rate of unemployment consists of**
A. cyclical unemployment only.
B. frictional and cyclical unemployment.
C. structural and cyclical unemployment.
D. frictional and structural unemployment.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The natural rate of unemployment is the rate that persists even when the economy is at "full employment". It is the sum of the unavoidable frictional unemployment and the more persistent structural unemployment. It excludes cyclical unemployment.

**150. An appropriate policy to reduce structural unemployment would be**
A. expansionary fiscal policy.
B. government-funded job retraining programs.
C. increasing unemployment benefits.
D. contractionary monetary policy.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Structural unemployment is a problem of skills mismatch. Therefore, supply-side policies like job retraining, education reform, and improving labor mobility are the most effective solutions.

### **Chapter 11: International Trade Theory**

**151. A country has an absolute advantage in producing a good if it**
A. has a lower opportunity cost in producing it.
B. can produce it using fewer resources than another country.
C. can produce it at a lower price in its own currency.
D. exports more of the good than it imports.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Absolute advantage, as defined by Adam Smith, refers to superior productivity—the ability to produce a unit of output with less input (e.g., fewer labor hours).

**152. The principle of comparative advantage was developed by**
A. Adam Smith.
B. John Maynard Keynes.
C. David Ricardo.
D. Alfred Marshall.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** David Ricardo extended Smith's idea to show that trade can be mutually beneficial even if one country has an absolute advantage in everything, as long as opportunity costs differ.

**153. A country should specialize in and export the good in which it has a**
A. comparative advantage.
B. absolute disadvantage.
C. higher production cost.
D. higher price.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The theory of comparative advantage states that countries gain from trade by specializing in the production of goods for which they have the lowest opportunity cost.

**154. If Ghana's opportunity cost of producing one unit of cocoa is 2 units of textiles, and Nigeria's is 3 units of textiles, then**
A. Nigeria has a comparative advantage in cocoa.
B. Ghana has a comparative advantage in cocoa.
C. Ghana has an absolute advantage in cocoa.
D. Nigeria should not produce cocoa.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Ghana gives up fewer textiles (2) to produce a unit of cocoa than Nigeria does (3). Therefore, Ghana has a lower opportunity cost and a comparative advantage in cocoa production.

**155. The Heckscher-Ohlin model predicts that a country will export goods that**
A. are produced with its relatively abundant factors.
B. are produced with its relatively scarce factors.
C. have the highest domestic price.
D. are produced by the government.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The Heckscher-Ohlin (or Factor Proportions) theory argues that trade patterns are determined by differences in factor endowments. A country will export goods that make intensive use of the factors (e.g., labor, capital) that it has in abundance.

**156. An improvement in a country's terms of trade means that**
A. its export prices have fallen relative to its import prices.
B. it can obtain more imports for a given amount of exports.
C. its exports have become less competitive.
D. it is importing more than it is exporting.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Terms of Trade = (Index of Export Prices / Index of Import Prices) x 100. An improvement means this ratio has increased, so each unit of export can buy more units of imports.

**157. A tax imposed on an imported good is called a**
A. quota.
B. subsidy.
C. tariff.
D. non-tariff barrier.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A tariff is a specific type of tax levied on goods as they cross national borders, usually on imports.

**158. The infant industry argument is a reason used to justify**
A. free trade.
B. a floating exchange rate.
C. trade protectionism.
D. foreign aid.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This argument posits that new, emerging domestic industries should be temporarily protected from established international competitors until they are mature and efficient enough to compete on their own.

**159. Which of the following is an effect of an import quota?**
A. It generates revenue for the government.
B. It increases the quantity of imports.
C. It lowers the price of the imported good.
D. It raises the price of the imported good.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** A quota is a quantitative limit on imports. By restricting supply, it causes the domestic price of the good to rise above the world price.

**160. An agreement between countries to remove all trade barriers among themselves but maintain their own individual tariffs against non-members is a**
A. customs union.
B. common market.
C. free trade area.
D. preferential trading area.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This is the definition of a free trade area (e.g., NAFTA/USMCA). A customs union goes further by establishing a common external tariff.

**161. A major feature of the European Union (EU) that goes beyond a customs union is the**
A. common external tariff.
B. free trade among members.
C. free movement of factors of production like labor and capital.
D. use of a common language.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The EU functions as a single or common market, which includes not just free trade and a common external tariff, but also the free movement of labor, capital, and services.

**162. 'Trade diversion' occurs when a regional trade bloc**
A. causes trade to shift from a more efficient non-member to a less efficient member.
B. causes overall trade to increase.
C. attracts more foreign direct investment.
D. leads to political cooperation.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Trade diversion is a potential disadvantage of a trade bloc. Due to the preferential tariffs for members, a country might start importing from a higher-cost member country instead of a lower-cost non-member, which represents an inefficient shift in trade patterns.

**163. ECOWAS aims to promote**
A. political union in Asia.
B. free trade in North America.
C. economic integration in West Africa.
D. military cooperation in Europe.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS) is a regional trade bloc whose primary objective is to achieve economic integration among its West African member states.

**164. "Dumping" in international trade refers to**
A. exporting environmentally hazardous waste.
B. selling goods abroad at a price below cost or below the home market price.
C. imposing a high tariff on imported goods.
D. a sudden surge in imports.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Dumping is an international price discrimination strategy where a company exports a product at a price lower than the price it normally charges in its own home market, or below its cost of production. It is often seen as an unfair trade practice.

**155. Which of the following is a non-tariff barrier (NTB)?**
A. A 10% tax on imported cars.
B. A specific duty of $1 per kg of sugar.
C. A complex and time-consuming customs procedure.
D. An export subsidy.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** NTBs are trade barriers that restrict imports or exports through means other than tariffs. Complicated administrative procedures can act as a significant obstacle to trade.

### **Chapter 12: Balance of Payments and Foreign Exchange Markets**

**166. A country's Balance of Payments is a systematic record of**
A. its government budget.
B. all economic transactions between its residents and the rest of the world.
C. its total exports and imports of goods only.
D. the value of its currency.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This is the definition of the BoP. It is a comprehensive account of all monetary transactions between a country and the rest of the world over a period.

**167. A transaction that leads to an inflow of foreign currency into a country is recorded as a**
A. debit.
B. credit.
C. deficit.
D. liability.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** In BoP accounting, any transaction that earns foreign exchange (e.g., exports, receiving foreign investment) is a credit (+).

**168. The Balance of Trade (BoT) is the difference between the value of**
A. total credits and total debits.
B. exports of goods and imports of goods.
C. exports of services and imports of services.
D. capital inflows and capital outflows.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The Balance of Trade (or Merchandise Trade Balance) specifically refers to the balance of tangible or visible trade, i.e., goods.

**169. Which of the following would be recorded in the Current Account of the BoP?**
A. A citizen purchasing shares in a foreign company.
B. The central bank buying foreign currency.
C. A foreign company building a factory in the country.
D. A tourist from another country spending money on a hotel.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Tourism is an export of a service. The purchase of shares and building a factory are financial account transactions. The central bank's action is a reserve asset transaction.

**170. The exchange rate is the**
A. interest rate charged on foreign loans.
B. price of one currency in terms of another.
C. balance between exports and imports.
D. total amount of foreign currency held by the central bank.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** An exchange rate expresses the value of one currency relative to another, e.g., how many cedis are needed to buy one US dollar.

**171. Under a fixed exchange rate system, the value of the currency is maintained by**
A. the stock market.
B. commercial banks.
C. central bank intervention in the forex market.
D. international law.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The central bank commits to buying and selling its currency (using foreign reserves) to keep the market rate at the officially pegged level.

**172. A deliberate downward adjustment of a fixed exchange rate is called**
A. depreciation.
B. revaluation.
C. appreciation.
D. devaluation.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** "Devaluation" is the term used for an official reduction in the value of a currency under a fixed exchange rate regime. "Depreciation" is used for a market-driven fall under a floating regime.

**173. The demand for a country's currency in the foreign exchange market is derived from**
A. the country's demand for imports.
B. foreign demand for the country's exports.
C. the country's residents traveling abroad.
D. the country making investments abroad.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Foreigners need to buy the domestic currency in order to pay for the country's exported goods and services, creating demand for that currency.

**174. According to the Purchasing Power Parity (PPP) theory, if inflation in country A is higher than in country B, country A's currency should**
A. appreciate.
B. depreciate.
C. be devalued.
D. remain stable.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Higher inflation erodes the purchasing power of a currency. To maintain parity, the exchange rate of the higher-inflation country should fall (depreciate).

**175. The primary objective of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) is to**
A. provide long-term loans for development projects.
B. promote free trade by setting rules.
C. promote international monetary cooperation and exchange rate stability.
D. reduce global poverty.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The IMF's core mandate is to ensure the stability of the international monetary system, including exchange rates and the balance of payments.

**176. Which of the following is part of the World Bank Group?**
A. World Trade Organization (WTO)
B. International Development Association (IDA)
C. Bank for International Settlements (BIS)
D. African Development Bank (AfDB)
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The IDA is the part of the World Bank Group that provides concessional loans and grants to the world's poorest countries.

**177. An increase in a country's domestic interest rates, relative to foreign rates, is likely to cause**
A. an outflow of short-term capital and currency depreciation.
B. an inflow of short-term capital and currency appreciation.
C. a decrease in the money supply.
D. a rise in exports.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Higher interest rates attract "hot money" from foreign investors seeking better returns, which increases the demand for the domestic currency and causes it to appreciate.

**178. A current account deficit must be financed by a**
A. trade surplus.
B. government budget surplus.
C. surplus in the combined capital and financial accounts or a fall in reserves.
D. rise in foreign aid.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A country spending more than it earns on current transactions must finance this by borrowing from abroad or selling assets to foreigners (a financial account surplus) or by using its official foreign reserves.

**179. A country operating a currency board arrangement gives up its**
A. fiscal policy independence.
B. ability to devalue.
C. monetary policy independence.
D. ability to join the WTO.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Under a currency board, the money supply is directly tied to foreign exchange reserves. The central bank cannot independently create money or set interest rates to manage the domestic economy.

**180. An inflow of foreign direct investment (FDI) would be recorded as a credit in which part of the Balance of Payments?**
A. Current Account
B. Capital Account
C. Financial Account
D. Official Reserves
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** FDI represents a purchase of a long-term interest in a domestic enterprise by a foreigner. This transaction in assets is recorded in the Financial Account.

### **Chapter 13: Concepts and Indicators of Development**

**181. The main difference between economic growth and economic development is that development**
A. only refers to an increase in real GDP.
B. is a broader measure that includes improvements in human well-being.
C. is only relevant for rich countries.
D. excludes economic growth.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Economic development is a qualitative, multidimensional concept that encompasses not just income growth but also improvements in health, education, equity, and overall quality of life.

**182. A situation where a person lacks the resources to meet basic needs like food and shelter is termed**
A. relative poverty.
B. absolute poverty.
C. inequality.
D. underdevelopment.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Absolute poverty refers to a severe deprivation of basic human necessities, often defined by a universal standard.

**183. The GINI coefficient is a measure of**
A. economic growth.
B. the poverty rate.
C. human development.
D. income or wealth inequality.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The GINI coefficient measures the distribution of income (or wealth) within a population, with 0 representing perfect equality and 1 representing perfect inequality.

**184. A GINI coefficient of 0 indicates**
A. perfect equality.
B. perfect inequality.
C. the highest level of poverty.
D. zero economic growth.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** A GINI of 0 would mean that the Lorenz curve coincides with the line of perfect equality, indicating that every person has the same income.

**185. The Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) measures poverty based on deprivations in**
A. income, consumption, and savings.
B. employment, wages, and working conditions.
C. health, education, and living standards.
D. political freedom, social justice, and environmental quality.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The MPI is designed to capture a more complete picture of poverty by looking at deprivations across these three key dimensions of life.

**186. Sustainable development is development that**
A. focuses only on economic growth.
B. maximizes the use of natural resources.
C. meets the needs of the present without compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
D. relies solely on foreign aid.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This is the classic definition of sustainable development from the Brundtland Commission, emphasizing intergenerational equity.

**187. The "resource curse" refers to the phenomenon where countries with abundant natural resources**
A. always have high levels of economic development.
B. sometimes experience slow growth, corruption, and conflict.
C. have a comparative advantage in manufacturing.
D. attract very little foreign investment.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The paradox of plenty, or resource curse, observes that resource-rich nations can paradoxically suffer from poor economic performance due to factors like dependency, currency appreciation (Dutch disease), and poor governance.

**188. Structural transformation during economic development typically involves a shift of employment**
A. from services to industry.
B. from industry to agriculture.
C. from agriculture to industry, and then to services.
D. to a balanced mix of all three sectors from the start.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** This is the classic pattern of structural change: as an economy develops, its reliance on agriculture diminishes, followed by a rise in the industrial sector, and finally a dominance of the service sector in mature economies.

**189. Which of the following is an example of the primary sector?**
A. Car manufacturing
B. Banking
C. Fishing
D. Road construction
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The primary sector involves the direct extraction of raw materials from nature. Fishing fits this description.

**190. An improvement in a nation's human capital can be achieved through investment in**
A. new machinery and equipment.
B. education and healthcare.
C. roads and bridges.
D. foreign currency reserves.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Human capital refers to the stock of knowledge, skills, and health embodied in the population. Investment in education and healthcare directly enhances this stock.
