**191. The Lorenz curve is a graphical representation of**
A. the business cycle.
B. income distribution.
C. a country's production possibilities.
D. the relationship between inflation and unemployment.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The Lorenz curve plots the cumulative share of income against the cumulative share of the population, providing a visual representation of income inequality.

**192. Which of the following is NOT one of the three main pillars of sustainable development?**
A. Economic sustainability
B. Social sustainability
C. Military sustainability
D. Environmental sustainability
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The three recognized and interconnected pillars of sustainable development are economic, social, and environmental.

**193. Which of the following would NOT be included in the MPI's dimension of "Living Standards"?**
A. Access to electricity.
B. Access to safe drinking water.
C. Mean years of schooling.
D. Type of cooking fuel used.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Mean years of schooling is an indicator under the "Education" dimension of the Multidimensional Poverty Index.

**194. The shift in demand away from basic foods towards services as incomes rise is partly explained by**
A. Say's Law.
B. the law of comparative advantage.
C. Engel's Law.
D. the law of diminishing returns.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Engel's Law observes that as household income increases, the percentage of income spent on food decreases, while the percentage spent on other goods and services (like healthcare, education, leisure) increases.

**195. A major driver of the structural shift to a service-based economy is**
A. a fall in agricultural productivity.
B. the discovery of new natural resources.
C. advancements in information and communication technology (ICT).
D. a rise in protectionism.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The ICT revolution has created a vast range of new service industries and boosted productivity in existing ones (e.g., finance, media, consulting), accelerating the shift to a service-dominant economy.

### **Chapter 14: Strategies for Economic Development**

**196. The Import Substitution Industrialization (ISI) strategy focuses on**
A. producing goods for export to international markets.
B. reducing reliance on agriculture.
C. replacing imported manufactured goods with domestic production.
D. liberalizing trade and removing all tariffs.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** ISI is an inward-looking strategy aimed at fostering domestic industry by protecting it from foreign competition and encouraging it to produce goods that were previously imported.

**197. A common policy tool used in ISI strategies is**
A. high tariffs on imported consumer goods.
B. export subsidies.
C. an undervalued exchange rate.
D. encouraging foreign direct investment in all sectors.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** To protect fledgling domestic industries, ISI strategies typically rely on trade barriers like high tariffs and quotas to make imported goods more expensive and less competitive.

**198. Export-Oriented Industrialization (EOI) aims to achieve growth by**
A. focusing on the domestic market.
B. promoting production for international markets.
C. banning all imports.
D. nationalizing all major industries.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** EOI is an outward-looking strategy that leverages a country's comparative advantage to produce manufactured goods for export, driving growth through access to global demand.

**199. A major benefit of EOI compared to ISI is that it**
A. guarantees self-sufficiency.
B. avoids any reliance on foreign technology.
C. forces domestic firms to become more efficient through international competition.
D. isolates the economy from global recessions.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** By having to compete in global markets, firms under an EOI strategy are constantly pushed to improve efficiency, quality, and technology, which often did not happen for firms sheltered under ISI protection.

**200. Concessional loans and grants provided by governments and international organizations to developing countries are called**
A. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI).
B. Remittances.
C. Portfolio Investment.
D. Foreign Aid (ODA).
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** This is the definition of Official Development Assistance (ODA), or foreign aid, which is given on more favorable terms than commercial loans to support development.

**201. An investment made by a company to acquire a lasting interest and significant influence in a foreign enterprise is called**
A. Foreign Aid.
B. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI).
C. Remittances.
D. a short-term loan.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** FDI is characterized by long-term interest and a degree of control, distinguishing it from more passive portfolio investment.

**202. A major potential benefit of FDI for a host developing country is**
A. increased aid dependence.
B. a reduction in local competition.
C. technology transfer and skills development.
D. repatriation of profits.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Multinational corporations often bring with them advanced technology, management techniques, and production processes that can be transferred to the local workforce and economy.

**203. Money sent by migrant workers to their families in their home country is known as**
A. FDI.
B. ODA.
C. profit repatriation.
D. remittances.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Remittances are personal transfers of funds from individuals working abroad back to their home country.

**204. Investing in education and health is a strategy for developing a country's**
A. natural resources.
B. financial capital.
C. physical infrastructure.
D. human capital.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Human capital represents the skills, knowledge, and health of the population. Education and healthcare are the primary means of investing in and enhancing it.

**205. The Grameen Bank in Bangladesh is a well-known pioneer of**
A. commercial banking.
B. central banking.
C. microfinance.
D. export promotion.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The Grameen Bank, founded by Muhammad Yunus, is famous for popularizing microcredit and the group lending model to provide small loans to the poor, especially women.

**206. A key principle of cooperative enterprises is**
A. maximizing profit for external shareholders.
B. democratic control by members.
C. government ownership and control.
D. limiting production to the domestic market.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Cooperatives are owned and controlled by their members, who typically have equal voting rights (e.g., "one member, one vote"), regardless of their capital contribution.

**207. A potential criticism of Foreign Aid is that it can**
A. increase technology transfer.
B. create long-term dependency and undermine local resource mobilization.
C. always reach the poorest people directly.
D. reduce a country's foreign exchange.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Critics of aid argue that if not managed well, it can make recipient governments reliant on donors and reduce their incentive to improve domestic tax systems and self-sufficiency.

**208. The East Asian "Tiger" economies are often cited as successful examples of which development strategy?**
A. Import Substitution Industrialization (ISI)
B. Agricultural development only
C. Export-Oriented Industrialization (EOI)
D. Resource-based development
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Countries like South Korea, Taiwan, and Singapore achieved rapid economic growth by focusing on manufacturing goods for export to developed markets.

**209. Microfinance primarily aims to provide financial services to**
A. large multinational corporations.
B. national governments.
C. high-income individuals.
D. low-income households and microenterprises.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** The core mission of microfinance is to promote financial inclusion by reaching those who are excluded from the formal banking system due to lack of collateral or low income.

**210. A major advantage of remittances as a source of external finance is that they**
A. are a form of loan that must be repaid.
B. go directly to households and can reduce poverty.
C. are managed by the government.
D. are used primarily to fund large infrastructure projects.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Unlike government aid or corporate FDI, remittances are personal transfers that directly boost the income of recipient families, often used for immediate needs like food, health, and education.

### **Chapter 15: Globalization and Its Impacts**

**211. Globalization can be defined as the process of**
A. increasing self-sufficiency of nations.
B. increasing government control over economies.
C. increasing interconnectedness and interdependence of countries.
D. promoting regional conflicts.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Globalization is characterized by the growing flow of goods, services, capital, and information across national borders, integrating national economies into a global one.

**212. A major technological driver of modern globalization is the**
A. invention of the steam engine.
B. development of the internet and communication technologies.
C. discovery of new medicines.
D. use of crop rotation in agriculture.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The ICT revolution drastically lowered the cost and increased the speed of global communication and information transfer, which has been fundamental to the recent wave of globalization.

**213. The World Trade Organization (WTO) and its predecessor, GATT, have promoted globalization primarily by**
A. providing loans for development.
B. stabilizing exchange rates.
C. working to reduce tariffs and other trade barriers.
D. controlling the flow of international capital.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The main function of the GATT/WTO system is to provide a forum for multilateral trade negotiations aimed at liberalizing international trade.

**214. Which of the following groups is most likely to be considered a "winner" from globalization?**
A. Unskilled workers in high-wage developed countries.
B. Domestic firms in developing countries that cannot compete with MNCs.
C. Consumers who get access to cheaper and more varied goods.
D. Governments that want to maintain national sovereignty over all policies.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Consumers generally benefit from globalization through lower prices (from competition and efficient sourcing), greater variety of products, and improved quality.

**215. A potential negative impact of globalization on developed countries is**
A. a lack of access to new technology.
B. job losses in manufacturing sectors due to import competition.
C. a reduction in the variety of goods available.
D. higher prices for consumer goods.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A common concern in developed nations is that competition from lower-wage countries can lead to the decline of certain industries and job losses for less-skilled workers in those sectors.

**216. A company contracting its IT support to another firm located in a different country is an example of**
A. Captive Offshoring.
B. Domestic Outsourcing.
C. Offshore Outsourcing.
D. Nearshoring.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** It is "offshore" because the function is moved to another country, and it is "outsourcing" because it is contracted to an external third-party company.

**217. The 2008 Global Financial Crisis originated in the**
A. European sovereign debt market.
B. Japanese stock market.
C. US subprime mortgage market.
D. Chinese manufacturing sector.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The crisis was triggered by widespread defaults on subprime mortgages in the US, which then spread globally through complex financial securities linked to these mortgages.

**218. A developing country's external debt crisis can be triggered by**
A. a sharp rise in the prices of its export commodities.
B. a fall in global interest rates.
C. a sharp fall in the prices of its export commodities.
D. receiving large amounts of foreign aid.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Many developing countries rely on a few export commodities. A fall in the price of these commodities drastically reduces their export earnings, making it difficult to service foreign currency-denominated debt.

**219. "Offshoring" is the practice of**
A. contracting a function to a domestic third party.
B. relocating a business process or operation to another country.
C. selling goods in a foreign market.
D. borrowing money from foreign banks.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Offshoring specifically involves moving a part of a company's business activities to a different country, often to reduce costs.

**220. The "contagion effect" seen in financial crises refers to**
A. the spread of a virus.
B. the spread of financial or economic instability from one market or country to others.
C. governments using fiscal policy to stimulate their economies.
D. central banks coordinating to lower interest rates.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Due to the high level of interconnectedness in a globalized financial system, problems in one country (like the US in 2008) can quickly spread and cause problems in others.

**221. Which group is often considered to be at a disadvantage due to globalization within developing countries?**
A. Highly skilled workers in the tech sector.
B. Workers in export-oriented industries.
C. Domestic firms serving local markets that must compete with large MNCs.
D. Consumers who buy imported electronics.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Small, local firms may lack the capital, technology, and economies of scale to compete effectively when large, efficient multinational corporations enter their market.

**222. Which of the following is NOT a driver of globalization?**
A. Advances in transportation technology
B. Liberalization of capital flows
C. A rise in protectionist trade policies
D. The spread of multinational corporations
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Protectionism (the use of tariffs, quotas, etc.) is a force that works *against* globalization by restricting the free flow of goods and services across borders.

**223. The "gig economy" refers to a labor market characterized by**
A. stable, long-term employment with a single company.
B. a dominance of agricultural jobs.
C. the prevalence of short-term contracts and freelance work.
D. government-guaranteed employment.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The gig economy involves flexible, temporary, or freelance jobs, often connecting clients or customers to workers via online platforms.

**224. Which of these is a reason a company might choose to outsource its payroll function?**
A. To gain direct control over employee data.
B. Because payroll is its main business activity.
C. To focus on its core competencies and reduce administrative costs.
D. To increase the number of in-house employees.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Outsourcing non-core functions like payroll to a specialized provider can be cheaper and more efficient, allowing the company to concentrate its resources on what it does best.

**225. A key lesson from recent economic crises is that globalization increases the need for**
A. national self-sufficiency.
B. international cooperation and financial regulation.
C. higher tariffs on all goods.
D. fixed exchange rate systems.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Because problems can spread so quickly across borders, there is a greater need for countries and institutions like the IMF to cooperate on monitoring financial risks and strengthening regulations.

### **Chapter 16: Environmental Economics and Resource Management**

**226. An externality is a cost or benefit that is**
A. borne by the producer of a good.
B. received by the consumer of a good.
C. imposed on a third party not involved in the transaction.
D. always accounted for in the market price.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Externalities are third-party or spillover effects. The market transaction fails to account for them, leading to a divergence between private and social costs/benefits.

**227. Air pollution from a factory is an example of a**
A. positive externality.
B. negative externality.
C. public good.
D. merit good.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The pollution imposes a cost (e.g., health problems, environmental damage) on society that is not paid for by the factory, making it a negative externality or external cost.

**228. Because of the free-rider problem, public goods are often**
A. overprovided by the market.
B. efficiently provided by the market.
C. underprovided or not provided by the market.
D. provided only to those who can afford them.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Since it's impossible to exclude non-payers, private firms have no incentive to supply public goods. This market failure often requires government provision.

**229. A good that is non-rivalrous but excludable is known as a**
A. private good.
B. public good.
C. common resource.
D. club good.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** A club good (e.g., cable TV, a private park) is one where many people can enjoy it without diminishing its availability (non-rivalrous), but people can be excluded if they don't pay.

**230. The "Tragedy of the Commons" describes the**
A. under-exploitation of a shared resource.
B. efficient management of a public good.
C. over-exploitation of a shared, rivalrous, but non-excludable resource.
D. benefit of privatizing all resources.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** The tragedy occurs because each individual has a private incentive to use as much of the common resource as possible, leading to its collective depletion or ruin.

**231. The Hedonic Pricing Method values environmental amenities by analyzing their effect on**
A. travel costs.
B. property prices.
C. survey responses.
D. defensive expenditures.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This method statistically teases out how much of a property's price can be attributed to environmental factors like clean air, a good view, or proximity to a park.

**232. A tax levied on each unit of pollution a firm emits is called a**
A. corporate income tax.
B. value-added tax.
C. Pigouvian tax.
D. tariff.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Named after Arthur Pigou, a Pigouvian tax is a market-based solution designed to correct for a negative externality by making the emitter pay the cost of the externality.

**233. An emissions trading system is also known as a**
A. command-and-control system.
B. subsidy system.
C. Pigouvian tax system.
D. cap-and-trade system.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Cap-and-trade sets a total limit (cap) on emissions and allows firms to trade permits. This creates a market for the right to pollute and provides an incentive to reduce emissions efficiently.

**234. In Cost-Benefit Analysis (CBA), a project is considered economically worthwhile if the**
A. total cost is greater than the total benefit.
B. Net Present Value (NPV) is positive.
C. environmental impacts are zero.
D. number of benefits is greater than the number of costs.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The NPV is the present value of all benefits minus the present value of all costs. A positive NPV means the monetized benefits outweigh the costs, making the project economically desirable.

**235. Which of the following is an example of a renewable resource?**
A. Coal
B. Crude oil
C. Forests
D. Copper ore
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Forests are a renewable resource because, if managed sustainably, they can be replenished through natural growth and reforestation. The others are non-renewable.

**236. The challenge in managing non-renewable resources primarily concerns the**
A. rate of regeneration.
B. rate of depletion.
C. sustainable yield.
D. free-rider problem.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Since non-renewable resources exist in a finite stock, the central management issue is deciding how quickly to use them up, balancing current and future needs.

**237. The Contingent Valuation Method asks people their**
A. past travel expenses.
B. income and education levels.
C. willingness to pay for an environmental good.
D. property value.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** CVM is a survey-based technique that creates a hypothetical market and directly asks people what they would be willing to pay (or accept) for a change in an environmental resource.

**238. From an economic perspective, climate change is considered a**
A. positive externality.
B. global negative externality.
C. pure public good.
D. local commons problem.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** GHG emissions by any one country or firm impose costs (climate damages) on the entire world, and these costs are not reflected in the price of fossil fuels. This makes it the ultimate global negative externality.

**239. A key characteristic of a public good is that it is**
A. non-rivalrous and non-excludable.
B. rivalrous and excludable.
C. provided only by private firms.
D. scarce.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** These are the two defining properties of a public good: one person's use doesn't prevent another's (non-rivalrous), and people cannot be stopped from using it (non-excludable).

**240. Which of the following is the best solution for the "Tragedy of the Commons" in a local fishery?**
A. Subsidizing fishing boats.
B. Encouraging more people to fish.
C. Establishing clear property rights or a system of tradable quotas.
D. Doing nothing, as the market will self-correct.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** To prevent overfishing, the open-access nature of the fishery must be addressed. Establishing ownership or limiting the total catch through enforceable quotas creates an incentive for conservation.

### **Final Comprehensive Questions (Covering multiple chapters)**

**241. A country's government decides to increase spending on education, financed by borrowing. This is an example of**
A. contractionary monetary policy.
B. expansionary fiscal policy which could have a crowding-out effect.
C. supply-side policy to increase exports.
D. a balanced budget policy.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Increasing government spending is an expansionary fiscal policy. Financing it through borrowing can increase interest rates, potentially "crowding out" private investment. The spending on education is also an investment in human capital (Ch 14).

**242. A depreciating domestic currency will likely lead to**
A. a fall in the price of imported goods and a decrease in exports.
B. a rise in the price of imported goods and an increase in exports.
C. an improvement in the country's terms of trade.
D. a decrease in demand-pull inflation.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Depreciation makes domestic goods cheaper for foreigners, boosting exports. It also makes foreign goods more expensive for domestic consumers, raising the price of imports (Ch 12 & 11).

**243. The development of an E-commerce platform that connects Ghanaian textile producers directly with European buyers primarily relates to the concepts of**
A. monetary policy and infant industries.
B. globalization and export-oriented growth.
C. public goods and the tragedy of the commons.
D. price floors and government deficits.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** This represents globalization (connecting markets via technology, Ch 15) and facilitates an export-oriented strategy for the textile producers (Ch 14).

**244. If a country's MPC is 0.8 and it experiences a fall in investment of GHS 10 billion due to a global recession, what is the maximum potential fall in its national income?**
A. GHS 8 billion
B. GHS 12.5 billion
C. GHS 10 billion
D. GHS 50 billion
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Multiplier = 1 / (1 - MPC) = 1 / 0.2 = 5. Change in Income = Multiplier x Change in Autonomous Spending = 5 x (-10 billion) = -50 billion. This links the multiplier (Ch 9) to a cause of business cycles (Ch 10) and globalization (Ch 15).

**245. An increase in the world price of crude oil would likely cause which combination of effects in an oil-importing country?**
A. Cost-push inflation and an improvement in the current account.
B. Demand-pull inflation and an improvement in the terms of trade.
C. Cost-push inflation and a deterioration of the current account.
D. A rightward shift in the SRAS and a fall in the domestic price level.
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** Higher oil prices are a negative supply shock, causing cost-push inflation (SRAS shifts left, Ch 10). For an oil-importer, it also means a higher import bill, worsening the current account balance (Ch 12).

**246. A central bank's policy of raising interest rates to combat inflation is likely to cause the country's currency to**
A. appreciate due to capital inflows.
B. depreciate due to capital outflows.
C. devalue.
D. have no effect on the exchange rate.
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Higher interest rates attract foreign capital seeking better returns, increasing demand for the domestic currency and causing it to appreciate (Ch 8, 10, 12).

**247. The construction of a new hydro-electric dam by a government represents**
A. an increase in C and an investment in physical capital.
B. an increase in G and an investment in physical capital.
C. a transfer payment and a current account transaction.
D. a recurrent expenditure and an investment in human capital.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** It is government spending (G) on infrastructure (Ch 8 & 7) and an addition to the nation's stock of physical capital (Ch 6).

**248. The introduction of a minimum wage (a price floor in the labor market) set above the equilibrium wage is likely to**
A. increase employment and create a labor shortage.
B. decrease employment and create a labor surplus (unemployment).
C. have no effect on the labor market.
D. shift the demand curve for labor to the right.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A binding price floor raises the price (wage), causing quantity supplied (workers willing to work) to exceed quantity demanded (jobs offered by firms), leading to unemployment (a surplus of labor) (Ch 2 & 10).

**249. Which of the following policies addresses a negative externality using a market-based approach?**
A. Banning the production of a polluting good.
B. Setting a strict limit on the amount of pollution allowed.
C. Launching an advertising campaign to discourage pollution.
D. Imposing a tax on each unit of pollution emitted.
**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** A tax (Pigouvian tax) is a market-based instrument because it uses the price mechanism to alter incentives, unlike a command-and-control regulation like a ban or a limit (Ch 16).

**250. When a developed country's company offshores its call center to a developing country, it primarily seeks to reduce costs. For the developing country, this represents an**
A. import of services and a debit on its current account.
B. export of services and a credit on its current account.
C. inflow of FDI and a credit on its financial account.
D. outflow of human capital and a fall in employment.
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The developing country is providing a call center *service* to a foreign company and receiving payment for it. This is an export of a service, recorded as a credit in its current account (Ch 12 & 15).
Excellent. Here is the second set of 250 WASSCE-standard Multiple Choice Questions, with answers and explanations, continuing from the previous set and based on your provided textbook content.

Chapter 1: Introduction to Economics and Basic Concepts

251. A key difference between 'scarcity' and a 'shortage' is that scarcity is
A. temporary, while a shortage is permanent.
B. a universal and permanent condition, while a shortage is temporary.
C. caused by high prices, while a shortage is caused by low prices.
D. the same as poverty, while a shortage is not.
Answer: B
Explanation: Scarcity is the fundamental, permanent problem of limited resources relative to unlimited wants. A shortage is a temporary market condition where quantity demanded exceeds quantity supplied at a specific price.

252. The payment made for the use of capital as a factor of production is
A. Rent.
B. Profit.
C. Interest.
D. Wages.
Answer: C
Explanation: Interest is the income earned by the owners of capital for its use in the production process.

253. Which of the following best illustrates the concept of 'alternative uses'?
A. A student can use their time to either study or watch a movie.
B. A factory can produce both cars and trucks.
C. A farm can grow cocoa and maize.
D. All of the above.
Answer: D
Explanation: The concept of alternative uses, a key part of the economic problem, means that a scarce resource (like time, a factory, or a farm) can be put to different productive purposes.

254. A mixed economic system is characterized by the co-existence of
A. the primary and secondary sectors.
B. a central bank and commercial banks.
C. private enterprise and government intervention.
D. domestic and international trade.
Answer: C
Explanation: A mixed economy blends the price mechanism and private ownership of a market system with government ownership, regulation, and provision of public goods found in a command system.

255. The concept of 'choice' in economics arises directly from
A. the existence of unlimited wants and scarce resources.
B. the government's need to tax its citizens.
C. the interaction between supply and demand.
D. the desire of firms to make a profit.
Answer: A
Explanation: Because resources are scarce and wants are unlimited, choices must be made about how to allocate those resources, which wants to satisfy, and which to leave unsatisfied.

256. Which of the following is NOT a primary function of an entrepreneur?
A. Undertaking risk-bearing.
B. Operating machinery on the factory floor.
C. Making key business decisions.
D. Coordinating other factors of production.
Answer: B
Explanation: Operating machinery is a function of labor. The entrepreneur's role is to organize, make strategic decisions, innovate, and bear the risks associated with the business.

257. An economy is operating on its Production Possibility Curve (PPC). To produce more of Good A, it must
A. improve its technology.
B. reduce unemployment.
C. produce less of Good B.
D. wait for the population to grow.
Answer: C
Explanation: Being on the PPC means the economy is operating efficiently. Therefore, the only way to increase the production of one good is to shift resources away from the production of another good, which demonstrates opportunity cost.

258. The 'perishability' of labor as a factor of production means that
A. a laborer's skills decline over time.
B. labor cannot be stored for future use.
C. a laborer can easily move between jobs.
D. labor is a human factor.
Answer: B
Explanation: If a worker is idle for a day, that day's labor is lost forever and cannot be saved and used later. It is perishable.

259. A society in which economic roles are passed down from generation to generation and production methods are primitive operates a
A. command system.
B. market system.
C. mixed system.
D. traditional system.
Answer: D
Explanation: A traditional economic system bases its economic decisions on custom, habit, and historical precedent.

260. Which of these falls under the study of Microeconomics?
A. The determination of the market price of rice.
B. The national unemployment rate.
C. The country's overall inflation rate.
D. The rate of economic growth (GDP).
Answer: A
Explanation: Microeconomics focuses on the behavior and decisions of individual economic units, such as households, firms, and the determination of price and output in a specific market (like the market for rice).

261. The problem of 'how to produce' in an economy is concerned with
A. the choice of goods and services.
B. the distribution of output.
C. the choice of production techniques.
D. the final consumers of the product.
Answer: C
Explanation: This fundamental question deals with the methods of production to be used—for example, whether to use labor-intensive or capital-intensive techniques.

262. Capital, as a factor of production, is distinct from financial capital because it refers to
A. money and stocks.
B. man-made goods used for further production.
C. profits reinvested in the business.
D. natural resources.
Answer: B
Explanation: In economics, capital refers to physical, man-made assets like machinery, tools, and factories that are used to produce other goods and services, not money itself.

263. An increase in a country's population, without a corresponding increase in technology or capital, will likely
A. shift its PPC inwards.
B. have no effect on its PPC.
C. cause a movement along its PPC.
D. cause a biased outward shift in its PPC along the labor-intensive good axis.
Answer: D
Explanation: An increase in one factor (labor) will increase the potential production of all goods, but will most significantly increase the potential production of goods that use labor intensively, causing a biased or non-parallel shift.

264. Human wants are said to be 'complementary' when
A. they compete for limited resources.
B. satisfying one want creates another.
C. two or more wants must be satisfied together.
D. they recur at regular intervals.
Answer: C
Explanation: Complementary wants are those that go together, like a car and petrol, or a phone and a charger. The satisfaction of one requires the satisfaction of the other.

265. What is the opportunity cost of a government choosing to spend funds on building a new hospital?
A. The cost of doctors' salaries.
B. The future revenue the hospital will generate.
C. The new school or road that could have been built instead.
D. The total construction cost of the hospital.
Answer: C
Explanation: The opportunity cost is the value of the next best alternative use of those government funds, which might have been a school, a road, or another public project.

Chapter 2: Demand, Supply, and Market Equilibrium

266. A rightward shift in the demand curve for a product is called
A. an increase in quantity demanded.
B. an extension of demand.
C. a decrease in demand.
D. an increase in demand.
Answer: D
Explanation: A shift of the entire curve to the right signifies an "increase in demand," meaning more is demanded at every price, caused by a change in a non-price determinant.

267. If the government announces that the price of petrol will increase significantly next week, the demand for petrol this week will likely
A. decrease.
B. shift to the right.
C. shift to the left.
D. become perfectly elastic.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is due to consumer expectations. Consumers will increase their current demand to buy petrol now at the lower price before the expected price hike, causing the demand curve to shift right.

268. An 'exceptional' or 'abnormal' demand curve slopes
A. downwards from left to right.
B. upwards from left to right.
C. horizontally.
D. vertically.
Answer: B
Explanation: An exceptional demand curve, such as for a Giffen good or a Veblen good (article of ostentation), shows a direct relationship where a higher price leads to a higher quantity demanded.

269. The market is in equilibrium when
A. quantity demanded is greater than quantity supplied.
B. price is at its lowest possible level.
C. quantity demanded equals quantity supplied.
D. there is a surplus of goods.
Answer: C
Explanation: Equilibrium is the state of balance in a market where the plans of buyers and sellers match, resulting in the quantity demanded being exactly equal to the quantity supplied.

270. Joint supply occurs when
A. two or more goods are needed together to satisfy a want.
B. a single firm supplies the entire market.
C. the production of one good results in the production of another good.
D. many firms jointly agree on a price.
Answer: C
Explanation: Joint supply refers to goods that are produced together from the same source, such as beef and leather from a cow. An increase in the supply of one leads to an increase in the supply of the other.

271. If a firm's total revenue decreases when it lowers its price, the price elasticity of demand for its product is
A. elastic.
B. unit elastic.
C. perfectly elastic.
D. inelastic.
Answer: D
Explanation: When demand is inelastic (PED < 1), the percentage increase in quantity demanded from a price cut is smaller than the percentage decrease in price, leading to a fall in total revenue.

272. A perfectly inelastic supply curve is
A. horizontal.
B. vertical.
C. upward sloping.
D. downward sloping.
Answer: B
Explanation: Perfectly inelastic supply (PES = 0) means that the quantity supplied does not change at all, regardless of the price. This is represented by a vertical supply curve.

273. The imposition of an indirect tax on a good with perfectly inelastic demand will result in the tax burden being borne
A. entirely by the seller.
B. entirely by the buyer.
C. equally by the buyer and seller.
D. entirely by the government.
Answer: B
Explanation: If demand is perfectly inelastic, consumers will buy the same quantity regardless of the price. Therefore, producers can pass the entire tax on to the consumers in the form of a higher price, and consumers will bear the full burden.

274. If the price of pens rises from GHS 2 to GHS 3, and the quantity demanded falls from 100 to 50, what is the arc (midpoint) price elasticity of demand?
A. -1.67 (Elastic)
B. -0.6 (Inelastic)
C. -1.0 (Unit Elastic)
D. -1.5 (Elastic)
Answer: A
Explanation: %ΔQ = (50-100)/((50+100)/2) = -50/75 = -0.667. %ΔP = (3-2)/((3+2)/2) = 1/2.5 = 0.4. PED = -0.667 / 0.4 = -1.67.

275. If a binding price ceiling is imposed on a good, which of the following is NOT a likely consequence?
A. A shortage of the good.
B. The emergence of a black market.
C. Non-price rationing mechanisms like queuing.
D. A surplus of the good.
Answer: D
Explanation: A price ceiling (a maximum price) set below the equilibrium price causes quantity demanded to exceed quantity supplied, leading to a shortage, not a surplus.

276. A key determinant of the price elasticity of supply is the
A. proportion of income spent on the good.
B. availability of consumer substitutes.
C. time period under consideration.
D. income of consumers.
Answer: C
Explanation: Supply is more elastic in the long run than in the short run because producers have more time to adjust their production processes and capacity in response to a price change.

277. Producer surplus is graphically represented as the area
A. below the demand curve and above the price.
B. above the supply curve and below the price.
C. between the supply and demand curves.
D. below the demand curve.
Answer: B
Explanation: Producer surplus is the difference between the price producers receive and the minimum price they would have been willing to accept (their marginal cost, represented by the supply curve).

278. An income elasticity of demand of -0.4 indicates that the good is
A. a normal good and a necessity.
B. a normal good and a luxury.
C. a substitute good.
D. an inferior good.
Answer: D
Explanation: A negative income elasticity of demand (YED < 0) is the defining characteristic of an inferior good, meaning that as consumer income rises, demand for the good falls.

279. The demand for a product is said to be 'derived' when it is demanded
A. for its own sake.
B. by consumers from all income groups.
C. because it is an input for the production of another good.
D. jointly with another product.
Answer: C
Explanation: Derived demand occurs when the demand for one good (often a factor of production or an intermediate good) is dependent on the demand for the final good it helps to produce (e.g., the demand for wood is derived from the demand for furniture).

280. If an increase in the price of Good X leads to a decrease in the demand for Good Y, then the cross-price elasticity is
A. positive and the goods are substitutes.
B. negative and the goods are complements.
C. positive and the goods are complements.
D. negative and the goods are substitutes.
Answer: B
Explanation: This pattern describes complementary goods (goods used together). If the price of X rises, less X is consumed, and therefore less Y is consumed as well. The XED will be negative.

Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly

281. In a perfectly competitive market, the condition P = AR = MR holds true because
A. firms are price makers.
B. products are differentiated.
C. the firm can sell any quantity at the constant market price.
D. there are significant barriers to entry.
Answer: C
Explanation: As a price taker, the firm faces a horizontal demand curve. The price (P) is constant. Average revenue (TR/Q) is also the price. Marginal revenue (ΔTR/ΔQ) from selling one more unit is also the constant price.

282. In perfect competition, supernormal profits can be earned
A. only in the long run.
B. both in the short run and the long run.
C. never.
D. only in the short run.
Answer: D
Explanation: In the short run, a firm can earn supernormal profits if the market price is above its ATC. However, these profits attract new entrants, which drives the price down to the normal profit level in the long run.

283. Price discrimination is the practice of
A. charging different prices based on production costs.
B. selling different products at the same price.
C. selling the same product to different buyers at different prices.
D. charging a price higher than marginal cost.
Answer: C
Explanation: Price discrimination involves charging different prices for the same good or service where the price difference is not justified by a difference in cost. It is a practice available to firms with market power, like monopolists.

284. Which of these is a LEGAL barrier to entry for a monopoly?
A. Predatory pricing.
B. A patent on an invention.
C. High advertising expenditure.
D. Economies of scale.
Answer: B
Explanation: A patent is a legal right granted by the government that gives the inventor exclusive rights to their invention for a period, creating a legal monopoly. Predatory pricing is usually illegal.

285. A monopolist will set its output where MR=MC, and determine its price from the
A. supply curve.
B. demand (AR) curve.
C. marginal cost curve.
D. average total cost curve.
Answer: B
Explanation: After finding the profit-maximizing quantity (Q*) where MR=MC, the monopolist goes up to the demand curve to find the highest price consumers are willing to pay for that quantity.

286. Compared to a single-price monopolist, a perfectly price-discriminating monopolist will produce
A. a lower output and have a smaller deadweight loss.
B. a higher output and have a smaller deadweight loss.
C. the same output but earn higher profits.
D. a higher output but have a larger deadweight loss.
Answer: B
Explanation: A perfectly price-discriminating monopolist charges each customer their maximum willingness to pay. This means its MR curve is the same as its demand curve. It produces where P=MC (the allocatively efficient output), capturing all consumer surplus as profit and eliminating deadweight loss.

287. Productive inefficiency in a monopoly occurs because the monopolist
A. always produces at minimum ATC.
B. may not produce at the minimum point of its ATC curve.
C. produces where P > MC.
D. produces where MR=MC.
Answer: B
Explanation: Unlike a perfectly competitive firm in the long run, a monopolist faces no competitive pressure to produce at the lowest possible average cost (minimum ATC). Its profit-maximizing output level often does not coincide with this point.

288. A key assumption of the model of perfect competition is
A. perfect mobility of factors of production.
B. significant government intervention.
C. a small number of large firms.
D. interdependence between firms.
Answer: A
Explanation: For the long-run adjustment process to work smoothly (firms entering and exiting), it is assumed that resources like labor and capital can move freely between industries in response to profit signals.

289. In a perfectly competitive market, market price is determined by
A. the largest firm in the industry.
B. the government.
C. the interaction of industry demand and supply.
D. the sum of the costs of all firms.
Answer: C
Explanation: While individual firms are price takers, the overall market price they take is determined by the collective actions of all buyers and sellers in the industry.

290. What is a "break-even" price for a perfectly competitive firm?
A. The price that equals minimum average variable cost.
B. The price that equals marginal cost.
C. The price that equals minimum average total cost.
D. The price at which the firm maximizes revenue.
Answer: C
Explanation: The break-even point is where the firm earns zero economic profit (a normal profit). This occurs when the price is exactly equal to the average total cost, so TR=TC. This happens at the minimum point of the ATC curve in long-run equilibrium.

Chapter 4: Monopolistic Competition and Oligopoly

291. Firms in monopolistic competition have some control over their price because
A. they sell identical products.
B. there are high barriers to entry.
C. they sell differentiated products.
D. there are few firms in the industry.
Answer: C
Explanation: Product differentiation creates a form of mini-monopoly for each firm over its specific brand or version of the product, allowing it to have a downward-sloping demand curve and some price-setting power.

292. A Nash Equilibrium in game theory is an outcome where
A. all players receive the same payoff.
B. one player dominates all others.
C. each player is doing the best they can, given the actions of the other players.
D. the total payoff for all players is maximized.
Answer: C
Explanation: In a Nash equilibrium, no player has an incentive to unilaterally change their strategy, because their current strategy is their best response to the strategies of their opponents.

293. Which market structure is most likely to have firms engaging in a price war?
A. Perfect competition
B. Monopoly
C. Oligopoly
D. Monopolistic competition
Answer: C
Explanation: Due to interdependence, a price cut by one firm in an oligopoly often triggers retaliatory price cuts from rivals, which can escalate into a destructive price war. This is why oligopolists often try to avoid price competition.

294. Compared to perfect competition, monopolistic competition is less efficient due to
A. P > MC and P > minimum ATC in the long run.
B. firms earning supernormal profits in the long run.
C. government regulation.
D. a vertical demand curve.
Answer: A
Explanation: Monopolistic competition is allocatively inefficient because price is greater than marginal cost, and it is productively inefficient because firms operate with excess capacity (price is greater than minimum ATC).

295. Which of these is a characteristic of oligopoly but not monopolistic competition?
A. Downward-sloping demand curve.
B. Product differentiation.
C. Strategic interdependence and potential for collusion.
D. Earning normal profits in the long run.
Answer: C
Explanation: The need to consider rivals' reactions (interdependence) and the possibility of forming cartels (collusion) are hallmarks of an oligopoly, with its few dominant firms. In monopolistic competition, firms are assumed to act independently.

296. A market with a very large number of firms selling differentiated products is
A. a monopoly.
B. an oligopoly.
C. perfect competition.
D. monopolistic competition.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is the definition of monopolistic competition, blending a "large number of firms" (like perfect competition) with "differentiated products" (like monopoly).

297. Predatory pricing is a strategy where a firm
A. sets a high price to signal high quality.
B. sets a very low price to drive competitors out of the market.
C. matches the price changes of its rivals.
D. forms a cartel with its rivals to fix prices.
Answer: B
Explanation: Predatory pricing is an aggressive (and often illegal) competitive strategy to eliminate rivals by temporarily selling at a loss, with the goal of later raising prices to monopoly levels.

298. Oligopolists often prefer non-price competition because
A. it is cheaper than price competition.
B. price competition can lead to mutually destructive price wars.
C. their products are identical.
D. government regulations ban price changes.
Answer: B
Explanation: Since price cuts are easily and quickly matched by rivals, they can easily spiral into price wars that lower profits for all firms involved. Non-price strategies like branding can provide a more sustainable competitive advantage.

299. The defining characteristic used to distinguish between different market structures is the
A. location of the firms.
B. age of the industry.
C. number and size of firms, and nature of the product.
D. level of government taxation.
Answer: C
Explanation: Market structures are classified based on characteristics like the number of sellers, degree of product differentiation, ease of entry, and nature of competition.

300. In the kinked demand curve model, the demand curve is more elastic
A. for price increases than for price decreases.
B. for price decreases than for price increases.
C. at all points.
D. at the kink.
Answer: A
Explanation: The model assumes that if a firm raises its price, rivals will not follow, so the firm will lose a lot of customers (elastic demand). If it lowers its price, rivals will match, so it gains few customers (inelastic demand).

Chapter 5: Consumer Theory

301. Cardinal utility theory suggests that consumer satisfaction is
A. unmeasurable.
B. measurable and comparable in numerical units.
C. always increasing.
D. constant across all individuals.
Answer: B
Explanation: The cardinal approach assumes utility can be measured in units called "utils," which can be added and compared, much like weight or height.

302. What happens to Total Utility (TU) when Marginal Utility (MU) is positive but diminishing?
A. TU decreases at an increasing rate.
B. TU increases at a constant rate.
C. TU increases at a decreasing rate.
D. TU remains constant.
Answer: C
Explanation: Each additional unit adds a positive but smaller amount to the total, so the total utility continues to rise, but the curve becomes less steep.

303. An indifference curve for two perfect substitutes would be
A. a straight line with a negative slope.
B. L-shaped.
C. convex to the origin.
D. concave to the origin.
Answer: A
Explanation: If two goods are perfect substitutes, the consumer is willing to trade them at a constant rate. This results in a constant Marginal Rate of Substitution (MRS), making the indifference curve a straight line.

304. The Marginal Rate of Substitution (MRS) is the
A. slope of the budget line.
B. ratio of the prices of two goods.
C. rate at which a consumer is willing to trade one good for another while maintaining the same utility.
D. rate at which total utility diminishes.
Answer: C
Explanation: MRS is the slope of the indifference curve and reflects the consumer's personal willingness to substitute between two goods based on their preferences.

305. A decrease in the price of one of the two goods a consumer buys will cause the budget line to
A. shift inwards in a parallel manner.
B. shift outwards in a parallel manner.
C. become steeper or flatter by pivoting.
D. become kinked.
Answer: C
Explanation: A price change for one good changes the slope of the budget line (-Px/Py). It pivots around the intercept of the good whose price did not change.

306. For a normal good, the income effect and substitution effect of a price fall
A. work in opposite directions.
B. both lead to a decrease in quantity demanded.
C. both lead to an increase in quantity demanded.
D. cancel each other out.
Answer: C
Explanation: For a normal good, the substitution effect (buy more because it's relatively cheaper) and the income effect (buy more because real income has increased) both work in the same direction to increase the quantity demanded.

307. The Price Consumption Curve (PCC) can be used to derive an individual's
A. supply curve.
B. Engel curve.
C. demand curve.
D. budget line.
Answer: C
Explanation: The PCC tracks the consumer's equilibrium points as the price of one good changes. By plotting these price-quantity combinations, the individual's demand curve can be derived.

308. If the Engel curve for a good slopes downwards (bends backwards) after a certain income level, the good is
A. a normal good.
B. a luxury good.
C. a substitute good.
D. an inferior good.
Answer: D
Explanation: A backward-bending Engel curve shows that as income rises beyond a point, the quantity demanded of the good falls, which is the definition of an inferior good.

309. The "Weak Axiom of Revealed Preference" (WARP) ensures
A. consumers always maximize utility.
B. consumer choices are consistent.
C. consumer preferences are transitive.
D. indifference curves are convex.
Answer: B
Explanation: WARP states that if a consumer chooses bundle A over bundle B when both are affordable, they will not then choose bundle B over bundle A in a situation where both are still affordable. It ensures consistency.

310. Consumer sovereignty means
A. consumers can buy anything they want.
B. consumer choices ultimately determine what is produced.
C. consumers have the highest income in the economy.
D. consumers are not affected by advertising.
Answer: B
Explanation: In a market economy, producers respond to consumer demand. The spending choices of consumers send signals to firms about what to produce, a concept known as consumer sovereignty.

Chapter 6: Producer Theory

311. Economic profit is calculated as Total Revenue minus
A. only explicit costs.
B. only variable costs.
C. both explicit and implicit costs.
D. only fixed costs.
Answer: C
Explanation: Economic profit gives a true picture of a firm's profitability by subtracting all costs, including the opportunity costs of the firm's own resources (implicit costs).

312. The law of diminishing marginal returns is a phenomenon of the
A. short run, when a variable input is added to a fixed input.
B. long run, when all inputs are variable.
C. market period, when supply is fixed.
D. secular period, covering many years.
Answer: A
Explanation: This law specifically applies in the short run, where at least one factor of production is held constant while others are varied.

313. When the marginal product (MP) of a variable input is rising, the marginal cost (MC) of output is
A. rising.
B. falling.
C. constant.
D. at its maximum.
Answer: B
Explanation: There is an inverse relationship between marginal product and marginal cost. When each additional worker (MP) is adding more to output than the previous one, the additional cost (MC) of each unit of output is falling.

314. The U-shape of the short-run average total cost (ATC) curve is explained by
A. economies and diseconomies of scale.
B. the law of diminishing marginal utility.
C. initially increasing, then diminishing marginal returns to the variable input.
D. the firm's goal of profit maximization.
Answer: C
Explanation: Initially, as increasing returns set in, average costs fall. Eventually, as diminishing returns set in, average costs begin to rise, creating the U-shape. Economies of scale explain the shape of the long-run AC curve.

315. The Minimum Efficient Scale (MES) of production is the output level at which
A. short-run average cost is minimized.
B. long-run average cost is first minimized.
C. marginal cost equals average cost.
D. total revenue equals total cost.
Answer: B
Explanation: MES is a long-run concept. It is the lowest level of output at which a firm can achieve the full benefits of economies of scale and minimize its long-run average cost.

316. An isocost line shows all combinations of two inputs that
A. produce the same level of output.
B. have the same price.
C. a firm can purchase for a given total expenditure.
D. are technically efficient.
Answer: C
Explanation: An isocost line is the producer's equivalent of a consumer's budget line, showing all combinations of labor and capital that can be bought for a certain total cost outlay.

317. For a firm operating in an imperfectly competitive market, its demand curve is
A. upward sloping.
B. horizontal.
C. downward sloping.
D. vertical.
Answer: C
Explanation: Firms in imperfect competition (monopoly, oligopoly, monopolistic competition) face downward-sloping demand curves because they have some price-setting power and must lower their price to sell more units.

318. If a firm's marginal revenue is GHS 10 and its marginal cost is GHS 8, the firm should
A. decrease its output.
B. shut down.
C. increase its output.
D. keep its output constant.
Answer: C
Explanation: Since the additional revenue from the next unit (GHS 10) is greater than its additional cost (GHS 8), producing it will add GHS 2 to the firm's profit. The firm should continue to expand output until MR=MC.

319. Normal profit is considered
A. an explicit cost of production.
B. an implicit cost of production.
C. a supernormal profit.
D. the same as accounting profit.
Answer: B
Explanation: Normal profit is the minimum return required to keep the entrepreneur's talent and capital in the business. It represents the opportunity cost of those self-owned resources and is therefore an implicit cost.

320. Which of these curves always slopes downwards as output increases?
A. Marginal Cost (MC)
B. Average Variable Cost (AVC)
C. Average Total Cost (ATC)
D. Average Fixed Cost (AFC)
Answer: D
Explanation: Since Total Fixed Cost (TFC) is constant, Average Fixed Cost (TFC/Q) must decline continuously as output (Q) increases.

Chapter 7: National Income Accounting

321. "Imputed rent" refers to the
A. rent paid by tenants to landlords.
B. estimated rental value of owner-occupied housing.
C. rent paid on government buildings.
D. unpaid rent by defaulting tenants.
Answer: B
Explanation: To ensure comprehensive measurement, national income accounts include an imputed value for the "service" that owner-occupied homes provide to their owners, as if they were renting their homes to themselves.

322. The largest component of the expenditure approach to GDP in most economies is
A. government spending (G).
B. net exports (X-M).
C. gross investment (I).
D. consumption spending (C).
Answer: D
Explanation: Spending by households on goods and services is consistently the largest part of total expenditure in virtually all market-based economies.

323. The income approach to GDP measures the sum of
A. all spending on final goods and services.
B. the value added by all sectors of the economy.
C. all factor incomes: wages, rent, interest, and profit.
D. all tax revenue and government borrowing.
Answer: C
Explanation: The income approach calculates GDP by summing all the incomes earned by the factors of production for their contribution to generating that output.

324. A country's GDP will be greater than its GNP if
A. its exports are greater than its imports.
B. income earned by foreigners in the country is greater than income earned by its residents abroad.
C. its residents earn more abroad than foreigners earn in the country.
D. its depreciation is very high.
Answer: B
Explanation: GNP = GDP + NFIA. If the income flowing out to foreign factors is larger than the income flowing in from resident factors abroad, then Net Factor Income from Abroad (NFIA) is negative, making GNP smaller than GDP.

325. Real GDP per capita is often used as a proxy for
A. the rate of inflation.
B. the level of income inequality.
C. the average standard of living.
D. the size of the population.
Answer: C
Explanation: By measuring the real output per person, real GDP per capita gives a rough indication of the average amount of goods and services available to each individual, which is often used as a measure of average living standards.

Chapter 8: Money, Banking, and Fiscal Policy

326. The ability to use money for borrowing and lending is related to which function of money?
A. Medium of exchange
B. Unit of account
C. Store of value
D. Standard of deferred payment
Answer: D
Explanation: This function means that money can be used to denominate debts and future payments, which is the foundation of all credit transactions.

327. "High-powered money" or the "monetary base" is denoted by
A. M0
B. M1
C. M2
D. M3
Answer: A
Explanation: M0, the monetary base, consists of currency in circulation plus commercial bank reserves held at the central bank. It is the money directly controlled by the monetary authority.

328. If the central bank lowers the discount rate, it is engaging in
A. expansionary monetary policy.
B. contractionary monetary policy.
C. expansionary fiscal policy.
D. contractionary fiscal policy.
Answer: A
Explanation: Lowering the discount rate makes it cheaper for commercial banks to borrow from the central bank, encouraging them to lend more and thus expanding the money supply.

329. If the required reserve ratio is 20%, what is the maximum amount of demand deposits the banking system can create from an initial new deposit of GHS 1,000?
A. GHS 200
B. GHS 1,000
C. GHS 4,000
D. GHS 5,000
Answer: D
Explanation: Multiplier = 1 / RRR = 1 / 0.20 = 5. Total Deposit Creation = Initial Deposit x Multiplier = 1,000 x 5 = GHS 5,000.

330. A regressive tax is one that takes a
A. smaller percentage of income from high-income earners than from low-income earners.
B. larger percentage of income from high-income earners than from low-income earners.
C. constant percentage of income from all earners.
D. tax on exported goods.
Answer: A
Explanation: A regressive tax places a heavier proportional burden on the poor than on the rich. A lump-sum tax, or a tax on a basic necessity, would have this effect.

331. The primary deficit is defined as
A. Total Expenditure - Total Revenue.
B. Revenue Deficit - Grants.
C. Fiscal Deficit - Interest Payments.
D. Fiscal Deficit + Borrowings.
Answer: C
Explanation: The primary deficit shows the borrowing requirement of the government for the current year's activities, excluding the interest burden inherited from past debts.

332. Moral suasion, as a central bank tool, involves
A. setting legal limits on lending.
B. changing interest rates.
C. buying and selling government bonds.
D. persuading commercial banks to follow a certain policy.
Answer: D
Explanation: Moral suasion is a qualitative credit control tool where the central bank uses its influence and powers of persuasion to encourage commercial banks to voluntarily adopt certain lending practices.

333. Which of the following is a function of a central bank but not typically a commercial bank?
A. Accepting deposits.
B. Providing loans.
C. Acting as a lender of last resort.
D. Foreign exchange transactions.
Answer: C
Explanation: The central bank provides emergency liquidity to commercial banks that are solvent but face short-term liquidity problems. This role as lender of last resort is exclusive to the central bank.

334. When a government finances its deficit by borrowing from the central bank (printing money), it is known as
A. debt financing.
B. deficit financing or monetization of debt.
C. external borrowing.
D. fiscal consolidation.
Answer: B
Explanation: This method involves the central bank creating new money to lend to the government, which directly increases the money supply and can be highly inflationary.

335. Which tax structure promotes income equality the most?
A. Proportional tax
B. Regressive tax
C. Indirect tax
D. Progressive tax
Answer: D
Explanation: A progressive tax system, by taking a larger percentage of income from higher earners, works to reduce the gap between high and low incomes after tax.

Chapter 9: Classical and Keynesian Models

336. A key belief of classical economists was that
A. wages and prices are sticky downwards.
B. the economy is self-regulating and tends towards full employment.
C. government intervention is necessary to manage demand.
D. the short-run is more important than the long-run.
Answer: B
Explanation: Classical theory is built on the foundation of flexible prices, wages, and interest rates that automatically adjust to clear all markets and restore full employment.

337. The "Paradox of Thrift" suggests that if everyone tries to save more during a recession,
A. total savings will increase and the economy will grow.
B. total savings may not increase (or could fall) because aggregate income will fall.
C. interest rates will fall, stimulating investment immediately.
D. the government budget will move into surplus.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is a Keynesian concept. An attempt by everyone to increase savings means less spending, which reduces aggregate demand, leading to a fall in national income. Since saving depends on income, the fall in income can offset the initial desire to save more.

338. A decrease in taxes has a smaller multiplier effect on GDP than an equal increase in government spending because
A. the government spends money inefficiently.
B. not all of the tax cut is spent; some is saved.
C. tax cuts are only for the rich.
D. government spending is an injection, while tax cuts are a leakage.
Answer: B
Explanation: When the government spends GHS 100, all GHS 100 is immediately injected into the spending stream. When it cuts taxes by GHS 100, households will save a portion of it (determined by the MPS), so the initial injection into spending is only GHS 100 x MPC.

339. An inflationary gap occurs when
A. the intersection of AD and SRAS is to the right of the LRAS curve.
B. the intersection of AD and SRAS is to the left of the LRAS curve.
C. the price level is falling.
D. the economy is in long-run equilibrium.
Answer: A
Explanation: An inflationary gap means the economy's actual short-run output is above its long-run potential, putting upward pressure on wages and prices.

340. According to classical economists, a period of unemployment would be corrected by
A. a fall in wages.
B. an increase in government spending.
C. an increase in the money supply.
D. a rise in wages.
Answer: A
Explanation: The classical view assumes wage flexibility. A surplus of labor (unemployment) would cause the price of labor (wages) to fall, increasing the quantity of labor demanded by firms until the market clears.

341. In the Keynesian framework, investment spending is primarily determined by
A. current income.
B. interest rates and expected future profitability.
C. savings.
D. government policy.
Answer: B
Explanation: Keynes stressed that investment decisions are volatile and forward-looking, depending on firms' expectations of future profits (what he called the "marginal efficiency of capital") compared against the cost of borrowing (the interest rate).

342. A rightward shift in the Long-Run Aggregate Supply (LRAS) curve represents
A. an increase in the price level.
B. an increase in aggregate demand.
C. a temporary economic boom.
D. an increase in the economy's potential output (economic growth).
Answer: D
Explanation: The LRAS represents the productive capacity of the economy. It shifts right only if there is a long-term improvement in the quantity or quality of factors of production or in technology.

343. According to Keynes, in a deep recession, monetary policy might be ineffective because of a
A. "liquidity trap."
B. "crowding out effect."
C. "paradox of thrift."
D. "rational expectations."
Answer: A
Explanation: A liquidity trap is a situation where interest rates are so low that monetary policy (which works by lowering them further) becomes powerless because people are willing to hold any amount of extra money rather than buying bonds.

344. If an economy is operating below its potential output, what fiscal policy would a Keynesian recommend?
A. Decrease G and increase T.
B. Increase G and decrease T.
C. Increase the money supply.
D. Implement supply-side reforms.
Answer: B
Explanation: To close a recessionary gap, a Keynesian would advocate for expansionary fiscal policy—increasing government spending and/or cutting taxes—to boost aggregate demand.

345. A supply-side shock, such as a drought, would be represented in the AD/AS model by a
A. leftward shift of the AD curve.
B. rightward shift of the AD curve.
C. leftward shift of the SRAS curve.
D. rightward shift of the LRAS curve.
Answer: C
Explanation: A drought reduces agricultural output, which is a negative supply shock. It increases costs and reduces the quantity of goods that can be supplied at any given price level, shifting SRAS to the left.

Chapter 10: Inflation, Unemployment, and Business Cycles

346. Deflation is a period of
A. falling rates of inflation.
B. sustained fall in the general price level.
C. slow economic growth.
D. high unemployment.
Answer: B
Explanation: Deflation (negative inflation) is when the average price level is persistently decreasing. This is distinct from disinflation, which is a slowdown in the rate of price increases.

347. The labor force is defined as
A. the total population of a country.
B. the entire population of working age.
C. the sum of all employed and unemployed persons actively seeking work.
D. all persons who are currently employed.
Answer: C
Explanation: The labor force is the pool of people available for work. It includes those with jobs and those who are jobless but are actively looking for one.

348. A factory worker laid off during a recession is an example of
A. frictional unemployment.
B. cyclical unemployment.
C. structural unemployment.
D. seasonal unemployment.
Answer: B
Explanation: This type of job loss is directly tied to the economic downturn (the business cycle) and the lack of overall demand in the economy.

349. Supply-side policies are best suited to address
A. cyclical unemployment.
B. seasonal unemployment.
C. a lack of aggregate demand.
D. structural unemployment and the natural rate of unemployment.
Answer: D
Explanation: Supply-side policies, such as education, training, and improving labor market flexibility, aim to improve the productive capacity of the economy and address skills mismatches, which are the root causes of structural unemployment and determine the natural rate.

350. What is a likely effect of high and unpredictable inflation?
A. It encourages long-term savings in banks.
B. It increases business investment due to certainty.
C. It redistributes wealth from creditors to debtors.
D. It makes exports more competitive.
Answer: C
Explanation: Creditors are harmed because the money they are repaid has less purchasing power. Debtors benefit because they repay their fixed loans with this cheaper money.

351. The trough of a business cycle marks the
A. highest point of an expansion.
B. end of a contraction and the beginning of a recovery.
C. period of fastest economic growth.
D. point where inflation is at its highest.
Answer: B
Explanation: The trough is the bottom turning point of the business cycle. After the trough, economic activity begins to pick up again in the expansion/recovery phase.

352. The idea that there is no long-run trade-off between inflation and unemployment is associated with the concept of the
A. short-run Phillips curve.
B. law of diminishing returns.
C. vertical long-run Phillips curve.
D. Keynesian multiplier.
Answer: C
Explanation: The vertical LRPC, set at the natural rate of unemployment, illustrates that in the long run, unemployment is independent of the rate of inflation, which is determined by monetary policy.

353. Which of these is a cost of expected inflation?
A. Menu costs
B. Sudden wealth redistribution
C. A surprise fall in real wages
D. A rise in unemployment
Answer: A
Explanation: Menu costs are the real costs to firms of having to frequently change their listed prices (on menus, catalogs, price tags) due to ongoing inflation.

354. NAIRU stands for
A. National Average Investment Return Unit.
B. Non-Accelerating Inflation Rate of Unemployment.
C. Net Aggregate Income and Revenue Union.
D. Newly Acquired Investment and Real Unemployment.
Answer: B
Explanation: NAIRU is the unemployment rate at which the rate of inflation is stable (i.e., not accelerating). It is conceptually very similar to the Natural Rate of Unemployment.

355. Counter-cyclical stabilization policy would involve
A. cutting taxes during a boom and increasing them in a recession.
B. increasing government spending during a boom and cutting it in a recession.
C. easing monetary policy during a recession and tightening it during a boom.
D. balancing the budget every year, regardless of the economic cycle.
Answer: C
Explanation: Counter-cyclical policy means acting against the direction of the business cycle. Easing policy (lower interest rates) during a recession helps stimulate activity, while tightening policy (higher interest rates) during an inflationary boom helps cool it down.

Chapter 11: International Trade Theory

356. If a country can produce every good with fewer resources than its trading partner, it has
A. a comparative advantage in all goods.
B. an absolute advantage in all goods.
C. a need for protectionism.
D. no incentive to trade.
Answer: B
Explanation: Absolute advantage refers to superior productivity. However, even in this case, the country will still only have a comparative advantage (lower opportunity cost) in some, but not all, goods, meaning there are still gains from trade.

357. The terms of trade are said to deteriorate if
A. import prices fall relative to export prices.
B. export prices rise relative to import prices.
C. export prices fall relative to import prices.
D. the volume of exports increases.
Answer: C
Explanation: A deterioration means the country receives less for its exports relative to what it pays for its imports, requiring it to export more to buy the same quantity of imports.

358. The Leontief Paradox challenged the
A. theory of comparative advantage.
B. Heckscher-Ohlin model.
C. gravity model of trade.
D. infant industry argument.
Answer: B
Explanation: Wassily Leontief's empirical test found that the US (a capital-abundant country) was exporting labor-intensive goods, which contradicted the predictions of the Heckscher-Ohlin factor proportions theory.

359. A "common external tariff" is a key feature that distinguishes a
A. free trade area from a preferential trading area.
B. customs union from a free trade area.
C. common market from a customs union.
D. economic union from a common market.
Answer: B
Explanation: Both FTAs and customs unions have free trade among members. However, a customs union goes a step further by requiring all members to adopt the same set of tariffs against non-member countries.

360. Intra-industry trade refers to
A. trade between two different industries.
B. trade within a single multinational corporation.
C. a country exporting and importing similar types of goods.
D. trade in intermediate goods only.
Answer: C
Explanation: This refers to trade in differentiated products within the same industry, for example, a country both exporting and importing cars. This type of trade is not well explained by traditional comparative advantage models but by models incorporating economies of scale and product differentiation.

361. An export subsidy is a policy designed to
A. reduce the volume of exports.
B. increase the price of exports for foreign buyers.
C. decrease the price of exports for foreign buyers and increase their volume.
D. generate revenue for the government.
Answer: C
Explanation: By giving a payment to domestic producers for goods they export, the government allows them to sell at a lower price in the world market, making their exports more competitive and increasing the quantity sold.

362. One of the main arguments against trade protectionism is that it
A. increases government revenue.
B. reduces prices for domestic consumers.
C. leads to inefficient resource allocation and a net welfare loss.
D. increases the variety of goods available.
Answer: C
Explanation: By shielding inefficient domestic industries, protectionism prevents a country from fully specializing according to its comparative advantage, leading to higher consumer prices and a loss of overall economic welfare (deadweight loss).

363. The Prebisch-Singer hypothesis suggests that
A. the terms of trade for primary commodity exporters tend to decline in the long run.
B. all countries gain equally from trade.
C. free trade is always the best policy.
D. protectionism is necessary for industrialization.
Answer: A
Explanation: This hypothesis argues that the prices of primary commodities (like agricultural products and minerals) tend to fall over time relative to the prices of manufactured goods, worsening the terms of trade for developing countries that rely on exporting those commodities.

364. Rules of origin are necessary in a(n)
A. customs union.
B. common market.
C. economic union.
D. free trade area.
Answer: D
Explanation: In a free trade area, where members have different external tariffs, rules of origin are needed to prevent "trade deflection"—whereby goods from a non-member enter the bloc through the country with the lowest tariff and then move freely to other members.

365. The benefit of increased competition from international trade is that it can
A. increase profits for all domestic firms.
B. encourage domestic firms to become more efficient and innovative.
C. guarantee jobs for all domestic workers.
D. make it easier for domestic firms to form a cartel.
Answer: B
Explanation: The pressure of having to compete with foreign firms in both domestic and international markets forces domestic firms to lower costs, improve quality, and innovate in order to survive and prosper.

Chapter 12: Balance of Payments and Foreign Exchange Markets

366. If Ghana exports cocoa to Switzerland, the transaction is recorded in Ghana's Balance of Payments as a
A. credit in the current account.
B. debit in the current account.
C. credit in the financial account.
D. debit in the financial account.
Answer: A
Explanation: The export of a good earns foreign exchange, so it is a credit. Since it is a transaction in goods, it is recorded in the current account.

367. The Capital Account in the Balance of Payments primarily records
A. trade in goods and services.
B. factor income flows.
C. transfers of non-financial, non-produced assets and capital transfers like debt forgiveness.
D. transactions in shares and bonds.
Answer: C
Explanation: Under the modern (IMF) definition, the capital account is a small component that includes specific transactions like the transfer of patents or capital transfers, not to be confused with general capital flows in the financial account.

368. An item included to ensure the BoP always balances in an accounting sense is called
A. the overall balance.
B. the current account balance.
C. official reserves.
D. net errors and omissions.
Answer: D
Explanation: Due to imperfections in data collection, total debits may not equal total credits. This statistical discrepancy is a "plug" figure to make the accounts balance.

369. If a country's central bank sells its holdings of foreign currency, this transaction is recorded as a
A. debit in the reserve assets account.
B. credit in the current account.
C. debit in the current account.
D. credit in the reserve assets account.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is a counter-intuitive point. A fall in reserves is like an "export" of an asset; it represents a balancing inflow of funds to finance a deficit elsewhere. Therefore, a decrease in official reserves is recorded as a credit.

370. The "managed float" or "dirty float" exchange rate system is one where
A. the exchange rate is permanently fixed.
B. the rate is determined by market forces, with periodic central bank intervention.
C. the exchange rate is determined by the price of gold.
D. there is no central bank.
Answer: B
Explanation: A managed float is a hybrid system where the central bank does not commit to a specific rate but intervenes to smooth out volatility or guide the rate towards a desired level.

371. Appreciation of a currency means its value
A. increases under a fixed exchange rate system.
B. decreases under a fixed exchange rate system.
C. increases due to market forces under a floating system.
