D. decreases due to market forces under a floating system.
Answer: C
Explanation: "Appreciation" is the term for a market-driven rise in the value of a currency in a floating or flexible exchange rate system.

372. The theory of interest rate parity links exchange rates to
A. relative inflation rates.
B. relative interest rates.
C. relative trade balances.
D. relative income levels.
Answer: B
Explanation: Interest rate parity suggests that in a world with mobile capital, the difference in interest rates between two countries will be offset by the expected change in the exchange rate, equalizing the return on assets.

373. An American firm buys a factory in Ghana. This is recorded in Ghana's BoP as
A. a debit in the current account.
B. a credit in the current account.
C. a debit in the financial account.
D. a credit in the financial account.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is an inflow of foreign direct investment (FDI). It is an inflow of foreign currency to buy a domestic asset, so it is a credit. As it's a transaction in financial assets, it goes into the financial account.

374. One of the main functions of the World Bank is to
A. provide short-term loans to correct balance of payments problems.
B. provide long-term loans and assistance for economic development projects.
C. regulate international trade.
D. act as a central bank for the world.
Answer: B
Explanation: The World Bank's primary mission is poverty reduction and financing long-term development and reconstruction in its member countries. Short-term BoP assistance is the IMF's role.

375. If a country with a fixed exchange rate experiences a persistent BoP deficit, it may be forced to
A. revalue its currency.
B. sell its currency in the forex market.
C. devalue its currency.
D. lower its interest rates.
Answer: C
Explanation: A persistent deficit means the currency is likely overvalued. To correct this fundamentally, and to stop losing foreign reserves trying to defend the peg, the government may be forced to officially devalue the currency to make exports cheaper and imports more expensive.

Chapter 13: Concepts and Indicators of Development

376. Economic growth is a necessary but not sufficient condition for economic development because
A. development can occur without any growth.
B. growth can occur without improvements in poverty, inequality, or human well-being.
C. growth and development are mutually exclusive.
D. growth measures only the service sector.
Answer: B
Explanation: A country's GDP can increase (growth), but if the benefits are not widely shared or if they come at the expense of the environment or social welfare, then true development has not occurred.

377. Relative poverty is defined
A. by a universal standard like $2 a day.
B. in comparison to the average standard of living in a particular society.
C. by a person's ability to find a job.
D. as a lack of access to public goods.
Answer: B
Explanation: Relative poverty is a measure of inequality. A person is considered relatively poor if their income falls significantly below the median income in their own country.

378. The "vicious circle of poverty" suggests that
A. poverty is easily escaped.
B. poverty tends to be self-perpetuating.
C. only rich countries can help poor countries.
D. poverty is caused by a single factor.
Answer: B
Explanation: The concept describes how factors like low income, low savings, low investment, and low productivity can reinforce each other, trapping individuals and nations in a cycle of poverty.

379. If a country's Lorenz curve moves closer to the 45-degree line of perfect equality over time, it means that
A. income inequality has worsened.
B. the GINI coefficient has increased.
C. income inequality has improved.
D. the poverty rate has definitely fallen.
Answer: C
Explanation: Moving closer to the line of perfect equality signifies a more even distribution of income, which means inequality has decreased and the GINI coefficient has fallen.

380. A key advantage of the Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) over income-based measures is that it
A. is easier to calculate.
B. captures various simultaneous deprivations in health, education, and living standards.
C. is used for measuring inequality.
D. focuses only on extreme poverty.
Answer: B
Explanation: The MPI provides a more comprehensive picture by looking directly at non-monetary deprivations that constitute poverty.

Chapter 14: Strategies for Economic Development

381. A potential drawback of an ISI strategy is that protected domestic firms may
A. become highly efficient and innovative.
B. suffer from intense foreign competition.
C. lack the incentive to become efficient and competitive.
D. focus too much on exporting their products.
Answer: C
Explanation: When shielded from international competition by high trade barriers, firms may have little pressure to lower costs, improve quality, or innovate, leading to high-cost, low-quality goods for domestic consumers.

382. An undervalued exchange rate is a policy tool often associated with
A. Import Substitution Industrialization (ISI).
B. Export-Oriented Industrialization (EOI).
C. controlling inflation.
D. attracting foreign aid.
Answer: B
Explanation: An undervalued currency makes a country's exports cheaper for foreign buyers, giving a competitive boost to its export-oriented industries.

383. 'Tied aid' refers to foreign aid that
A. is given with no conditions.
B. must be used to purchase goods and services from the donor country.
C. is given in the form of a grant, not a loan.
D. must be repaid within one year.
Answer: B
Explanation: Tied aid is criticized because it can reduce the value of the aid to the recipient if they are forced to buy more expensive or less suitable products from the donor, rather than from the cheapest source.

384. A 'greenfield' FDI is when a foreign company
A. buys an existing factory in the host country.
B. invests in an environmentally friendly project.
C. builds a new facility from the ground up in the host country.
D. invests in the agricultural sector.
Answer: C
Explanation: Greenfield investment involves creating a brand-new operation, whereas acquiring an existing company is known as a brownfield investment or a merger/acquisition.

385. An agricultural cooperative can help small farmers increase their income by
A. encouraging them to compete against each other.
B. increasing their bargaining power in selling produce and buying inputs.
C. replacing farming with manufacturing.
D. providing direct government subsidies.
Answer: B
Explanation: By pooling their resources and produce, small farmers can negotiate better prices from buyers and get discounts on bulk purchases of inputs like seeds and fertilizer, which they could not achieve individually.

Chapter 15: Globalization and Its Impacts

386. A multinational corporation (MNC) organizing different stages of production in different countries is an example of a
A. national supply chain.
B. protectionist policy.
C. global value chain.
D. command economy.
Answer: C
Explanation: A global value chain or supply chain involves sourcing, producing, and marketing activities that are spread across multiple countries to take advantage of cost, skill, or market opportunities in each location.

387. A major concern about globalization for developing countries is the risk of
A. a 'race to the bottom' in environmental and labor standards.
B. losing access to foreign technology.
C. being unable to attract tourists.
D. facing no competition for their firms.
Answer: A
Explanation: The "race to the bottom" argument suggests that to attract investment, countries might compete by lowering their environmental regulations or worker protections, leading to negative social and environmental outcomes.

388. The main reason a firm offshores a part of its business is typically to
A. increase its domestic workforce.
B. gain more control over all processes.
C. reduce operating costs.
D. comply with domestic regulations.
Answer: C
Explanation: The primary driver for offshoring, whether through outsourcing or a captive subsidiary, is to take advantage of lower costs, particularly lower labor costs, in another country.

389. The group most likely to be negatively affected by offshoring of manufacturing jobs from a developed country is
A. the company's shareholders.
B. consumers of the company's product.
C. the low-skilled manufacturing workers in the developed country.
D. the highly-skilled engineers in the developed country.
Answer: C
Explanation: When manufacturing is moved to a lower-cost country, the domestic workers who performed those jobs are often displaced, and they may struggle to find new employment at similar wages.

390. A lesson from the 2008 Global Financial Crisis is that
A. financial deregulation always leads to stability.
B. economic events in one major country have little impact on others.
C. financial interconnectedness can rapidly spread crises across the globe.
D. a floating exchange rate system can prevent all economic problems.
Answer: C
Explanation: The GFC was a stark reminder of how deeply integrated the world's financial markets are and how quickly a crisis originating in one market (US housing) could trigger a global meltdown.

Chapter 16: Environmental Economics and Resource Management

391. A government subsidy for university education is a policy intended to correct for
A. a negative externality.
B. a positive externality.
C. the tragedy of the commons.
D. a lack of public goods.
Answer: B
Explanation: Education is believed to have significant positive externalities (benefits to society beyond the individual student). A subsidy lowers the private cost and encourages more people to get an education, moving consumption closer to the socially optimal level.

392. The 'Coase Theorem' suggests that private parties can solve externality problems on their own if
A. the government imposes a tax.
B. property rights are clearly defined and transaction costs are low.
C. the externality affects a large number of people.
D. the government provides a subsidy.
Answer: B
Explanation: Ronald Coase argued that efficient outcomes can be reached through private bargaining, without government intervention, under these specific conditions.

393. The choice of a discount rate in a Cost-Benefit Analysis is particularly important for projects
A. with short-term benefits only.
B. that have benefits and costs spread over a very long time.
C. that are funded entirely by the government.
D. with no environmental impact.
Answer: B
Explanation: A discount rate is used to convert future values to present values. For long-term projects like those dealing with climate change, a lower discount rate gives more weight to the well-being of future generations, while a higher rate de-emphasizes it.

394. What is a key difference between a carbon tax and a cap-and-trade system?
A. A carbon tax provides price certainty, while cap-and-trade provides quantity certainty.
B. A carbon tax is a market-based solution, while cap-and-trade is not.
C. A carbon tax increases emissions, while cap-and-trade reduces them.
D. Only a carbon tax generates revenue for the government.
Answer: A
Explanation: With a carbon tax, the price of emitting a ton of carbon is fixed, but the total amount of emissions reduction is uncertain. With cap-and-trade, the total amount of emissions is fixed (capped), but the price of an emission permit will fluctuate based on market forces.

395. Overfishing of an open-ocean fishery is a classic example of
A. a positive externality.
B. the free-rider problem.
C. a natural monopoly.
D. the tragedy of the commons.
Answer: D
Explanation: The ocean is a common resource (non-excludable but rivalrous). Each fisherman has an incentive to catch as many fish as possible, leading to the collective depletion of the fish stock.

396. The "Sustainable Yield" of a renewable resource like a forest is the
A. maximum amount that can be harvested in a single year.
B. harvest rate that equals the natural regeneration rate.
C. harvest rate that drives the resource to extinction.
D. economic value of the forest's timber.
Answer: B
Explanation: Harvesting at the sustainable yield allows the resource stock to be maintained indefinitely because you are only taking out what nature is putting back in.

397. If the private cost of producing a good is GHS 10 and the external cost is GHS 3, the socially optimal Pigouvian tax would be
A. GHS 13.
B. GHS 10.
C. GHS 7.
D. GHS 3.
Answer: D
Explanation: A Pigouvian tax should be set equal to the value of the negative externality (the external cost) at the socially optimal quantity, in order to force the producer to internalize this cost.

398. When vaccination rates are high, unvaccinated individuals are less likely to get a disease. This is an example of a
A. negative externality in consumption.
B. positive externality in consumption.
C. public good.
D. common resource.
Answer: B
Explanation: The act of an individual getting vaccinated provides an external benefit (reduced risk of infection) to third parties (unvaccinated people), for which they do not pay.

399. Hotelling's Rule is a principle that applies to the management of
A. public goods.
B. renewable resources.
C. non-renewable resources.
D. common property.
Answer: C
Explanation: Hotelling's rule concerns the optimal depletion rate for an exhaustible or non-renewable resource, suggesting its price should rise over time at the rate of interest.

400. In a market for a good with a negative production externality, the market equilibrium output is
A. equal to the socially optimal output.
B. less than the socially optimal output.
C. greater than the socially optimal output.
D. zero.
Answer: C
Explanation: Since producers do not account for the external costs they impose, the private supply curve lies to the right of the social cost curve. This results in the market producing a quantity that is inefficiently large from society's perspective.

Final Comprehensive Questions (Continued)

401. A government policy to provide free primary education is an attempt to address
A. a positive externality and improve human capital.
B. a negative externality and reduce inflation.
C. a monopoly problem and increase competition.
D. a trade deficit and stabilize the currency.
Answer: A
Explanation: Education generates positive externalities (societal benefits beyond the individual). Providing it free is a form of subsidy to increase its consumption and also serves as a long-term development strategy to improve human capital (Ch 16 & 14).

402. In the AD/AS model, a successful EOI strategy leading to higher productivity and more exports would be shown as a
A. leftward shift of AD only.
B. rightward shift of both SRAS and AD.
C. leftward shift of SRAS only.
D. rightward shift of LRAS and AD.
Answer: B
Explanation: An EOI strategy increases exports, which shifts AD right (AD = C+I+G+X-M). Higher productivity also lowers production costs, shifting SRAS right. If sustained, it also shifts LRAS right, but the immediate effect is on SRAS and AD (Ch 9 & 14).

403. A rise in worker remittances to a country will directly improve its
A. balance of trade.
B. financial account balance.
C. current account balance.
D. government budget balance.
Answer: C
Explanation: Remittances are a form of unilateral transfer and are recorded as a credit under "Secondary Income" in the Current Account of the Balance of Payments (Ch 12 & 14).

404. An oligopolistic industry, like petroleum exporting, trying to act like a monopoly is called a ____, and is prone to collapse because of the ____.
A. cartel; prisoner's dilemma.
B. duopoly; law of demand.
C. competitive firm; tragedy of the commons.
D. government agency; multiplier effect.
Answer: A
Explanation: Oligopolists forming a price-fixing agreement create a cartel (Ch 4). These are unstable because each member faces a prisoner's dilemma: cooperating is collectively best, but cheating is individually rational (Ch 4).

405. If a country's GINI coefficient changes from 0.55 to 0.45, it indicates that
A. the economy has grown.
B. poverty has been eliminated.
C. income distribution has become more equal.
D. income distribution has become less equal.
Answer: C
Explanation: A lower GINI coefficient signifies less inequality and a Lorenz curve that has moved closer to the line of perfect equality (Ch 13).

406. Which of the following government policies is LEAST likely to reduce the natural rate of unemployment?
A. Increased funding for job retraining schemes.
B. An increase in aggregate demand through tax cuts.
C. Improved information on job vacancies.
D. Policies to increase labor mobility.
Answer: B
Explanation: The natural rate of unemployment consists of frictional and structural unemployment. Policies to reduce it must be supply-side (improving skills, information, mobility). Increasing aggregate demand addresses cyclical unemployment, not the natural rate (Ch 10).

407. An increase in the capital stock of a nation will cause
A. a movement up along the PPC.
B. a leftward shift of the LRAS curve.
C. a rightward shift of the LRAS curve.
D. a leftward shift of the AD curve.
Answer: C
Explanation: An increase in the stock of capital (a factor of production) increases the economy's potential output, which is represented by a rightward shift of the vertical Long-Run Aggregate Supply curve (Ch 9 & 1).

408. A farmer uses GHS 10 worth of seed (bought from another firm) to grow maize which they sell to a kenkey seller for GHS 50. The kenkey seller uses the maize and sells kenkey to final consumers for GHS 120. The total value added is
A. GHS 180.
B. GHS 170.
C. GHS 120.
D. GHS 110.
Answer: C
Explanation: Value Added = (Value of output - cost of intermediate input). Farmer's VA = 50 - 10 = 40. Kenkey seller's VA = 120 - 50 = 70. Total Value Added = 40 + 70 = GHS 110. Oh wait, the seed firm also has a value added. The farmer bought seed from another firm for GHS 10. Let's assume that firm produced it from zero cost. Firm's VA = 10. Farmer's VA = 50 - 10 = 40. Seller's VA = 120-50 = 70. Total = 10+40+70 = 120. A simpler way: The total value added is always equal to the market value of the final good sold to the consumer, which is GHS 120. Let me correct my intermediate step. Yes, the sum of value added at each stage equals the final good's value. The final good is the kenkey sold for GHS 120. The maize and seed are intermediate goods. So the total contribution to GDP is GHS 120 (Ch 7).

409. If the MPC is 0.6, a tax increase of GHS 20 billion will cause national income to
A. fall by GHS 50 billion.
B. rise by GHS 30 billion.
C. fall by GHS 30 billion.
D. fall by GHS 12 billion.
Answer: C
Explanation: The tax multiplier is -MPC / (1 - MPC) = -0.6 / 0.4 = -1.5. Change in income = Tax Multiplier x Change in Taxes = -1.5 x 20 billion = -30 billion. Alternatively, the tax increase reduces disposable income by 20bn, which reduces initial spending by 20bn x 0.6 = 12bn. The spending multiplier is 1/(1-0.6) = 2.5. So, total income fall = 12bn x 2.5 = 30bn.

410. If the interest rate in Ghana is higher than in the UK, interest rate parity implies that the Ghana Cedi is expected to
A. appreciate against the British Pound.
B. depreciate against the British Pound.
C. be pegged to the British Pound.
D. have no change in value.
Answer: B
Explanation: For the returns on assets to be equal for an international investor, the higher interest gained on the Cedi-denominated asset must be offset by an expected fall (depreciation) in the value of the Cedi itself when it's converted back to Pounds (Ch 12).

411. Which is an example of a "flow" variable?
A. National debt.
B. A country's capital stock.
C. A person's wealth.
D. Annual Gross Domestic Product.
Answer: D
Explanation: A flow variable is measured over a period of time (e.g., per year). GDP, income, and saving are flows. A stock variable is measured at a specific point in time (debt, capital stock, wealth) (Ch 7 & 8).

412. According to the principle of comparative advantage, total world output is greatest when each country
A. produces only what it needs.
B. tries to achieve an absolute advantage in all goods.
C. specializes in producing the good with the lowest opportunity cost.
D. imposes tariffs to protect its industries.
Answer: C
Explanation: This is the core insight of Ricardian trade theory. Global efficiency is maximized when countries specialize based on their relative, not absolute, production efficiencies (Ch 11).

413. If a drought destroys half of a country's cocoa crop, it will likely lead to
A. a rightward shift of the supply curve for cocoa and a fall in price.
B. a leftward shift of the supply curve for cocoa and a rise in price.
C. a rightward shift of the demand curve for cocoa.
D. a movement down along the supply curve for cocoa.
Answer: B
Explanation: The drought is a negative supply shock that reduces the quantity of cocoa available at every price, shifting the supply curve to the left (or upwards) and leading to a higher equilibrium price (Ch 2).

414. An example of 'dissaving' in the Keynesian model is when
A. a person's income is greater than their consumption.
B. a person's consumption is greater than their income.
C. a person puts money in a savings account.
D. the MPC is equal to 1.
Answer: B
Explanation: Dissaving occurs when spending exceeds current income, which is financed by drawing down past savings or by borrowing. This happens at low-income levels in the consumption function, linked to autonomous consumption (Ch 9).

415. The 'brain drain' phenomenon is a potential disadvantage associated with
A. international migration and remittances.
B. foreign direct investment.
C. import substitution.
D. free trade in goods.
Answer: A
Explanation: While remittances are a benefit, the emigration of highly skilled and educated workers (doctors, engineers) from a developing country to a developed one represents a loss of valuable human capital for the home country (Ch 14 & 15).

416. A central bank's "dual mandate," as often practiced by the US Federal Reserve, typically involves targeting both
A. a balanced budget and low taxes.
B. exchange rate stability and low trade deficits.
C. price stability (low inflation) and maximum employment.
D. zero bank failures and high stock prices.
Answer: C
Explanation: Many central banks are tasked with balancing the sometimes-competing goals of keeping inflation low and stable while also promoting a level of economic growth consistent with maximum or full employment (Ch 8 & 10).

417. Which economic system most relies on consumer sovereignty to answer the 'what to produce' question?
A. Command economy
B. Traditional economy
C. Market economy
D. Feudal economy
Answer: C
Explanation: In a market economy, producers are motivated by profit. They produce what consumers are willing and able to buy. Therefore, consumer spending choices ("dollar votes") direct resource allocation (Ch 1 & 5).

418. A major reason for the shape of the long-run average cost (LRAC) curve is
A. the law of diminishing marginal returns.
B. economies and diseconomies of scale.
C. the law of demand.
D. the price of inputs.
Answer: B
Explanation: The downward-sloping part of the U-shaped LRAC is due to economies of scale (increasing returns to scale), while the upward-sloping part is due to diseconomies of scale (decreasing returns to scale) (Ch 6).

419. An increase in the marginal propensity to save (MPS) will
A. increase the value of the expenditure multiplier.
B. decrease the value of the expenditure multiplier.
C. make the consumption function steeper.
D. shift the aggregate demand curve to the right.
Answer: B
Explanation: The multiplier (k) is 1 / MPS. Therefore, a higher MPS (meaning a larger leakage from the spending stream) will result in a smaller multiplier effect (Ch 9).

420. The price elasticity of demand for a good with many close substitutes is likely to be
A. inelastic.
B. perfectly inelastic.
C. unit elastic.
D. elastic.
Answer: D
Explanation: If many substitutes are available, consumers can easily switch to an alternative if the price of one good rises. This makes the quantity demanded highly responsive to price changes, hence, elastic (Ch 2).

421. The value of an economy's total accumulated knowledge, skills, and health is its
A. physical capital.
B. human capital.
C. financial capital.
D. social capital.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of human capital, a key ingredient for long-term economic growth and development (Ch 1 & 14).

422. A perfectly competitive firm is in long-run equilibrium when
A. MR = MC
B. P = minimum ATC
C. P = MC
D. All of the above are true.
Answer: D
Explanation: In long-run equilibrium for a perfectly competitive firm, all these conditions hold simultaneously. P = min ATC ensures normal profit. P=MC ensures allocative efficiency. MR=MC is the profit-maximizing rule which, because P=MR for a competitive firm, means P=MC. All these coincide at a single point (Ch 3).

423. A tariff and a quota on an imported good can both lead to a(n)
A. fall in the domestic price of the good.
B. increase in government revenue.
C. rise in the domestic price of the good.
D. increase in consumer surplus.
Answer: C
Explanation: Both a tariff and an import quota restrict the supply of the imported good on the domestic market, which causes its price to rise above the world price (Ch 11).

424. If an economy's real GDP grows at 3% and its population grows at 2%, its real GDP per capita grows at approximately
A. 5%
B. 1.5%
C. 1%
D. 6%
Answer: C
Explanation: The growth rate of a per capita figure is approximately the growth rate of the numerator minus the growth rate of the denominator. Growth in GDP per capita ≈ Growth in GDP - Growth in Population = 3% - 2% = 1% (Ch 7 & 13).

425. If the central bank buys bonds from commercial banks, the immediate effect is a(n)
A. decrease in commercial bank reserves.
B. increase in commercial bank reserves.
C. increase in the discount rate.
D. increase in the required reserve ratio.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is an open market operation. When the central bank buys bonds, it pays for them by crediting the reserves of the commercial banks, increasing the monetary base and enabling banks to lend more (Ch 8).

426. The shift of labor from low-productivity agriculture to high-productivity manufacturing is a key part of
A. structural transformation.
B. business cycles.
C. the multiplier effect.
D. consumer theory.
Answer: A
Explanation: Structural transformation is the long-term shift in the sectoral composition of an economy's output and employment as it develops, typically from agriculture to industry and then to services (Ch 13).

427. The World Bank's International Development Association (IDA) provides funds primarily to
A. the poorest developing countries.
B. private sector companies.
C. middle-income countries.
D. resolve sovereign debt crises.
Answer: A
Explanation: The IDA is the "soft loan" window of the World Bank, specifically designed to provide grants and highly concessional loans to the world's lowest-income countries (Ch 12).

428. What best explains why a professional footballer earns more than a teacher?
A. A footballer's work is a public good.
B. The demand for top footballers is very high and the supply is very low.
C. Football has more positive externalities than teaching.
D. The footballer's marginal utility is higher.
Answer: B
Explanation: Wages and incomes are determined by the forces of demand and supply in the labor market. The demand for elite sporting talent is extremely high (from clubs, sponsors, fans) while the supply of such talent is extremely limited, driving their earnings up significantly (Ch 2).

429. Which of the following is an example of an investment in the national income accounts?
A. A student paying university tuition fees.
B. A family buying a new car.
C. A firm buying a new piece of machinery.
D. An individual buying government bonds.
Answer: C
Explanation: In GDP accounting, 'Investment' (I) refers to spending on new physical capital (like machinery) and changes in inventories. Buying a car is consumption (C). Buying bonds is a financial transaction, not part of GDP (Ch 7).

430. An increase in income taxes is an example of
A. contractionary fiscal policy.
B. expansionary fiscal policy.
C. contractionary monetary policy.
D. expansionary monetary policy.
Answer: A
Explanation: Increasing taxes reduces disposable income, which dampens consumption and aggregate demand. This is a tool of contractionary fiscal policy, used to combat inflation (Ch 8 & 9).

431. A point on a single indifference curve indicates
A. different levels of utility.
B. a constant level of utility.
C. the consumer's income.
D. the prices of the goods.
Answer: B
Explanation: By definition, all points along a single indifference curve represent combinations of goods that provide the consumer with the exact same level of satisfaction or utility (Ch 5).

432. Which statement best describes a production function?
A. It shows the relationship between total cost and output.
B. It shows the technical relationship between inputs and maximum output.
C. It shows the profitability of a firm.
D. It shows the relationship between revenue and output.
Answer: B
Explanation: A production function is a purely technical concept that describes how a given quantity of inputs (labor, capital) can be transformed into a certain maximum quantity of output, given the state of technology (Ch 6).

433. The theory of comparative advantage suggests that trade is mutually beneficial
A. only between countries of similar size.
B. only if there is no government intervention.
C. even if one country has an absolute advantage in all goods.
D. only if it results in a trade surplus for both countries.
Answer: C
Explanation: This is the most powerful insight from Ricardo's theory. As long as opportunity costs differ, both countries can gain from specializing and trading, regardless of absolute productivity levels (Ch 11).

434. In the AD/AS model, a decrease in consumer and business confidence will, in the short run, lead to
A. higher inflation and lower unemployment.
B. a fall in the price level and a fall in real GDP.
C. a rise in the price level and a rise in real GDP.
D. no change in the price level but a fall in real GDP.
Answer: B
Explanation: A drop in confidence reduces both consumption (C) and investment (I), causing the Aggregate Demand (AD) curve to shift to the left. This results in a new short-run equilibrium at a lower price level and lower output (real GDP) (Ch 9).

435. In an oligopoly, 'collusion' is an attempt to
A. increase market competition.
B. reduce joint profits.
C. reduce barriers to entry.
D. coordinate actions to act like a single monopolist.
Answer: D
Explanation: Collusion is when rival firms cooperate instead of compete. The goal is typically to restrict output and raise prices to the monopoly level in order to maximize their collective profits (Ch 4).

436. The supply of agricultural products like tomatoes is likely to be
A. perfectly elastic in the short run.
B. price inelastic in the short run.
C. perfectly inelastic in the long run.
D. unit elastic at all times.
Answer: B
Explanation: In the short run, it is difficult to quickly increase the supply of an agricultural product in response to a price rise, as it depends on growing seasons. This makes supply price inelastic (Ch 2).

437. What is a key similarity between monopoly and monopolistic competition?
A. Both face a perfectly elastic demand curve.
B. Both face a downward-sloping demand curve.
C. Both earn supernormal profits in the long run.
D. Both have significant barriers to entry.
Answer: B
Explanation: Because both monopolists and monopolistically competitive firms sell products that are unique (in the monopolist's case) or differentiated (in the monopolistic competitor's case), they both face a downward-sloping demand curve and have some control over price (Ch 3 & 4).

438. Which of the following would NOT cause a shift in the supply curve for beef?
A. A rise in the price of cattle feed.
B. A successful advertising campaign for beef.
C. A new technology that reduces the cost of raising cattle.
D. An increase in the price of leather (a joint product).
Answer: B
Explanation: An advertising campaign affects consumers' tastes and would shift the demand curve for beef. The other options all affect the cost or profitability of producing beef from the supplier's perspective (Ch 2).

439. An economic system that mainly addresses the fundamental economic problems by relying on the 'invisible hand' of the market is called
A. socialism.
B. a market economy (capitalism).
C. a command economy.
D. a traditional economy.
Answer: B
Explanation: Adam Smith's concept of the "invisible hand" describes how the pursuit of self-interest by individuals and firms in a free market can lead to an efficient allocation of resources for society as a whole (Ch 1).

440. Which factor of production is defined as any human effort, mental or physical, directed towards production?
A. Land
B. Labour
C. Capital
D. Enterprise
Answer: B
Explanation: This is the standard economic definition of labor, encompassing the work of a doctor, a teacher, a factory worker, or a programmer (Ch 1).

441. In calculating the GDP of Ghana, the value of cars produced by a German-owned company (e.g., VW) in Accra WOULD be included in
A. Ghana's GNP and Germany's GDP.
B. Ghana's GDP and Germany's GNP.
C. only Ghana's GNP.
D. only Germany's GDP.
Answer: B
Explanation: It's included in Ghana's GDP because it was produced within Ghana's borders. The profits sent back to Germany would be part of Germany's GNP because it's income earned by German nationals (Ch 7).

442. The main goal of a 'carbon tax' policy is to
A. increase government revenue.
B. make polluting firms internalize the social cost of their emissions.
C. subsidize renewable energy.
D. ban the use of fossil fuels completely.
Answer: B
Explanation: A carbon tax is a Pigouvian tax designed to correct the negative externality of pollution. By putting a price on carbon emissions, it forces firms to account for the environmental damage their activities cause, creating an incentive to pollute less (Ch 16).

443. Which of the following situations describes structural unemployment?
A. A tour guide is jobless during the rainy season.
B. A construction worker is laid off because the economy is in a deep recession.
C. A recent graduate is searching for their first professional job.
D. A skilled coal miner loses their job as the country switches to renewable energy.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is a classic case of structural unemployment. The worker's skills are no longer in demand due to a long-term change in the structure of the economy (a shift away from coal) (Ch 10).

444. If the consumer price index (CPI) was 120 in Year 1 and 126 in Year 2, the inflation rate was
A. 6%
B. 5%
C. 4.76%
D. 1.05%
Answer: B
Explanation: Inflation Rate = [(CPI_Y2 - CPI_Y1) / CPI_Y1] x 100 = [(126 - 120) / 120] x 100 = [6 / 120] x 100 = 0.05 x 100 = 5% (Ch 7).

445. If the Bank of Ghana wishes to reduce the money supply, it can
A. decrease the repo rate.
B. sell foreign exchange.
C. buy government bonds.
D. lower the reserve requirement for banks.
Answer: B
Explanation: Selling foreign exchange (e.g., USD) means commercial banks pay for it with Cedis, thus reducing the Cedi liquidity (reserves) in the banking system. Buying bonds, lowering the repo rate, or lowering the reserve requirement are all expansionary policies (Ch 8).

446. When a consumer's income rises, their demand for an inferior good will
A. increase.
B. decrease.
C. remain unchanged.
D. become more elastic.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of an inferior good. As consumers become richer, they switch from the inferior good to higher-quality substitutes (Ch 2).

447. The short-run shut-down condition for a firm is to cease production if
A. P < MC
B. TR < TC
C. P < AFC
D. P < AVC
Answer: D
Explanation: A firm should shut down in the short run if the price it receives per unit is not enough to even cover its variable costs per unit. By shutting down, it limits its loss to its fixed costs (Ch 3 & 6).

448. An example of a trade bloc moving towards an economic union is the
A. World Trade Organization (WTO).
B. European Union (EU).
C. United Nations (UN).
D. International Monetary Fund (IMF).
Answer: B
Explanation: The EU is the most integrated trade bloc, having features of a common market, a common currency (the Eurozone), and significant harmonization of economic policies, all characteristics of an economic union (Ch 11).

449. Which concept best explains why a student might choose to sleep instead of attending an early morning lecture?
A. The law of demand.
B. Opportunity cost.
C. The law of diminishing returns.
D. A budget deficit.
Answer: B
Explanation: The opportunity cost of attending the lecture is the value of the next best alternative given up, which in this case is the benefit or satisfaction from getting more sleep (Ch 1).

450. Which is NOT a component of a country's Human Development Index (HDI)?
A. Life expectancy at birth.
B. The GINI coefficient.
C. Mean years of schooling.
D. Gross National Income (GNI) per capita.
Answer: B
Explanation: The HDI has three dimensions: health (life expectancy), education (schooling years), and standard of living (GNI per capita). The GINI coefficient measures inequality, which is a separate (though related) aspect of development (Ch 7 & 13).

Of course. Here is the third set of 250 WASSCE-standard Multiple Choice Questions, with answers and explanations, continuing from the previous sets and based on your provided textbook content.

Chapter 1: Introduction to Economics and Basic Concepts

451. Economics is called a social science because it studies
A. the physical laws of nature.
B. how societies are formed.
C. human behavior and decision-making.
D. the history of political systems.
Answer: C
Explanation: Like other social sciences, economics is concerned with the actions and interactions of people—specifically, how they make choices to deal with scarcity.

452. The factor of production that combines all other factors and bears the risk of production is
A. Land.
B. Labour.
C. Capital.
D. Enterprise.
Answer: D
Explanation: Enterprise (or entrepreneurship) is the human skill of organizing land, labor, and capital, innovating, and taking on the uncertainties of a business venture.

453. In a market economy, the "What to produce?" question is primarily determined by
A. government directive.
B. consumer demand.
C. the country's resource endowment.
D. traditional methods.
Answer: B
Explanation: In a market system, firms produce goods and services that consumers are willing and able to buy, as this is how they make a profit. This is the essence of consumer sovereignty.

454. Which of the following is an example of 'Land' in the economic sense?
A. A tractor on a farm.
B. An irrigation canal built by a farmer.
C. Mineral deposits of bauxite.
D. A degree in agricultural science.
Answer: C
Explanation: "Land" refers to all natural resources. Mineral deposits are a gift of nature. A tractor and canal are capital, and a degree is human capital (an attribute of labor).

455. The heterogeneity of labour refers to the fact that
A. all workers earn the same wage.
B. labor cannot be stored.
C. workers differ in skill, education, and productivity.
D. labor can move between geographical locations.
Answer: C
Explanation: "Heterogeneous" means non-uniform. The labor force is diverse, with workers possessing different levels of skill, training, and efficiency.

456. An economic system where resources are owned and controlled by the state is called
A. a market economy.
B. a traditional economy.
C. a command economy.
D. a laissez-faire economy.
Answer: C
Explanation: A key feature of a command (or planned) economy is state ownership and central control over the means of production.

457. A straight-line Production Possibility Curve (PPC) illustrates
A. increasing opportunity cost.
B. decreasing opportunity cost.
C. constant opportunity cost.
D. zero opportunity cost.
Answer: C
Explanation: A straight-line PPC means that the rate of trade-off between the two goods is constant. This would happen if resources were perfectly substitutable between the production of both goods, which is unrealistic.

458. The depreciation of capital goods refers to the
A. increase in their value over time.
B. decrease in their value due to wear and tear or obsolescence.
C. cost of borrowing money to buy them.
D. wages paid to operate them.
Answer: B
Explanation: Depreciation is the consumption or wearing out of capital goods during the production process, reducing their value.

459. The study of how a household decides to allocate its income between food and clothing is a subject of
A. Macroeconomics.
B. Development Economics.
C. Monetary Economics.
D. Microeconomics.
Answer: D
Explanation: Microeconomics studies the decisions of individual economic units, such as a single household or a single firm.

460. Which of these is a positive economic statement?
A. The government should reduce the tax on petrol.
B. The unemployment rate is too high.
C. A rise in the minimum wage will lead to an increase in unemployment among young workers.
D. The distribution of income in Ghana is unfair.
Answer: C
Explanation: A positive statement is a testable, factual statement about what is, was, or will be. It can be verified or falsified with data. The other statements are normative (value judgments).

Chapter 2: Demand, Supply, and Market Equilibrium

461. The responsiveness of quantity supplied to a change in price is measured by the
A. price elasticity of demand.
B. income elasticity of demand.
C. price elasticity of supply.
D. cross-price elasticity of demand.
Answer: C
Explanation: This is the definition of Price Elasticity of Supply (PES), which measures how much the quantity supplied changes in percentage terms for a given percentage change in price.

462. If an increase in the price of sugar leads to an increase in the supply of sugar, this is called
A. an increase in supply.
B. a decrease in supply.
C. a change in quantity supplied.
D. an exceptional supply.
Answer: C
Explanation: This describes a movement along the existing supply curve, which is known as a change (or extension) in quantity supplied, caused by a change in the good's own price.

463. Which of the following will cause the supply curve of rice to shift to the left?
A. A new high-yield variety of rice seed.
B. A fall in the price of fertilizer.
C. A government subsidy for rice farmers.
D. A widespread disease affecting the rice crop.
Answer: D
Explanation: A crop disease is a negative natural shock that will reduce the amount of rice that can be produced and supplied at any given price, shifting the supply curve to the left.

464. If a government-imposed tax on a good is shared equally between buyers and sellers, it implies that
A. demand is more elastic than supply.
B. supply is more elastic than demand.
C. the elasticities of demand and supply are equal.
D. demand is perfectly inelastic.
Answer: C
Explanation: The incidence (burden) of a tax depends on the relative elasticities. If the burden is shared equally, it means the responsiveness of buyers and sellers to the price change is the same.

465. Two goods are said to be in joint demand when
A. they are produced together.
B. they are strong substitutes for each other.
C. they are both demanded by the government.
D. they are consumed together.
Answer: D
Explanation: Joint demand is another term for complementary demand. It refers to goods that are used in conjunction with each other, like a printer and an ink cartridge.

466. If the demand for a good is perfectly elastic, its demand curve is
A. horizontal.
B. vertical.
C. downward sloping.
D. upward sloping.
Answer: A
Explanation: Perfectly elastic demand (PED = ∞) means consumers are willing to buy an infinite amount at a specific price, but none at a higher price. This is represented by a horizontal demand curve.

467. At any price above the equilibrium price, there will be
A. excess demand.
B. excess supply.
C. market clearing.
D. an increase in demand.
Answer: B
Explanation: If the price is above equilibrium, sellers are willing to supply more than buyers are willing to purchase, creating a surplus or excess supply.

468. For which of the following goods is demand likely to be the most inelastic?
A. A specific brand of rice.
B. A luxury sports car.
C. An essential life-saving drug.
D. A foreign holiday package.
Answer: C
Explanation: A life-saving drug is a necessity with few or no close substitutes, so its demand will be very unresponsive to price changes (highly inelastic).

469. A market in disequilibrium will eventually return to equilibrium through the action of
A. the government.
B. price adjustments.
C. consumer groups.
D. foreign trade.
Answer: B
Explanation: The market mechanism relies on price flexibility. A surplus will cause prices to fall, and a shortage will cause prices to rise, automatically moving the market back towards equilibrium.

470. A decrease in consumers' income will cause the equilibrium price of a normal good to
A. rise and quantity to fall.
B. fall and quantity to rise.
C. fall and quantity to fall.
D. rise and quantity to rise.
Answer: C
Explanation: A decrease in income shifts the demand curve for a normal good to the left. With an upward-sloping supply curve, the new intersection point will be at a lower price and a lower quantity.

Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly

471. In a perfectly competitive industry, long-run equilibrium is allocatively and productively efficient because
A. P = MC and P = min ATC.
B. firms can price discriminate.
C. barriers to entry protect firms.
D. the government regulates the price.
Answer: A
Explanation: Allocative efficiency occurs where P=MC. Productive efficiency occurs where firms produce at their minimum average total cost. Both conditions are met in the long-run equilibrium of perfect competition.

472. Which market structure faces no competition at all?
A. Perfect competition
B. Monopolistic competition
C. Oligopoly
D. Pure monopoly
Answer: D
Explanation: A pure monopoly, by definition, is the single seller of a product with no close substitutes, meaning it has no direct competitors in its market.

473. A key reason why a monopolist's marginal revenue is less than its price is that
A. it is a price taker.
B. to sell more, it must lower the price on all units.
C. its costs are always falling.
D. its demand curve is horizontal.
Answer: B
Explanation: The monopolist faces the market demand curve. When it lowers the price to sell an additional unit, it loses some revenue on all the previous units it could have sold at a higher price. This makes the MR less than the price.

474. For a perfectly competitive firm to be in long-run equilibrium,
A. it must be making supernormal profits.
B. it must be making a loss.
C. other firms must have an incentive to enter the market.
D. there must be no incentive for firms to enter or leave the market.
Answer: D
Explanation: Long-run equilibrium is a stable state. This can only happen when existing firms are earning just a normal profit, providing no incentive for new firms to enter (which would lower prices) or existing firms to leave (which would raise prices).

475. A regulated natural monopoly is often required by the government to set its price equal to
A. Marginal Revenue.
B. Marginal Cost or Average Cost.
C. the highest price consumers will pay.
D. the world price.
Answer: B
Explanation: To prevent a natural monopoly from exploiting its market power, regulators may impose price controls. Setting P=AC (average cost pricing) allows the firm to break even (earn a normal profit), while setting P=MC (marginal cost pricing) achieves allocative efficiency but might require a subsidy if MC is below AC.

476. The shutdown point for any firm in the short run occurs where
A. Price = minimum Average Total Cost.
B. Price = minimum Average Variable Cost.
C. Total Revenue = Total Cost.
D. Marginal Revenue = Marginal Cost.
Answer: B
Explanation: The shutdown point is the level of price and output where the firm is just covering its variable costs. Below this price, it is better to shut down and only incur fixed costs.

477. In the model of perfect competition, it is assumed that firms aim to
A. maximize sales revenue.
B. maximize market share.
C. maximize profits.
D. minimize total costs.
Answer: C
Explanation: Profit maximization (producing where MR=MC) is the standard behavioral assumption for firms in most microeconomic models, including perfect competition.

478. Which statement is true for a monopoly but NOT for a perfectly competitive firm?
A. It produces where MR=MC.
B. It seeks to maximize profit.
C. It can earn supernormal profits in the long run.
D. It has a marginal cost curve.
Answer: C
Explanation: Significant barriers to entry protect a monopolist from new competitors, allowing it to sustain supernormal profits in the long run. In perfect competition, free entry competes away such profits.

479. The term "price taker" means that a firm
A. takes whatever price its competitors are charging.
B. has no control over the market price of its product.
C. can set any price it wishes.
D. takes instructions on pricing from the government.
Answer: B
Explanation: A price taker, like a firm in perfect competition, is so small relative to the market that it must accept the prevailing market price as given.

480. An industry with a large number of firms and a standardized product is
A. a monopoly.
B. monopolistic competition.
C. perfect competition.
D. an oligopoly.
Answer: C
Explanation: A large number of firms and a homogeneous/standardized product are two of the key defining characteristics of a perfectly competitive market.

Chapter 4: Monopolistic Competition and Oligopoly

481. An industry characterized by a few large firms, where each firm considers the reactions of its rivals, is an
A. Oligopoly.
B. Monopoly.
C. Monopolistic Competition.
D. Perfect Competition.
Answer: A
Explanation: The defining feature of an oligopoly is the strategic interdependence among the few firms that dominate the market.

482. Restaurants, hair salons, and clothing stores are common examples of firms in
A. perfect competition.
B. monopoly.
C. monopolistic competition.
D. an oligopoly.
Answer: C
Explanation: These industries have many firms (easy entry) but each offers a differentiated product or service (different cuisine, style, brand, location), which are the key features of monopolistic competition.

483. 'Excess capacity' in monopolistic competition means that in the long run, firms
A. produce at an output level greater than the one that minimizes ATC.
B. have a lot of unsold stock.
C. produce at an output level less than the one that minimizes ATC.
D. can increase output without any change in total cost.
Answer: C
Explanation: Because of the downward-sloping demand curve, the long-run equilibrium (where P=ATC) occurs on the falling portion of the ATC curve, meaning firms could lower their average cost if they were to produce more.

484. The success of a cartel is threatened by
A. the incentive of its members to cheat.
B. high barriers to entry.
C. the inelasticity of market demand.
D. economies of scale.
Answer: A
Explanation: The fundamental problem for any cartel is that while collusion maximizes joint profits, each individual member has a strong incentive to secretly break the agreement (e.g., sell more than their quota) to increase their own individual profit.

485. The Bertrand model of oligopoly focuses on competition through
A. output.
B. advertising.
C. price.
D. innovation.
Answer: C
Explanation: The Bertrand model assumes firms compete by setting prices, with each firm trying to undercut its rivals.

486. When a dominant firm in an oligopoly raises its price and the other firms follow, this is an example of
A. a cartel.
B. predatory pricing.
C. a price war.
D. price leadership.
Answer: D
Explanation: Price leadership is a form of tacit (informal) collusion where one firm acts as the price setter for the industry and other firms follow suit, avoiding direct price competition.

487. A major difference between monopoly and monopolistic competition is the
A. shape of the demand curve.
B. rule for profit maximization.
C. significance of barriers to entry.
D. goal of the firm.
Answer: C
Explanation: A monopoly is protected by significant, high barriers to entry, which allows for long-run supernormal profits. Monopolistic competition has low barriers to entry, which leads to normal profits in the long run.

488. Which of these provides a framework for analyzing strategic interactions between firms?
A. Cost-Benefit Analysis
B. The Production Possibility Curve
C. Indifference Curve Analysis
D. Game Theory
Answer: D
Explanation: Game theory is specifically designed to model and analyze situations where the outcome for one "player" (e.g., a firm) depends on the strategy chosen by other players.

489. Which market structure provides the widest variety of choices for consumers within a single product group?
A. Perfect competition
B. Monopoly
C. Monopolistic competition
D. Command economy
Answer: C
Explanation: The product differentiation inherent in monopolistic competition means that consumers are offered a wide range of varieties, brands, styles, and quality levels for a product like soap or restaurants.

490. If the firms in an oligopolistic industry successfully collude and form a cartel, the market outcome in terms of price and quantity will most closely resemble that of a
A. perfectly competitive market.
B. monopoly.
C. monopolistically competitive market.
D. foreign market.
Answer: B
Explanation: The entire point of a cartel is for the group of firms to act as if they were a single monopolist, restricting output and raising the price to the monopoly level to maximize their joint profits.

Chapter 5: Consumer Theory

491. An indifference map consists of
A. a single indifference curve and a budget line.
B. a set of several indifference curves for a consumer.
C. a consumer's demand and supply curves.
D. several different budget lines for a consumer.
Answer: B
Explanation: An indifference map is a collection of a consumer's indifference curves, with each curve representing a different level of utility. It provides a complete picture of the consumer's preferences.

492. If the price of Good X falls, a consumer's real income
A. increases.
B. decreases.
C. remains unchanged.
D. becomes irrelevant.
Answer: A
Explanation: A fall in the price of any good the consumer buys means their existing money income can now purchase more goods and services than before. This is an increase in their real income or purchasing power.

493. The substitution effect refers to the change in consumption of a good due to a change in
A. the consumer's income.
B. its relative price, holding utility constant.
C. the consumer's tastes.
D. its quality.
Answer: B
Explanation: The substitution effect isolates the impact of a change in the relative attractiveness of a good when its price changes, separate from the effect of the change in real income.

494. If the MUx/Px < MUy/Py, a utility-maximizing consumer should
A. buy more of Good X and less of Good Y.
B. buy more of Good Y and less of Good X.
C. buy more of both goods.
D. not change their consumption.
Answer: B
Explanation: The marginal utility per dollar spent on Good Y is greater than that for Good X. The consumer can increase their total utility by reallocating spending from X to Y.

495. For a Giffen good, the demand curve is
A. vertical.
B. horizontal.
C. downward sloping.
D. upward sloping.
Answer: D
Explanation: A Giffen good is a rare case of a good that violates the law of demand. As its price falls, quantity demanded also falls, resulting in an upward-sloping demand curve.

496. The horizontal intercept of a budget line represents
A. the consumer's total income.
B. the price of the good on the horizontal axis.
C. the maximum quantity of the good on the vertical axis that can be bought.
D. the maximum quantity of the good on the horizontal axis that can be bought.
Answer: D
Explanation: The horizontal intercept is found by assuming the consumer spends all their income (M) on the good on the horizontal axis (Good X). The quantity would be M / Px.

497. Two indifference curves for perfect complements (e.g., left shoes and right shoes) are
A. straight lines.
B. concave to the origin.
C. L-shaped.
D. upward sloping.
Answer: C
Explanation: Perfect complements must be consumed in a fixed ratio. Having an extra left shoe with no extra right shoe adds zero utility. This results in right-angled or L-shaped indifference curves.

498. The point of tangency between a budget line and an indifference curve indicates
A. the break-even point for a firm.
B. consumer equilibrium.
C. the shutdown point for a firm.
D. a recessionary gap.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is the graphical representation of consumer equilibrium, the point where the consumer is maximizing their satisfaction subject to their budget constraint.

499. Cardinal utility is not a favored approach in modern economics because
A. it is too simple to understand.
B. satisfaction is subjective and cannot be measured numerically.
C. it does not lead to a downward-sloping demand curve.
D. it was developed by a non-economist.
Answer: B
Explanation: The main criticism of the cardinal approach is its unrealistic assumption that utility can be measured in absolute units. The ordinal approach is considered more realistic.

500. A movement from a lower indifference curve to a higher one represents
A. a decrease in a consumer's real income.
B. an increase in a consumer's satisfaction.
C. a change in relative prices.
D. a fall in total utility.
Answer: B
Explanation: Higher indifference curves represent bundles containing more goods, and due to the non-satiation assumption ("more is better"), they signify a higher level of total utility or satisfaction.

Chapter 6: Producer Theory

501. The marginal product of labor is the
A. total output divided by the number of workers.
B. additional output from hiring one more worker.
C. total output of the firm.
D. wage rate paid to the worker.
Answer: B
Explanation: Marginal product measures the change in total output resulting from a one-unit increase in a variable input, in this case, labor.

502. The relationship between short-run production (marginal product) and short-run costs (marginal cost) is
A. direct.
B. inverse.
C. non-existent.
D. constant.
Answer: B
Explanation: When marginal product is rising, marginal cost is falling, and when marginal product is falling (diminishing returns), marginal cost is rising.

503. The vertical distance between the Average Total Cost (ATC) curve and the Average Variable Cost (AVC) curve represents
A. Marginal Cost (MC).
B. Total Fixed Cost (TFC).
C. Average Fixed Cost (AFC).
D. Economies of scale.
Answer: C
Explanation: Since ATC = AVC + AFC, the difference between the ATC and AVC curves at any given output level is equal to the Average Fixed Cost. This distance gets smaller as output increases.

504. The long run is a period in which
A. all factors of production are fixed.
B. technology is fixed.
C. at least one factor is fixed.
D. all factors of production are variable.
Answer: D
Explanation: The long run is defined as a time horizon long enough for a firm to be able to change all its inputs, including its scale of operation (plant size).

505. Spreading advertising costs over a larger output is an example of which type of economy of scale?
A. Technical
B. Managerial
C. Financial
D. Marketing
Answer: D
Explanation: Marketing economies of scale arise from efficiencies in the marketing process as a firm grows, including bulk advertising and distribution.

506. Total cost is equal to
A. Total Fixed Cost minus Total Variable Cost.
B. Total Revenue minus Total Profit.
C. Average Total Cost multiplied by Quantity.
D. Marginal Cost plus Average Cost.
Answer: C
Explanation: Since ATC = TC / Q, rearranging the formula gives TC = ATC x Q. Also, TC = TFC + TVC.

507. A firm earns normal profit when
A. Price = Marginal Cost.
B. Total Revenue > Total Cost.
C. Economic Profit is zero.
D. Accounting Profit is zero.
Answer: C
Explanation: Normal profit occurs when total revenue is just enough to cover all economic costs (explicit + implicit). At this point, economic profit is zero, though accounting profit may be positive.

508. A production function illustrates the relationship between
A. cost and revenue.
B. inputs and output.
C. profit and sales.
D. price and quantity.
Answer: B
Explanation: A production function is a technical relationship that shows the maximum output that can be obtained from a given set of inputs.

509. In the Cobb-Douglas production function Q = A * L^α * K^β, the parameter 'A' represents
A. total output.
B. the amount of capital.
C. total factor productivity or technology.
D. the output elasticity of labor.
Answer: C
Explanation: 'A' is a shift parameter that captures the overall efficiency of the production process, often interpreted as the level of technology.

510. At the producer's equilibrium (least-cost combination), the slope of the isoquant is
A. greater than the slope of the isocost line.
B. less than the slope of the isocost line.
C. equal to the slope of the isocost line.
D. zero.
Answer: C
Explanation: The equilibrium occurs at the point of tangency between the isoquant and the isocost line, where their slopes are equal. This means MRTS = PL/PK.

Chapter 7: National Income Accounting

511. The market value of all final goods and services produced by the nationals of a country, regardless of location, is the
A. Gross Domestic Product (GDP).
B. Gross National Product (GNP).
C. Net Domestic Product (NDP).
D. Personal Income (PI).
Answer: B
Explanation: GNP focuses on the income earned by a country's nationals (its factors of production), whether they are located at home or abroad.

512. The term "double counting" in national income accounting refers to
A. counting the value of final and intermediate goods.
B. counting both GDP and GNP.
C. counting the income of both husband and wife.
D. including transfer payments in GDP.
Answer: A
Explanation: Double counting occurs if the value of intermediate goods (e.g., flour used to make bread) is counted in addition to the value of the final good (bread), which already includes the value of the flour.

513. If the GDP deflator rises from 150 to 165 in a year, the inflation rate is
A. 15%
B. 10%
C. 5%
D. 1.1%
Answer: B
Explanation: Inflation Rate = [(New Deflator - Old Deflator) / Old Deflator] x 100 = [(165 - 150) / 150] x 100 = [15 / 150] x 100 = 0.1 x 100 = 10%.

514. The income that households actually receive before paying personal taxes is called
A. National Income (NI).
B. Net National Product (NNP).
C. Disposable Income (DI).
D. Personal Income (PI).
Answer: D
Explanation: Personal Income measures the income flowing to households. It is derived from NI by subtracting incomes earned but not received (like corporate taxes) and adding incomes received but not earned (like transfer payments).

515. The "underground economy" refers to
A. mining and quarrying activities.
B. illegal or unreported economic activities.
C. business conducted online.
D. transactions between a parent company and its subsidiary.
Answer: B
Explanation: The underground or shadow economy consists of economic transactions that are concealed from official statistics, either because the activity is illegal or to evade taxes. Its existence means official GDP understates true economic activity.

Chapter 8: Money, Banking, and Fiscal Policy

516. "Liquidity" refers to the
A. profitability of an asset.
B. riskiness of an asset.
C. ease and speed with which an asset can be converted into a medium of exchange.
D. total supply of an asset.
Answer: C
Explanation: An asset's liquidity is a measure of how quickly it can be turned into cash (the most liquid asset) without a significant loss in its value.

517. Which of these is a function of a commercial bank?
A. Printing currency notes.
B. Acting as a lender of last resort.
C. Implementing monetary policy.
D. Accepting deposits and providing loans.
Answer: D
Explanation: These are the core functions of commercial banks, which serve as financial intermediaries between savers (depositors) and borrowers. The other options are functions of the central bank.

518. If the central bank increases the cash reserve ratio (CRR), commercial banks' ability to create credit will
A. increase.
B. decrease.
C. remain unchanged.
D. become unlimited.
Answer: B
Explanation: A higher reserve ratio forces banks to hold a larger fraction of their deposits as reserves, leaving them with smaller excess reserves to lend out. This reduces the credit multiplier and contracts the money supply.

519. A major source of government revenue is
A. subsidies.
B. recurrent expenditure.
C. public debt.
D. taxation.
Answer: D
Explanation: Taxes, both direct and indirect, are the primary and most significant source of revenue for governments to finance their expenditures.

520. Expansionary fiscal policy would be appropriate when the economy is experiencing
A. high inflation.
B. a recessionary gap.
C. a trade surplus.
D. rapid economic growth.
Answer: B
Explanation: During a recession (when output is below potential), expansionary fiscal policy (increased G, decreased T) is used to boost aggregate demand and stimulate economic activity.

521. A proportional tax is also known as a(n)
A. progressive tax.
B. regressive tax.
C. flat tax.
D. ad valorem tax.
Answer: C
Explanation: A proportional or flat tax system applies the same tax rate to all taxpayers regardless of their income level.

522. When a government's total expenditure exceeds its total non-borrowed revenue, the difference is the
A. Primary deficit.
B. Revenue deficit.
C. Fiscal deficit.
D. Public debt.
Answer: C
Explanation: The fiscal deficit represents the total borrowing requirement of the government in a given year.

523. Which function of money is most negatively affected by high inflation?
A. Medium of exchange
B. Unit of account
C. Store of value
D. Standard of deferred payment
Answer: C
Explanation: Inflation erodes the purchasing power of money over time. When inflation is high, money becomes a poor store of value as its real worth decreases rapidly.

524. One of the main reasons for the existence of NPAs in the banking sector is
A. low interest rates.
B. poor credit appraisal and monitoring by banks.
C. excessive government regulation.
D. a high rate of household savings.
Answer: B
Explanation: If banks do not properly assess the creditworthiness of borrowers or fail to monitor loans effectively, it increases the likelihood that borrowers will default, leading to non-performing assets.

525. In most countries, fiscal policy is formulated by the
A. Central Bank.
B. Ministry of Finance / Treasury.
C. Judiciary.
D. private sector.
Answer: B
Explanation: The government, typically through its finance ministry, is responsible for preparing the budget and deciding on taxation and spending policies that constitute fiscal policy.

Chapter 9 to 16 (More Advanced and Comprehensive Questions)

526. The quantity theory of money (MV=PY), central to Classical economics, implies that if V and Y are stable, an increase in the money supply (M) leads to a proportional increase in
A. real output (Y).
B. the velocity of money (V).
C. the price level (P).
D. employment.
Answer: C
Explanation: This formula underpins the classical idea of money neutrality. With velocity (V) and real output (Y) assumed to be constant or stable in the long run, changes in the money supply translate directly into changes in the overall price level.

527. What distinguishes Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) from Foreign Portfolio Investment (FPI)?
A. FDI involves buying shares, while FPI involves building factories.
B. FDI implies a long-term interest and a degree of control, while FPI is more passive.
C. FDI is only undertaken by governments, while FPI is by private individuals.
D. FDI is recorded in the current account, while FPI is in the capital account.
Answer: B
Explanation: The key distinction is the level of control and intent. FDI aims to establish a lasting influence in a foreign enterprise, while FPI is a more short-term, passive investment in securities like stocks and bonds.

528. In the Keynesian cross model (expenditure-output diagram), equilibrium occurs where
A. the aggregate expenditure line crosses the 45-degree line.
B. consumption equals savings.
C. government spending equals taxation.
D. investment is at its maximum.
Answer: A
Explanation: The 45-degree line represents all points where aggregate output (Y) equals aggregate expenditure (AE). The equilibrium level of income and output is where the planned AE line intersects this line.

529. The main argument for 'carbon pricing' (either a tax or cap-and-trade) is to
A. create a public good.
B. address the problem of a positive externality.
C. make polluters internalize the negative externality of their emissions.
D. ensure the 'tragedy of the commons' is not solved.
Answer: C
Explanation: Carbon pricing works by putting a cost on GHG emissions, which is a negative externality. This forces firms and consumers to account for the social cost of their actions and provides an incentive to reduce emissions.

530. A country facing a balance of payments crisis might seek short-term financial assistance from the
A. World Bank (IBRD).
B. International Monetary Fund (IMF).
C. World Trade Organization (WTO).
D. United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).
Answer: B
Explanation: Providing temporary financial assistance to countries with balance of payments difficulties is the core lending function of the IMF. The World Bank focuses on long-term development.

531. According to the Expectations-Augmented Phillips Curve, an attempt to keep unemployment below the natural rate will lead to
A. accelerating inflation.
B. stable inflation.
C. falling inflation (disinflation).
D. a fall in the natural rate of unemployment.
Answer: A
Explanation: Once inflation expectations adjust upwards, policymakers must create even more inflation to get the same temporary reduction in unemployment, leading to a spiral of ever-accelerating price increases.

532. The 'J-curve' effect suggests that after a currency depreciation, a country's trade balance will
A. immediately improve.
B. immediately worsen before eventually improving.
C. immediately improve before eventually worsening.
D. remain unchanged.
Answer: B
Explanation: The J-curve effect occurs because in the short run, import and export volumes are inelastic. A depreciation immediately raises the cost of imports without a significant immediate increase in export volumes, initially worsening the trade balance. Over time, as volumes adjust, the balance improves.

533. Which of the following development strategies is most closely associated with the principle of comparative advantage?
A. Import Substitution Industrialization
B. Export-Oriented Industrialization
C. Microfinance
D. Community-Driven Development
Answer: B
Explanation: An EOI strategy explicitly involves a country specializing in and exporting goods in which it has a comparative advantage to sell on the world market.

534. A government budget surplus
A. increases public debt.
B. can be used to pay down public debt.
C. is an example of expansionary fiscal policy.
D. means the government has borrowed from the central bank.
Answer: B
Explanation: When government revenue exceeds expenditure, the resulting surplus provides funds that can be used to repay previously accumulated government debt.

535. The "crowding-out" hypothesis suggests that
A. expansionary monetary policy is ineffective.
B. import substitution is superior to export orientation.
C. budget deficits financed by borrowing can lead to higher interest rates and lower private investment.
D. cartels are inherently stable.
Answer: C
Explanation: This is a major critique of expansionary fiscal policy, arguing that the rise in government spending can be offset by a fall in private investment due to the upward pressure on interest rates from government borrowing.

536. If a nation's currency appreciates, its terms of trade will likely
A. deteriorate.
B. improve.
C. remain unchanged.
D. become negative.
Answer: B
Explanation: Appreciation means the currency is stronger. It can now buy more foreign goods (lower import prices in domestic currency terms) for the same amount of domestic goods (stable or higher export prices in foreign currency terms). This improves the ratio of export prices to import prices.

537. Which market structure is characterized by firms having downward-sloping demand curves but earning only normal profits in the long run?
A. Perfect Competition
B. Monopoly
C. Oligopoly
D. Monopolistic Competition
Answer: D
Explanation: The downward-sloping demand curve comes from product differentiation. The normal profit in the long run comes from the lack of significant barriers to entry, which allows new firms to compete away any supernormal profits.

538. Which type of unemployment is the focus of macroeconomic stabilization policies (fiscal and monetary)?
A. Frictional
B. Structural
C. Seasonal
D. Cyclical
Answer: D
Explanation: Stabilization policies aim to manage aggregate demand. Cyclical unemployment is caused by a lack of aggregate demand, so these policies are designed to directly address it. Frictional and structural unemployment require different, supply-side solutions.

539. A country's 'balance on services' would include
A. the value of its car exports.
B. payments for shipping its goods on a foreign-owned vessel.
C. dividends received from its investments abroad.
D. remittances sent home by its workers.
Answer: B
Explanation: Using a foreign shipping company is an import of a transportation service, which is recorded as a debit in the trade in services part of the current account.

540. The theory that markets fail to provide public goods because people can benefit without paying is known as
A. the paradox of thrift.
B. the tragedy of the commons.
C. moral hazard.
D. the free-rider problem.
Answer: D
Explanation: The free-rider problem arises from the non-excludability of public goods. It undermines the market mechanism for provision, as private firms cannot ensure they get paid for the service they provide.

541. If the demand for a product is price elastic, a firm should ______ its price to increase total revenue.
A. increase
B. decrease
C. keep constant
D. double
Answer: B
Explanation: When demand is elastic, a price decrease leads to a proportionally larger increase in quantity demanded, causing total revenue (P x Q) to rise.

542. A major achievement of the Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS) has been the
A. creation of a single currency, the Eco.
B. establishment of a full political union.
C. facilitation of the free movement of persons among member states.
D. complete harmonization of all tax policies.
Answer: C
Explanation: While goals like a single currency and deeper integration are ongoing, the ECOWAS Protocol on Free Movement of Persons has been a significant and largely implemented achievement, allowing citizens to travel more freely within the region.

543. The "law of one price," a concept underlying Purchasing Power Parity, states that identical goods should sell for the same price everywhere, after accounting for
A. trade barriers and exchange rates.
B. consumer tastes and incomes.
C. production costs and profits.
D. government subsidies.
Answer: A
Explanation: The law of one price suggests that arbitrage will ensure identical goods have the same price when expressed in a common currency, provided there are no transport costs, trade barriers, or other frictions.

544. What effect does a depreciation of the cedi have on Ghana's aggregate demand (AD) curve?
A. Shifts AD to the left.
B. Causes a movement up along the AD curve.
C. Shifts AD to the right.
D. Has no effect on the AD curve.
Answer: C
Explanation: A depreciation makes Ghanaian exports cheaper for foreigners and imports more expensive for Ghanaians. This increases net exports (X-M), which is a component of aggregate demand, thus shifting the AD curve to the right.

545. What happens to the demand for and price of government bonds when the central bank conducts expansionary open market operations?
A. Demand for bonds falls, and their price falls.
B. Demand for bonds rises, and their price rises.
C. Demand for bonds rises, and their price falls.
D. Demand for bonds falls, and their price rises.
Answer: B
Explanation: Expansionary OMO means the central bank is buying bonds. This increases the demand for bonds, which, like any good, causes their price to rise. (Note: A rise in bond prices corresponds to a fall in their effective interest rate or yield).

546. Which of these is a 'grassroots' development strategy?
A. A nationwide highway construction project funded by the World Bank.
B. A central bank policy to lower interest rates.
C. An IMF-mandated structural adjustment program.
D. A microfinance program providing small loans to village women.
Answer: D
Explanation: Grassroots strategies are bottom-up approaches that focus on empowering local communities and individuals. Microfinance is a classic example of this.

547. Which economic indicator would be the LEAST useful for analyzing short-term business cycle fluctuations?
A. Monthly unemployment data.
B. Quarterly real GDP data.
C. The Human Development Index (HDI).
D. The index of industrial production.
Answer: C
Explanation: The HDI is a measure of long-term development, based on slow-moving variables like life expectancy and years of schooling. It does not change significantly in the short term and is not useful for tracking business cycles.

548. A fall in the price of computer hardware is likely to cause what change in the market for computer software?
A. A fall in the supply of software.
B. A rise in the demand for software.
C. A fall in the demand for software.
D. A rise in the supply of software.
Answer: B
Explanation: Computer hardware and software are complementary goods. A fall in the price of hardware makes owning a computer cheaper, which will increase the demand for the complementary good, software.

549. For a single-price monopolist, the profit-maximizing level of output occurs where
A. P = MC.
B. demand is unit elastic.
C. MR = MC, and P > MR.
D. TR is maximized.
Answer: C
Explanation: The universal rule is to produce where MR=MC. For a monopolist with a downward-sloping demand curve, price is always greater than marginal revenue at this output level.

550. If the government gives a subsidy to the producers of a good with a positive externality, its aim is to
A. decrease the output to the socially optimal level.
B. increase the output towards the socially optimal level.
C. increase the price of the good for consumers.
D. raise tax revenue.
Answer: B
Explanation: A good with a positive externality is under-produced by the market. A subsidy lowers production costs, shifts the supply curve right, and increases the equilibrium output, moving it closer to the socially desirable level.

Excellent. Here is the fourth set of 250 WASSCE-standard Multiple Choice Questions, with answers and explanations, continuing from the previous sets and based on your provided textbook content.

Chapter 1: Introduction to Economics and Basic Concepts
