551. "Wants being unlimited and resources being finite" describes the concept of
A. opportunity cost.
B. scarcity.
C. economic growth.
D. consumer sovereignty.
Answer: B
Explanation: This is the core definition of scarcity—the fundamental economic problem that necessitates choice.

552. A normative economic statement is one that
A. is based on facts and can be tested.
B. expresses a value judgment or opinion.
C. describes a historical economic event.
D. is used only in microeconomics.
Answer: B
Explanation: Normative statements deal with what should be or ought to be and cannot be proven true or false. For example, "The government should provide free healthcare."

553. The factor of production, Capital, is rewarded with
A. rent.
B. profit.
C. interest.
D. salary.
Answer: C
Explanation: Interest is the payment for the use of man-made capital (a produced means of production) in the production process.

554. An inward shift of a country's Production Possibility Curve (PPC) could be caused by
A. a technological improvement.
B. an increase in the labor force.
C. the destruction of infrastructure due to a natural disaster.
D. a decrease in unemployment.
Answer: C
Explanation: An inward shift signifies a decrease in the economy's productive capacity. A major natural disaster that destroys factories, roads, and other capital would cause such a shift.

555. The satisfaction derived from consuming a good is known as
A. utility.
B. demand.
C. price.
D. cost.
Answer: A
Explanation: Utility is the economic term for the want-satisfying power of a good or service.

556. A society that relies on bartering for most of its transactions likely operates under a(n)
A. market economy.
B. command economy.
C. traditional economy.
D. mixed economy.
Answer: C
Explanation: Barter, the direct exchange of goods for goods, is a characteristic of simple, often traditional economies that lack a sophisticated monetary system.

557. Which of the following is a key question that all economic systems must answer?
A. How to eliminate scarcity?
B. How to achieve a perfectly equal distribution of income?
C. How to produce goods and services?
D. How to ensure profits for all firms?
Answer: C
Explanation: The three fundamental economic questions are What to produce, How to produce, and For whom to produce. Scarcity cannot be eliminated.

558. A university degree obtained by a worker increases their value as which factor of production?
A. Land
B. Labour
C. Capital
D. Enterprise
Answer: B
Explanation: Education and skills are components of human capital, which enhances the quality and productivity of labor.

559. If you decide to spend two hours studying for an economics exam instead of working at your part-time job that pays GHS 15 per hour, the opportunity cost of your decision is
A. zero, because studying has a future benefit.
B. the two hours of leisure time you also gave up.
C. the GHS 30 you would have earned.
D. the cost of the economics textbook.
Answer: C
Explanation: Opportunity cost is the value of the single best alternative foregone. In this case, the specific value given is the GHS 30 from the part-time job.

560. Mobility of labour refers to the ability of workers to
A. change occupations or geographical locations.
B. perform multiple tasks at once.
C. store their work for future use.
D. own the means of production.
Answer: A
Explanation: Mobility of labor is divided into occupational mobility (changing jobs/professions) and geographical mobility (moving to a different place for work).

Chapter 2: Demand, Supply, and Market Equilibrium

561. If two goods, A and B, are complements, a fall in the price of Good A will cause the
A. demand for Good B to fall.
B. demand for Good B to rise.
C. supply of Good B to rise.
D. demand for Good A to fall.
Answer: B
Explanation: If Good A becomes cheaper, more of it will be consumed. Since Good B is used with Good A, the demand for Good B will also increase (shift right).

562. An effective price floor must be set
A. above the equilibrium price.
B. below the equilibrium price.
C. at the equilibrium price.
D. by consumers.
Answer: A
Explanation: For a price floor (minimum price) to be binding and have an effect, it must be set above the level where the market would naturally clear.

563. An increase in the cost of raw materials used to produce a good will
A. shift the supply curve to the right.
B. shift the demand curve to the left.
C. cause a movement along the supply curve.
D. shift the supply curve to the left.
Answer: D
Explanation: Higher input costs make production less profitable at any given price, causing producers to supply less. This is shown as a leftward shift of the supply curve.

564. If a good has an income elasticity of demand (YED) of 1.8, the good is a
A. necessity and an inferior good.
B. luxury and an inferior good.
C. luxury and a normal good.
D. necessity and a normal good.
Answer: C
Explanation: Since YED is positive, it is a normal good. Since YED > 1, demand increases more than proportionally with income, which is the definition of a luxury good.

565. The term 'ceteris paribus' in the context of the law of demand means
A. consumers' incomes are equal.
B. all other factors affecting demand are held constant.
C. the economy is in equilibrium.
D. prices will not change.
Answer: B
Explanation: Ceteris paribus, Latin for "all other things being equal," is a crucial assumption used to isolate the relationship between just two variables (like price and quantity demanded).

566. Total revenue is maximized for a good with a downward-sloping linear demand curve at the point where the price elasticity of demand is
A. greater than 1.
B. less than 1.
C. equal to 1.
D. equal to 0.
Answer: C
Explanation: Total revenue reaches its peak when demand is unit elastic. At this point, any further change in price (up or down) will cause total revenue to fall.

567. A subsidy given to consumers of a product will
A. shift the demand curve left.
B. shift the supply curve left.
C. shift the demand curve right.
D. shift the supply curve right.
Answer: C
Explanation: A subsidy to consumers effectively lowers the price they have to pay, which increases their demand for the product at every market price level, shifting the demand curve to the right.

568. When the supply of a good is perfectly inelastic, the imposition of a tax on it will cause its price to
A. rise by the full amount of the tax.
B. rise by less than the full amount of the tax.
C. fall by the full amount of the tax.
D. remain unchanged.
Answer: D
Explanation: If supply is perfectly inelastic (vertical supply curve), producers will supply the same quantity regardless of the price they receive. Therefore, they cannot pass any of the tax forward to consumers and must bear the full burden themselves. The market price for consumers remains the same, but the after-tax price received by producers falls.

569. An increase in the number of firms producing a particular good will cause
A. the market supply curve to shift right.
B. the market supply curve to shift left.
C. the market demand curve to shift right.
D. the market demand curve to shift left.
Answer: A
Explanation: An increase in the number of sellers leads to a greater total quantity being supplied at any given price, which is a rightward shift of the market supply curve.

570. The existence of a shortage in a market indicates that the current price is
A. above the equilibrium price.
B. below the equilibrium price.
C. equal to the equilibrium price.
D. determined by the government.
Answer: B
Explanation: A shortage (excess demand) occurs when the price is too low, encouraging high demand and discouraging supply.

Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly

571. One major difference between a firm in perfect competition and a monopoly is the firm's ability to
A. earn profits in the short run.
B. control its price.
C. choose its output level.
D. minimize its costs.
Answer: B
Explanation: A perfectly competitive firm is a price taker with no control over price. A monopolist is a price maker with significant control over price.

572. A perfectly competitive firm is in short-run equilibrium, and its price is greater than its average total cost (P > ATC). The firm is
A. earning a supernormal profit.
B. earning a normal profit.
C. incurring a loss.
D. at its shutdown point.
Answer: A
Explanation: When the price (average revenue) per unit is greater than the average total cost per unit, the firm is earning a profit above the normal level.

573. In which market structure is the firm's demand curve also the market demand curve?
A. Perfect Competition
B. Monopolistic Competition
C. Oligopoly
D. Monopoly
Answer: D
Explanation: Since the monopolist is the only firm in the industry, it faces the entire market demand curve for its product.

574. For a firm in perfect competition, its marginal revenue is always equal to its
A. marginal cost.
B. average total cost.
C. price.
D. total revenue.
Answer: C
Explanation: Because the perfectly competitive firm is a price taker, each additional unit it sells adds an amount to its total revenue exactly equal to the constant market price. So, P = MR.

575. What is the main reason a monopoly can earn supernormal profits in the long run?
A. It produces where MR=MC.
B. Its demand curve is downward sloping.
C. It benefits from economies of scale.
D. There are high barriers to entry.
Answer: D
Explanation: Barriers to entry (legal, technical, or economic) prevent new firms from entering the market to compete away the monopolist's supernormal profits.

576. 'Allocative inefficiency' means that for the last unit produced,
A. price is equal to marginal cost.
B. price is greater than marginal cost.
C. price is less than marginal cost.
D. marginal cost is at its minimum.
Answer: B
Explanation: When P > MC, it means society values the last unit more than it cost to produce. An efficient allocation would involve producing more, up to the point where P=MC. This inefficiency (P>MC) is a hallmark of monopoly.

577. A 'patent' is a type of
A. tax on a firm's profits.
B. subsidy for research.
C. legal barrier to entry.
D. natural barrier to entry.
Answer: C
Explanation: A patent grants the inventor exclusive legal rights to an invention for a set period, legally preventing others from competing.

578. The process of new firms entering a perfectly competitive market where supernormal profits are being made will lead to
A. a fall in market supply and a rise in market price.
B. a rise in market supply and a rise in market price.
C. a rise in market supply and a fall in market price.
D. no change in market supply or price.
Answer: C
Explanation: New entrants increase the total number of sellers, shifting the market supply curve to the right. This leads to a lower equilibrium market price, which erodes the supernormal profits.

579. If a monopolist finds that its MR is greater than its MC, it should
A. increase its price.
B. decrease its output.
C. increase its output.
D. maintain its current output.
Answer: C
Explanation: If MR > MC, producing and selling one more unit adds more to revenue than to cost, so profit will increase. The firm should continue expanding output until MR = MC.

580. A public utility like an electricity distribution company is often a
A. perfect competitor.
B. monopolistic competitor.
C. natural monopoly.
D. cartel.
Answer: C
Explanation: These industries typically have very high fixed costs and large economies of scale, meaning a single firm can supply the entire market at a lower average cost than multiple firms. This makes it a natural monopoly.

Chapter 4: Monopolistic Competition and Oligopoly

581. Which of the following is NOT a feature of monopolistic competition?
A. Large number of firms
B. A standardized, identical product
C. Free entry and exit
D. Downward-sloping demand curve for each firm
Answer: B
Explanation: The defining feature of monopolistic competition is product differentiation, not a standardized product. A standardized product is a feature of perfect competition.

582. An example of a differentiated product is
A. crude oil from different countries.
B. shares of a specific company.
C. brands of laundry detergent.
D. electricity from a national grid.
Answer: C
Explanation: Brands of detergent are chemically similar but are differentiated in the minds of consumers through branding, packaging, scent, and advertising.

583. Game theory is most useful for analyzing the behavior of firms in
A. perfect competition.
B. monopoly.
C. monopolistic competition.
D. oligopoly.
Answer: D
Explanation: Game theory is essential for understanding oligopoly because of the strategic interdependence, where firms must make decisions based on what they expect their few rivals to do.

584. In the kinked demand curve model, it is assumed that rivals will
A. match both price increases and price decreases.
B. match price decreases but not price increases.
C. match price increases but not price decreases.
D. ignore all price changes.
Answer: B
Explanation: This asymmetric reaction is the core assumption. Rivals are assumed to protect their market share by matching price cuts, but are happy to gain market share by not matching price hikes.

585. Tacit collusion occurs when firms in an oligopoly
A. sign a formal agreement to fix prices.
B. engage in a price war.
C. merge to form a monopoly.
D. coordinate their actions without an explicit agreement.
Answer: D
Explanation: Tacit collusion is an informal understanding, such as price leadership, where firms act in parallel without openly communicating or signing contracts.

586. One reason cartels are difficult to maintain is because they are
A. generally legal and encouraged by governments.
B. subject to the Prisoner's Dilemma, creating an incentive to cheat.
C. less profitable than competing.
D. only possible in markets with thousands of firms.
Answer: B
Explanation: Each cartel member is in a Prisoner's Dilemma. The best outcome for the group is to cooperate, but the best outcome for an individual member (if others cooperate) is to cheat, which often leads to the collapse of the agreement.

587. "Excess capacity" is a feature of the long-run equilibrium in
A. monopoly only.
B. perfect competition only.
C. oligopoly only.
D. monopolistic competition only.
Answer: D
Explanation: Firms in monopolistic competition produce at a level below their productively efficient scale (minimum ATC) in the long run.

588. Compared to a monopoly outcome, the price in a monopolistically competitive market in the long run is likely to be
A. higher, and firms will earn supernormal profit.
B. lower, and firms will earn normal profit.
C. the same, and firms will earn normal profit.
D. lower, but firms will earn supernormal profit.
Answer: B
Explanation: Due to free entry, long-run profits are competed down to the normal level in monopolistic competition, which requires price to be lower than what a monopolist (with blocked entry) could sustain.

589. Which of the following is an industry dominated by an oligopoly?
A. Mobile telecommunications providers.
B. Street-side kenkey sellers.
C. Small-scale cassava farming.
D. Individual private tutors.
Answer: A
Explanation: The mobile telecommunications market in most countries is dominated by a few large firms (e.g., MTN, Vodafone, AirtelTigo in Ghana).

590. Firms engage in product differentiation to
A. reduce their production costs.
B. make their product a perfect substitute for others.
C. increase brand loyalty and gain some price-setting power.
D. encourage the entry of new firms.
Answer: C
Explanation: By making its product seem unique, a firm can create a loyal customer base and face a less elastic, downward-sloping demand curve, allowing it more control over its price.

Chapter 5: Consumer Theory

591. An Engel curve for a luxury good is
A. downward sloping.
B. upward sloping and becomes steeper as income rises.
C. vertical.
D. upward sloping and becomes flatter as income rises.
Answer: B
Explanation: A luxury good has a high income elasticity (YED > 1). This means that as income rises, expenditure on the good increases more than proportionally, making the Engel curve steeper.

592. The change in consumption resulting purely from a change in a consumer's purchasing power is called the
A. substitution effect.
B. price effect.
C. income effect.
D. utility effect.
Answer: C
Explanation: The income effect isolates how a change in real income (caused by a price change) affects the quantity of a good a consumer buys.

593. If a consumer moves along a single indifference curve, their total utility
A. increases.
B. decreases.
C. remains constant.
D. first increases, then decreases.
Answer: C
Explanation: An indifference curve connects all the bundles of goods that provide the exact same level of satisfaction, so utility is constant along the curve.

594. At consumer equilibrium, the marginal rate of substitution (MRS) is equal to
A. the consumer's total income.
B. the ratio of marginal utilities.
C. zero.
D. the ratio of the prices of the two goods.
Answer: D
Explanation: At the point of tangency, the slope of the indifference curve (MRS) is equal to the slope of the budget line (the price ratio, Px/Py).

595. A consumer's budget constraint shows the
A. combinations of two goods that give equal satisfaction.
B. relationship between price and quantity demanded.
C. combinations of two goods the consumer can afford.
D. relationship between income and quantity demanded.
Answer: C
Explanation: The budget constraint or budget line defines the boundary of the consumer's feasible choices, showing all the bundles they can purchase given their income and the prices of the goods.

596. A consumer's preference for an apple over an orange is an example of which approach to utility?
A. Cardinal
B. Ordinal
C. Marginal
D. Total
Answer: B
Explanation: Ordinal utility is based on ranking preferences (preferring A to B, or being indifferent) rather than assigning numerical values to them.

597. A key assumption of indifference curve analysis is 'transitivity'. This means if a consumer prefers bundle A to B, and B to C, then
A. they must prefer C to A.
B. they must prefer A to C.
C. they are indifferent between A and C.
D. they cannot afford any of the bundles.
Answer: B
Explanation: Transitivity ensures that consumer preferences are logical and consistent.

598. For an inferior good, the income effect of a price fall is
A. positive.
B. negative.
C. zero.
D. the same as the substitution effect.
Answer: B
Explanation: When the price falls, real income rises. For an inferior good, a rise in real income leads to a decrease in quantity demanded, hence the income effect is negative (works against the substitution effect).

599. What happens to the marginal rate of substitution as one moves down along a convex indifference curve?
A. It increases.
B. It decreases.
C. It remains constant.
D. It becomes zero.
Answer: B
Explanation: The slope of a convex indifference curve becomes flatter as you move down and to the right. This diminishing slope reflects a diminishing MRS.

600. The total utility derived from consuming four oranges is 50 utils, and from five oranges is 55 utils. The marginal utility of the fifth orange is
A. 55 utils.
B. 11 utils.
C. 5 utils.
D. 105 utils.
Answer: C
Explanation: Marginal utility is the additional utility from one more unit. MU(5th) = TU(5) - TU(4) = 55 - 50 = 5 utils.

Chapter 6: Producer Theory

601. A production process exhibits increasing returns to scale when
A. doubling all inputs less than doubles the output.
B. doubling all inputs exactly doubles the output.
C. doubling all inputs more than doubles the output.
D. doubling one input doubles the output.
Answer: C
Explanation: This is the definition of increasing returns to scale (which corresponds to economies of scale), a long-run phenomenon where output increases proportionally more than the increase in all inputs.

602. An isocost line will shift outwards in a parallel fashion if
A. the price of one input falls.
B. the firm's total budget for inputs increases.
C. technology improves.
D. the price of both inputs rises proportionally.
Answer: B
Explanation: An increase in the total cost outlay allows the firm to buy more of both inputs, causing a parallel outward shift of the isocost line.

603. Which of the following is NOT a reason for economies of scale?
A. Specialization of labor.
B. Managerial problems of coordination and control.
C. Bulk purchasing of raw materials.
D. Use of larger, more efficient machinery.
Answer: B
Explanation: Managerial problems are a source of diseconomies of scale, which cause average costs to rise as a firm becomes too large. The others are sources of economies of scale.

604. At the point where Marginal Cost (MC) equals Average Variable Cost (AVC), the
A. AVC is rising.
B. AVC is falling.
C. AVC is at its minimum.
D. AFC is at its minimum.
Answer: C
Explanation: The MC curve always intersects both the AVC and ATC curves at their respective minimum points.

605. If a firm's total revenue is GHS 10,000, its explicit costs are GHS 6,000, and its implicit costs are GHS 3,000, its economic profit is
A. GHS 4,000.
B. GHS 7,000.
C. GHS 1,000.
D. GHS 3,000.
Answer: C
Explanation: Economic Profit = Total Revenue - (Explicit Costs + Implicit Costs) = 10,000 - (6,000 + 3,000) = 10,000 - 9,000 = GHS 1,000.

606. Which cost is always zero when a firm's output is zero?
A. Total Fixed Cost
B. Average Fixed Cost
C. Total Variable Cost
D. Marginal Cost
Answer: C
Explanation: Variable costs, by definition, are costs that vary with output. If output is zero, there are no variable costs. Fixed costs must still be paid.

607. A firm's Total Revenue curve for a monopolist is
A. a straight upward-sloping line from the origin.
B. an inverted U-shape.
C. a horizontal line.
D. a rectangular hyperbola.
Answer: B
Explanation: As a monopolist increases output (by lowering price), TR initially rises, reaches a maximum (where demand is unit elastic), and then falls.

608. At the point of producer equilibrium (least-cost combination), the ratio of the marginal products of the inputs (MPL/MPK) is equal to
A. the firm's total output.
B. the firm's total cost.
C. the ratio of the input prices (PL/PK).
D. one.
Answer: C
Explanation: The equilibrium condition is MRTS = PL/PK. Since MRTS = MPL/MPK, the condition can be stated as MPL/MPK = PL/PK.

609. The long-run average cost (LRAC) curve is also known as the
A. "envelope curve."
B. "production function."
C. "isocost line."
D. "profit curve."
Answer: A
Explanation: The LRAC curve is drawn as an envelope of all the possible short-run average total cost (SRATC) curves, with each SRATC representing a different plant size.

610. The goal of a profit-maximizing firm is to produce the quantity where the
A. vertical distance between Total Revenue and Total Cost is maximized.
B. gap between price and average cost is maximized.
C. slope of the total cost curve is zero.
D. average cost is at its minimum.
Answer: A
Explanation: Profit is the difference between TR and TC. Maximizing profit means finding the output level where this positive difference is greatest. This is equivalent to producing where MR=MC.

Chapter 7 to 16 (More Advanced and Comprehensive Questions)

611. If the required reserve ratio is 10% and the central bank sells GHS 50 million worth of bonds, the maximum potential change in the money supply is a(n)
A. decrease of GHS 500 million.
B. increase of GHS 500 million.
C. decrease of GHS 50 million.
D. increase of GHS 50 million.
Answer: A
Explanation: Selling bonds is a contractionary policy that removes GHS 50 million from bank reserves. The multiplier is 1/0.10 = 10. The maximum contraction is 50m x 10 = GHS 500 million.

612. An increase in public debt will increase future
A. tax revenues.
B. capital expenditures.
C. recurrent expenditures in the form of interest payments.
D. foreign exchange reserves.
Answer: C
Explanation: The accumulated debt must be serviced, meaning the government must make regular interest payments, which are a form of recurrent expenditure in its budget.

613. Which of the following best represents a 'leakage' from the circular flow of income?
A. Government spending on infrastructure.
B. A firm's investment in new machinery.
C. A household saving part of its income.
D. A country's exports of goods.
Answer: C
Explanation: A leakage is a withdrawal from the spending stream. Savings, taxes, and imports are leakages. Injections are investment, government spending, and exports.

614. A key difference between a common market and a customs union is that a common market adds
A. free trade in goods.
B. a common external tariff.
C. free movement of factors of production.
D. a common currency.
Answer: C
Explanation: A common market incorporates all the features of a customs union (free internal trade + common external tariff) and adds the free movement of labor and capital among member countries.

615. 'Dutch Disease' is a phenomenon where
A. a boom in a natural resource export strengthens the currency, harming other export sectors.
B. a country provides extensive healthcare services.
C. a nation's human capital is eroded by emigration.
D. an entire banking system collapses due to bad loans.
Answer: A
Explanation: The influx of foreign currency from the booming resource sector causes the national currency to appreciate, making the country's other exports (e.g., manufacturing, agriculture) more expensive and less competitive on world markets.

616. The demand for a currency is 'derived' because it depends on the
A. interest rate set by the central bank.
B. demand for a country's goods, services, and assets.
C. supply of that currency on the forex market.
D. country's GDP growth rate.
Answer: B
Explanation: People don't demand a currency for its own sake, but because they need it to buy things from that country—its goods, services, or financial assets like stocks and bonds.

617. An 'automatic stabilizer' in fiscal policy is a program that
A. requires new legislative action each year to take effect.
B. automatically pushes the economy toward equilibrium without new government action.
C. keeps the government budget balanced at all times.
D. keeps the exchange rate stable.
Answer: B
Explanation: Automatic stabilizers like the progressive tax system or unemployment benefits automatically reduce spending power in a boom and support it in a recession, thus dampening the business cycle.

618. A major policy tool for addressing a negative externality like industrial pollution is a(n)
A. subsidy for production.
B. government purchase of the industry's output.
C. tax on the pollution (Pigouvian tax).
D. income tax cut for the industry's workers.
Answer: C
Explanation: A Pigouvian tax aims to make the polluter pay for the social cost of their activity, creating a market-based incentive to reduce the externality.

619. In the short run, a large, unexpected increase in aggregate demand will likely cause
A. cost-push inflation.
B. stagflation.
C. deflation.
D. demand-pull inflation.
Answer: D
Explanation: When aggregate demand rises faster than the economy can expand its supply, the excess demand "pulls" the general price level upwards.

620. The concept of "Human Capital" emphasizes the importance of which factors for economic development?
A. Machinery and infrastructure.
B. Natural resource endowments.
C. Education, skills, and health of the population.
D. Stable political systems and property rights.
Answer: C
Explanation: Human capital refers specifically to the productive capabilities embodied in people, which are primarily developed through investments in education and health.

621. An example of 'tied aid' would be
A. a loan from the IMF with policy conditions attached.
B. money given by a foreign government that must be used to buy equipment from that same foreign country.
C. remittances sent home by migrant workers.
D. a grant from an NGO for a specific community project.
Answer: B
Explanation: Tied aid restricts the recipient's purchasing choices to the donor country, which can make the aid less valuable than if the recipient could buy from the most competitive global source.

622. Which situation best illustrates the concept of 'deadweight loss'?
A. A consumer gets a good deal during a sale, increasing their consumer surplus.
B. A tax on a good causes the total economic surplus (consumer + producer) to fall.
C. A firm breaks even, earning only a normal profit.
D. The government runs a budget deficit.
Answer: B
Explanation: Deadweight loss is the loss of economic efficiency and total surplus that occurs when a market distortion, like a tax, prevents mutually beneficial trades from happening.

623. What does the vertical long-run Phillips curve imply about policymaking?
A. There is a permanent trade-off between inflation and unemployment.
B. Monetary policy can permanently keep unemployment below the natural rate.
C. To reduce unemployment permanently, supply-side policies are needed to lower the natural rate.
D. Fiscal policy is always more effective than monetary policy.
Answer: C
Explanation: The vertical LRPC implies that demand-side policies (monetary and fiscal) cannot sustainably lower unemployment below the natural rate. To achieve this, policies must address the causes of frictional and structural unemployment directly.

624. Which account in the Balance of Payments would record a Ghanaian company buying shares on the New York Stock Exchange?
A. Current Account, as a credit.
B. Current Account, as a debit.
C. Financial Account, as a credit.
D. Financial Account, as a debit.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is a purchase of a foreign financial asset, representing a capital outflow from Ghana. It's a portfolio investment, which is part of the financial account, and is recorded as a debit (-).

625. If Ghana's inflation rate is 10% and the UK's inflation rate is 2%, the theory of Purchasing Power Parity predicts that the Ghana Cedi will
A. appreciate against the British Pound by 8%.
B. depreciate against the British Pound by 8%.
C. appreciate against the British Pound by 12%.
D. remain stable against the British Pound.
Answer: B
Explanation: Relative PPP states that the exchange rate will adjust to reflect the inflation differential. Since Ghana's inflation is 8% higher, the Cedi's purchasing power is falling faster, so it is expected to depreciate by approximately 8% to maintain parity.

626. Which of these is a function of the World Trade Organization (WTO)?
A. Providing emergency loans to countries.
B. Providing long-term development finance.
C. Acting as a forum for multilateral trade negotiations and dispute settlement.
D. Regulating global health standards.
Answer: C
Explanation: The WTO's primary roles are to oversee the rules of international trade, facilitate negotiations to liberalize trade, and provide a mechanism for resolving trade disputes between member countries.

627. Which of these is a key feature of an oligopoly?
A. Ease of entry for new firms.
B. Strategic interdependence.
C. Large number of small firms.
D. Homogeneous product only.
Answer: B
Explanation: Due to the small number of dominant firms, each firm must consider the potential reactions of its rivals when making its own price and output decisions.

628. If a country imposes a tariff on an imported good, which of the following groups will benefit?
A. Domestic consumers of the good.
B. Domestic producers of the good.
C. Foreign producers of the good.
D. The society as a whole.
Answer: B
Explanation: The tariff raises the domestic price of the imported good, allowing domestic producers of the same good to also charge a higher price and sell more. They are protected from foreign competition.

629. When a firm's Long-Run Average Cost (LRAC) is increasing, it is experiencing
A. economies of scale.
B. constant returns to scale.
C. diseconomies of scale.
D. diminishing marginal returns.
Answer: C
Explanation: Diseconomies of scale occur when a firm grows so large that it becomes inefficient, causing its long-run average costs to rise as output increases.

630. According to the Keynesian consumption function C = 100 + 0.75Yd, what is the value of autonomous consumption?
A. 100
B. 0.75
C. 0.25
D. 75
Answer: A
Explanation: In the equation C = a + bYd, 'a' represents autonomous consumption—the amount consumed even when disposable income is zero. In this case, it is 100.

631. The transfer of a business process, such as customer support, to an external company is known as
A. offshoring.
B. outsourcing.
C. diversification.
D. insourcing.
Answer: B
Explanation: Outsourcing is the act of contracting a specific business function to a third-party provider, regardless of that provider's location.

632. Which of these is NOT considered a part of the labor force?
A. An employed factory worker.
B. A university graduate actively looking for a job.
C. A retired pensioner who is not looking for work.
D. A part-time shop assistant.
Answer: C
Explanation: The labor force includes the employed and the unemployed (who are actively seeking work). People who are not working and not looking for work (like retirees, full-time students not seeking jobs) are not in the labor force.

633. If a government increases unemployment benefits, it might lead to an increase in which type of unemployment?
A. Cyclical
B. Seasonal
C. Frictional
D. Demand-deficient
Answer: C
Explanation: More generous unemployment benefits can reduce the urgency for unemployed workers to find a new job, potentially increasing the time they spend searching. This increases the level of frictional unemployment and can raise the natural rate.

634. A government budget deficit is most likely to rise during
A. a business cycle peak.
B. a period of high inflation.
C. a recession.
D. a period of balanced trade.
Answer: C
Explanation: During a recession, tax revenues automatically fall (as incomes and profits drop) and government spending on automatic stabilizers like unemployment benefits rises, widening the budget deficit.

635. For a Giffen good, the
A. income effect is positive.
B. demand curve is vertical.
C. substitution effect is negative.
D. income effect is negative and larger than the substitution effect.
Answer: D
Explanation: A price fall for a Giffen good has a positive substitution effect (buy more) and a negative income effect (buy less). For it to be a Giffen good, this negative income effect must be so strong that it outweighs the substitution effect, causing overall quantity demanded to fall.

636. If the cross-price elasticity of demand for two goods is +1.5, the goods are
A. complements.
B. inferior goods.
C. substitutes.
D. normal goods.
Answer: C
Explanation: A positive cross-price elasticity indicates that an increase in the price of one good leads to an increase in the demand for the other. This defines them as substitute goods. A value greater than 1 means they are close substitutes.

637. What type of policy is the setting of a minimum wage?
A. Monetary policy
B. Fiscal policy
C. A price floor in the labor market
D. A price ceiling in the labor market
Answer: C
Explanation: A minimum wage is a legally mandated minimum price for labor, making it a type of price floor.

638. A central bank's "repo rate" is the interest rate at which
A. it lends to the government.
B. it lends short-term funds to commercial banks.
C. commercial banks lend to each other.
D. consumers can borrow from commercial banks.
Answer: B
Explanation: The repo (repurchase) rate is a key policy rate where the central bank provides short-term liquidity to the banking system, typically against the collateral of government securities.

639. An example of a non-tariff barrier to trade is
A. a 15% ad valorem duty on imported textiles.
B. a fixed tax of $100 on each imported car.
C. an import quota limiting the number of cars that can be imported.
D. an export subsidy.
Answer: C
Explanation: A quota is a non-tariff barrier because it restricts the quantity, not the price, of imports. Subsidies are also NTBs, but quotas are a more direct restriction on imports. Let me re-evaluate. Quotas are a classic example. C is a stronger answer than D as subsidies are often aimed at boosting exports, while NTBs are primarily about restricting imports.

640. 'Securitization', a key factor in the 2008 financial crisis, is the process of
A. making bank lending more secure.
B. increasing central bank reserves.
C. bundling individual loans into a new financial asset and selling it to investors.
D. governments issuing bonds to finance deficits.
Answer: C
Explanation: Securitization involves pooling non-tradable assets like mortgages and repackaging them into tradable securities (like Mortgage-Backed Securities) that can be sold on the open market.

641. Which statement best reflects the Keynesian view of a recession?
A. It is a temporary deviation that will quickly self-correct.
B. It is caused by an excess of aggregate supply.
C. It can persist due to sticky wages and insufficient aggregate demand.
D. It should be addressed with supply-side policies only.
Answer: C
Explanation: The core of Keynesian thought is that due to price and wage rigidities, the economy can get stuck in an underemployment equilibrium if aggregate demand is too low, requiring government intervention.

642. The "tragedy of the commons" can be applied to which global environmental problem?
A. The depletion of global fish stocks.
B. A local company polluting a small stream.
C. A person littering in a public park.
D. A factory generating noise that disturbs its neighbors.
Answer: A
Explanation: The open oceans are a classic global common resource. The fish are rivalrous (if I catch one, you can't) but non-excludable (it's hard to stop anyone from fishing), leading to over-exploitation.

643. When economists refer to "capital," they are typically referring to
A. money.
B. stocks and bonds.
C. produced goods used as inputs for further production.
D. natural resources.
Answer: C
Explanation: This distinguishes physical capital (machinery, tools, buildings) from financial capital (money, shares).

644. If the number of buyers in a market increases, the
A. supply curve will shift to the right.
B. supply curve will shift to the left.
C. demand curve will shift to the right.
D. demand curve will shift to the left.
Answer: C
Explanation: An increase in the number of buyers is a determinant of demand. More buyers mean a greater quantity demanded at every price level, shifting the demand curve right.

645. Which of the following is most likely to have a price elasticity of demand close to zero?
A. A specific brand of soap
B. A life-saving medication for a critically ill patient
C. A vacation in Dubai
D. A tomato from a specific seller at the market
Answer: B
Explanation: When a good is absolutely essential for survival and has no substitutes, its demand is virtually unresponsive to price, meaning it is perfectly or nearly perfectly inelastic (PED ≈ 0).

646. The law of diminishing returns explains why
A. a firm's long-run average cost curve is U-shaped.
B. a firm's short-run marginal cost curve eventually slopes upward.
C. the demand curve for a product slopes downward.
D. the production possibility curve is bowed outwards.
Answer: B
Explanation: As diminishing returns to a variable factor set in, each additional unit of input produces less additional output. This means the cost of producing each additional unit of output (marginal cost) must start to rise.

647. Which account of the BoP records interest and dividends paid to foreign investors?
A. The Financial Account, as a debit.
B. The Current Account, as a credit.
C. The Capital Account, as a debit.
D. The Current Account, as a debit.
Answer: D
Explanation: This is an income payment to a foreign factor of production (capital). It is recorded under "Primary Income" in the Current Account and is a debit because it's an outflow of funds.

648. A government's recurrent expenditure would include
A. construction of a new airport.
B. repayment of the principal on a loan.
C. investment in a state-owned enterprise.
D. payment of salaries to civil servants.
Answer: D
Explanation: Salaries are a day-to-day running cost that recurs annually, making them a recurrent expenditure. The other options are forms of capital expenditure.

649. The multiplier effect is made smaller by
A. a higher marginal propensity to consume.
B. a lower marginal propensity to save.
C. a higher marginal propensity to import.
D. a decrease in income taxes.
Answer: C
Explanation: The marginal propensity to import is a leakage from the circular flow of income. The larger this leakage, the less of each round of income is re-spent on domestic goods, thus reducing the size of the multiplier.

650. The difference between GDP at market prices and GDP at factor cost is
A. depreciation.
B. net factor income from abroad.
C. net indirect taxes (indirect taxes minus subsidies).
D. transfer payments.
Answer: C
Explanation: Market prices include indirect taxes levied by the government and are reduced by subsidies. Factor cost represents the income actually earned by the factors of production. To go from factor cost to market price, you add net indirect taxes.

Of course. Here are 250 WAEC-standard Multiple Choice Questions (MCQs) with answers and detailed explanations, generated from the provided textbook content.

---

### **Chapter 1: Introduction to Economics and Basic Concepts (Questions 1-16)**

1.  According to Lionel Robbins, economics is the science which studies human behavior as a relationship between ends and _______.
    A) scarce means with no alternative uses
    B) abundant means with alternative uses
    C) scarce means which have alternative uses
    D) abundant means with no alternative uses

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The definition by Lionel Robbins (1932) explicitly states that economics studies human behavior concerning "ends and scarce means which have alternative uses," highlighting the core concepts of scarcity and choice.

2.  The fundamental economic problem of every society is _______.
    A) the problem of unemployment
    B) the issue of inflation
    C) the challenge of poverty
    D) the existence of scarcity

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Scarcity is the fundamental economic problem that arises because human wants are unlimited while resources are limited. All other economic problems stem from this central issue.

3.  The factor of production that combines other factors and undertakes risks is known as _______.
    A) Labour
    B) Enterprise
    C) Capital
    D) Land

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Enterprise (or Entrepreneurship) is the specialized human skill responsible for organizing other factors (land, labour, capital) and bearing the risks associated with a business venture.

4.  The reward for the use of land in economics is known as _______.
    A) Profit
    B) Wages
    C) Interest
    D) Rent

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Rent is the payment made for the use of land or any natural resource used in production. Wages are for labour, interest is for capital, and profit is for enterprise.

5.  The concept of opportunity cost is best defined as the _______.
    A) total cost of producing a good
    B) money paid for a commodity
    C) value of the next best alternative foregone
    D) actual price of a commodity

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Opportunity cost represents the real cost of making a choice, measured in terms of the value of the next best alternative that had to be given up.

6.  A point *inside* a Production Possibility Curve (PPC) represents _______.
    A) an unattainable level of production
    B) full employment of resources
    C) inefficient production or unemployment
    D) economic growth

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Points inside the PPC indicate that the economy is producing less than its potential, which is a sign of inefficiency or underemployment of resources (e.g., labor, capital).

7.  Which of the following is a characteristic of human wants?
    A) They are limited.
    B) They are always necessities.
    C) They are competitive.
    D) They are constant over time.

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Human wants are competitive because resources are limited. Satisfying one want often means giving up the opportunity to satisfy another. Wants are unlimited, not always necessities, and they vary over time.

8.  In a command economic system, the three fundamental economic questions are primarily answered by _______.
    A) individual buyers and sellers
    B) traditions and customs
    C) a central planning authority
    D) market forces of supply and demand

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A command economy is characterized by centralized planning where the government or a state agency makes key economic decisions about what, how, and for whom to produce.

9.  Man-made goods that are used to produce other goods and services are referred to as _______.
    A) Land
    B) Labour
    C) Capital
    D) Enterprise

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Capital, in economics, specifically refers to produced means of production, such as machinery, tools, buildings, and infrastructure.

10. A shift of the entire Production Possibility Curve outwards is caused by _______.
    A) a decrease in unemployment
    B) an increase in demand
    C) technological advancement
    D) efficient allocation of resources

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** An outward shift of the PPC signifies economic growth, which is caused by an increase in the quantity or quality of resources or, as in this case, technological advancement that increases productive capacity.

11. The branch of economics that focuses on the economy as a whole is known as _______.
    A) Social economics
    B) Microeconomics
    C) Macroeconomics
    D) Normative economics

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Macroeconomics deals with aggregate economic variables like national income, inflation, unemployment, and economic growth, which pertain to the economy as a whole.

12. A car and petrol are examples of goods with _______ demand.
    A) competitive
    B) complementary
    C) composite
    D) derived

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Complementary goods are used in conjunction with each other. A want for a car is often accompanied by a want for petrol.

13. The main distinguishing feature of a mixed economy is the _______.
    A) dominance of the private sector
    B) complete absence of government intervention
    C) reliance solely on traditional methods
    D) coexistence of private and public sectors in resource allocation

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** A mixed economy combines elements of market and command economies, where the price mechanism operates but the government also intervenes and may own certain resources or enterprises.

14. Labour as a factor of production cannot be stored because it is _______.
    A) mobile
    B) heterogeneous
    C) perishable
    D) a human factor

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Labour is perishable, meaning if a worker is idle for a period, that period's labor is lost forever and cannot be saved for later use.

15. The three fundamental economic questions are _______.
    A) When, where, and how to produce?
    B) What, how, and for whom to produce?
    C) What, why, and when to produce?
    D) Who, what, and why to produce?

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Due to scarcity, every society must decide "What to produce?", "How to produce?", and "For whom to produce?". These are the three basic problems of choice.

16. Which of the following is an example of a luxury?
    A) A comfortable bed
    B) A fan
    C) Basic food
    D) A sports car

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Luxuries are non-essential goods desired for status or extreme comfort. A sports car fits this description, while the others are either necessities or comforts.

### **Chapter 2: Demand, Supply, and Market Equilibrium (Questions 17-32)**

17. The law of demand states that, ceteris paribus, the higher the price, the _______.
    A) lower the quantity supplied
    B) higher the quantity demanded
    C) higher the quantity supplied
    D) lower the quantity demanded

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The law of demand describes an inverse relationship between the price of a good and the quantity demanded of that good. As price rises, quantity demanded falls.

18. A shift in the demand curve to the right indicates _______.
    A) a decrease in demand
    B) an increase in quantity demanded
    C) an increase in demand
    D) a decrease in quantity demanded

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A rightward shift of the entire demand curve signifies an increase in demand, meaning consumers are willing to buy more at every price level due to a change in a non-price determinant.

19. If the price of tea rises, the demand for coffee is likely to _______.
    A) increase, because they are substitutes
    B) decrease, because they are substitutes
    C) increase, because they are complements
    D) decrease, because they are complements

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Tea and coffee are substitute goods. An increase in the price of tea makes coffee relatively cheaper, so consumers will switch to coffee, increasing its demand.

20. A government-imposed price set below the equilibrium price is known as a _______.
    A) price floor
    B) price ceiling
    C) subsidy
    D) tax

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A price ceiling is a legally established maximum price. If it is set below the equilibrium price, it becomes binding and effective.

21. If the percentage change in quantity demanded is greater than the percentage change in price, demand is said to be _______.
    A) perfectly inelastic
    B) inelastic
    C) unit elastic
    D) elastic

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Elastic demand (PED > 1) occurs when the quantity demanded is highly responsive to price changes, meaning a given percentage change in price leads to a larger percentage change in quantity demanded.

22. A perfectly inelastic supply curve is represented by a _______ line.
    A) vertical
    B) horizontal
    C) downward sloping
    D) upward sloping

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A vertical supply curve (PES = 0) indicates that the quantity supplied does not change at all, regardless of changes in price (e.g., supply of rare paintings).

23. A binding price floor set above the equilibrium price will result in a _______.
    A) surplus
    B) shortage
    C) market equilibrium
    D) black market

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A price floor is a minimum price. If set above the equilibrium, quantity supplied will exceed quantity demanded, creating a surplus or excess supply.

24. A tax imposed by the government on producers will typically shift the _______.
    A) demand curve to the left
    B) supply curve to the left
    C) demand curve to the right
    D) supply curve to the right

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A tax increases the cost of production for sellers, making them willing to supply a smaller quantity at any given price. This is represented by a leftward shift of the supply curve.

25. A cross-price elasticity of demand (XED) of -2.5 indicates that the two goods are _______.
    A) substitutes
    B) inferior goods
    C) complements
    D) normal goods

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A negative XED signifies that the goods are complements. When the price of one good increases, the quantity demanded of the other good decreases because they are used together.

26. The difference between what consumers are willing to pay and what they actually pay is called _______.
    A) Producer surplus
    B) Deadweight loss
    C) Economic profit
    D) Consumer surplus

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Consumer surplus is the net benefit consumers receive, measured as the difference between their maximum willingness to pay and the market price they actually pay.

27. If an increase in consumer income leads to a decrease in demand for a good, that good is classified as a/an _______.
    A) normal good
    B) substitute good
    C) complementary good
    D) inferior good

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** An inferior good is one for which demand falls as consumer income rises because consumers switch to better quality substitutes.

28. The income effect and substitution effect both explain why the _______.
    A) supply curve is upward sloping
    B) demand curve is downward sloping
    C) supply curve is vertical
    D) demand curve is horizontal

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Both the income effect (change in purchasing power) and the substitution effect (change in relative prices) are reasons for the inverse relationship between price and quantity demanded, thus explaining the downward slope of the demand curve.

29. A government payment to producers to lower their production costs is known as a _______.
    A) tariff
    B) tax
    C) subsidy
    D) quota

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A subsidy is a payment made by the government to producers to encourage production, which effectively lowers their costs and shifts the supply curve to the right.

30. The loss of economic efficiency that results from a market distortion like a tax is called _______.
    A) consumer surplus
    B) producer surplus
    C) tax revenue
    D) deadweight loss

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Deadweight loss represents the reduction in total surplus (consumer + producer surplus) that occurs when a market is not operating at its efficient equilibrium, for example, due to a tax.

31. A firm's total revenue will increase if it raises its price and the demand for its product is _______.
    A) elastic
    B) inelastic
    C) unit elastic
    D) perfectly elastic

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** When demand is inelastic (PED < 1), the percentage increase in price is greater than the percentage decrease in quantity demanded, leading to an overall increase in total revenue (TR = P x Q).

32. The point where the demand curve and the supply curve intersect is known as _______.
    A) market surplus
    B) market disequilibrium
    C) market equilibrium
    D) market shortage

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Market equilibrium is the state where quantity demanded equals quantity supplied. This occurs graphically at the intersection of the demand and supply curves.

### **Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly (Questions 33-47)**

33. Which of the following is NOT a characteristic of a perfectly competitive market?
    A) Large number of buyers and sellers
    B) Homogeneous product
    C) Significant barriers to entry
    D) Perfect information

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A key characteristic of perfect competition is free entry and exit, meaning there are no significant barriers to entry. Significant barriers are a feature of monopoly and oligopoly.

34. A firm operating in a perfectly competitive market is described as a _______.
    A) price maker
    B) price leader
    C) price taker
    D) price discriminator

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A perfectly competitive firm is a price taker because it is too small relative to the market to influence the market price. It must accept the prevailing market price.

35. The demand curve faced by an individual firm in a perfectly competitive market is _______.
    A) a vertical line
    B) a horizontal line
    C) downward sloping
    D) upward sloping

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The demand curve for an individual perfectly competitive firm is perfectly elastic (a horizontal line) at the market price, meaning it can sell any quantity at that price but none above it.

36. The profit-maximizing rule for any firm, regardless of market structure, is to produce where _______.
    A) MR = ATC
    B) P = MC
    C) MR = MC
    D) TR = TC

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The condition for profit maximization (or loss minimization) is to produce at the output level where marginal revenue (MR) equals marginal cost (MC).

37. A key source of monopoly power is _______.
    A) a large number of firms
    B) free entry and exit
    C) the existence of close substitutes
    D) significant barriers to entry

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** A monopoly is sustained by high barriers to entry, which prevent new firms from entering the market and competing with the monopolist.

38. In the long run, a firm in perfect competition will earn _______.
    A) supernormal profits
    B) only normal profits
    C) persistent economic losses
    D) monopoly profits

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Due to free entry and exit, if firms are making supernormal profits, new firms will enter, driving down the price until only normal profits (zero economic profit) are earned in the long run.

39. For a monopolist, the marginal revenue (MR) curve _______.
    A) is the same as the demand curve
    B) lies above the demand curve
    C) is a horizontal line
    D) lies below the demand curve

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Because a monopolist must lower its price to sell more units, its marginal revenue from an additional unit is less than the price. Therefore, the MR curve lies below the demand (AR) curve.

40. When P = MC in a market, it signifies _______.
    A) productive efficiency
    B) allocative efficiency
    C) X-inefficiency
    D) economic loss

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Allocative efficiency occurs when resources are allocated such that the price consumers are willing to pay (P, representing marginal benefit) equals the marginal cost of production (MC).

41. A firm should shut down in the short run if the price is less than its _______.
    A) Average Total Cost (ATC)
    B) Marginal Cost (MC)
    C) Average Fixed Cost (AFC)
    D) Average Variable Cost (AVC)

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** If the price (AR) falls below the average variable cost (AVC), the firm cannot even cover its variable costs of production and will minimize its losses by shutting down immediately.

42. A natural monopoly exists when _______.
    A) a firm controls an essential natural resource
    B) the government grants a patent
    C) a single firm can supply the entire market at a lower average cost than multiple firms
    D) a firm engages in predatory pricing

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A natural monopoly arises due to significant economies of scale, where the long-run average cost curve declines over the entire range of market demand, making it more efficient for one firm to serve the market.

43. Compared to a perfectly competitive market, a monopoly typically produces _______.
    A) a higher output and charges a lower price
    B) a lower output and charges a higher price
    C) the same output and charges the same price
    D) a lower output and charges a lower price

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A monopolist maximizes profit by restricting output (to where MR=MC) and charging a higher price than would prevail in a competitive market, leading to deadweight loss.

44. Which of the following is a legal barrier to entry?
    A) Economies of scale
    B) High start-up costs
    C) A patent
    D) Brand loyalty

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A patent is a government-granted exclusive right to an inventor, which legally prevents other firms from producing and selling the invention for a certain period.

45. The deadweight loss associated with monopoly is a result of _______.
    A) P > MC
    B) P = MC
    C) P < MC
    D) P = min ATC

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Monopoly results in allocative inefficiency because the price charged (P) is greater than the marginal cost of production (MC), meaning society values additional units more than it costs to produce them, but the monopolist restricts output.

46. In long-run equilibrium, a perfectly competitive firm achieves productive efficiency because it produces at the point where _______.
    A) Price equals Marginal Cost
    B) Price is greater than Average Total Cost
    C) Average Revenue equals Marginal Revenue
    D) Price equals minimum Average Total Cost

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Productive efficiency means producing at the lowest possible average cost. In long-run equilibrium, a competitive firm is forced to operate at the minimum point of its ATC curve (P = min ATC).

47. When a monopolist is able to charge different prices to different customers for the same product, it is known as _______.
    A) predatory pricing
    B) price discrimination
    C) limit pricing
    D) price leadership

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Price discrimination is the practice of selling the same good to different buyers at different prices, where the price differences are not based on cost differences.

### **Chapter 4: Monopolistic Competition and Oligopoly (Questions 48-62)**

48. The key distinguishing feature of monopolistic competition is _______.
    A) a single seller
    B) significant barriers to entry
    C) product differentiation
    D) a standardized product

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Product differentiation, where firms sell products that are close but not perfect substitutes, is the hallmark of monopolistic competition.

49. Which market structure is characterized by the strategic interdependence of firms?
    A) Monopoly
    B) Perfect Competition
    C) Monopolistic Competition
    D) Oligopoly

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Interdependence is the crucial feature of oligopoly, where each firm's decisions on price and output directly affect its few rivals, who are likely to react.

50. In the long run, a monopolistically competitive firm earns _______.
    A) only normal profit
    B) supernormal profit
    C) economic losses
    D) cartel profits

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Similar to perfect competition, free entry in monopolistic competition ensures that any short-run supernormal profits are competed away by new entrants, leading to only normal profits in the long run.

51. An agreement among oligopolistic firms to fix prices and limit output is known as _______.
    A) competition
    B) a merger
    C) collusion
    D) differentiation

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Collusion is an agreement among firms to restrict competition. A formal collusive agreement is called a cartel.

52. The kinked demand curve model is used to explain _______.
    A) profit maximization in monopoly
    B) price rigidity in oligopoly
    C) long-run equilibrium in perfect competition
    D) consumer choice

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The kinked demand curve model suggests that oligopolistic firms are reluctant to change prices due to assumptions about rivals' reactions (they will match price cuts but not price increases), leading to price stability or rigidity.

53. An example of non-price competition is _______.
    A) a price war
    B) reducing the price of a good
    C) advertising and branding
    D) offering a volume discount

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Non-price competition involves competing on factors other than price, such as advertising, branding, quality, and customer service. The others are forms of price competition.

54. The Prisoner's Dilemma in game theory illustrates that _______.
    A) cooperation is always the best individual strategy
    B) firms always choose to cooperate
    C) individual rationality can lead to a collectively worse outcome
    D) cheating on agreements is never profitable

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The Prisoner's Dilemma shows a situation where players, acting in their own self-interest, choose a dominant strategy that results in a less optimal outcome for all than if they had cooperated.

55. In long-run equilibrium, a monopolistically competitive firm operates with _______.
    A) excess capacity
    B) maximum efficiency
    C) a perfectly elastic demand curve
    D) zero fixed costs

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** In the long run, a monopolistically competitive firm produces at an output level to the left of the minimum point of its ATC curve. This means it has excess capacity—it could produce more at a lower average cost.

56. The Cournot model of oligopoly assumes that firms compete by choosing their _______ simultaneously.
    A) price levels
    B) advertising budgets
    C) output levels
    D) product quality

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The Cournot model is a model of quantity competition where each firm decides its output level, assuming the output level of its rival is fixed.

57. A formal organization of firms that agree to coordinate their pricing and production decisions is called a _______.
    A) monopoly
    B) trust
    C) cooperative
    D) cartel

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** A cartel, such as OPEC, is a formal agreement among firms to act as a single monopolist to maximize joint profits by controlling price and output.

58. In the long run, which condition is true for monopolistic competition but NOT for perfect competition?
    A) MR = MC
    B) P = ATC
    C) P > MC
    D) P = MR

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Both market structures have MR=MC and P=ATC in the long run. However, due to the downward-sloping demand curve in monopolistic competition, Price (P) remains greater than Marginal Cost (MC), indicating allocative inefficiency. In perfect competition, P=MC.

59. Setting a price below the profit-maximizing level to discourage new firms from entering is known as _______.
    A) limit pricing
    B) price discrimination
    C) collusion
    D) price war

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Limit pricing is a strategic barrier to entry where an existing firm sets a low price to make entry seem unattractive or unprofitable for potential competitors.

60. OPEC (Organization of the Petroleum Exporting Countries) is a prominent example of a/an _______.
    A) perfectly competitive market
    B) international cartel
    C) multinational corporation
    D) free trade area

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** OPEC is a group of oil-exporting nations that coordinate their oil production policies to influence the global price of oil, fitting the definition of an international cartel.

61. The Bertrand model of oligopoly, with homogenous products and similar costs, predicts that the market price will be driven down to _______.
    A) monopoly level
    B) average total cost
    C) marginal cost
    D) zero

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The Bertrand model of price competition suggests that firms will continuously undercut each other's prices until the price equals marginal cost, the same outcome as in perfect competition.

62. Product differentiation gives a firm in monopolistic competition a _______.
    A) perfectly inelastic demand curve
    B) vertical supply curve
    C) small degree of monopoly power
    D) guarantee of long-run profits

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** By differentiating its product, a firm creates a unique version for which it is the sole producer, giving it a mini-monopoly and a downward-sloping demand curve, which allows for some control over price.

### **Chapter 5: Consumer Theory (Questions 63-75)**

63. The law of diminishing marginal utility states that as more units of a good are consumed, the _______.
    A) total utility eventually falls
    B) total utility always remains constant
    C) additional satisfaction from each extra unit eventually falls
    D) marginal utility always remains positive

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** This law posits that the marginal utility (additional satisfaction) derived from consuming successive units of a good will eventually decrease.

64. Total utility is at its maximum when marginal utility is _______.
    A) zero
    B) one
    C) positive and rising
    D) negative

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** When an additional unit provides zero extra satisfaction (MU=0), total satisfaction has reached its peak (satiation point). If MU becomes negative, TU starts to fall.

65. An indifference curve shows all combinations of two goods that _______.
    A) a consumer can buy with their income
    B) have the same price
    C) yield the same level of satisfaction to the consumer
    D) have zero marginal utility

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** An indifference curve connects various bundles of two goods among which the consumer is indifferent, meaning they all provide the same level of total utility.

66. The slope of a consumer's budget line is determined by the _______.
    A) consumer's income
    B) consumer's preferences
    C) ratio of the prices of the two goods
    D) marginal rate of substitution

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The slope of the budget line is given by -(P_x / P_y), which represents the rate at which the market allows the consumer to trade one good for the other.

6-7. The convexity of an indifference curve to the origin is due to _______.
    A) diminishing marginal utility
    B) diminishing marginal rate of substitution
    C) increasing marginal returns
    D) the psychological law of consumption

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The convex shape reflects the diminishing marginal rate of substitution (MRS). As a consumer has more of good X and less of good Y, they are willing to give up fewer units of Y to get one more unit of X.

68. Consumer equilibrium is achieved at the point where the budget line is _______.
    A) above the highest possible indifference curve
    B) intersecting the indifference curve at two points
    C) tangent to the highest attainable indifference curve
    D) below the lowest indifference curve

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A consumer maximizes utility by reaching the highest indifference curve possible given their budget constraint. This occurs at the point of tangency, where the slope of the budget line (price ratio) equals the slope of the indifference curve (MRS).

69. The change in quantity demanded of a good due solely to a change in its relative price is known as the _______.
    A) price effect
    B) income effect
    C) substitution effect
    D) preference effect

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The substitution effect isolates the change in consumption that results from a good becoming relatively cheaper or more expensive compared to other goods, holding real income constant.

70. For a normal good, the income effect of a price fall is _______.
    A) positive
    B) negative
    C) zero
    D) uncertain

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A fall in price increases real income. For a normal good, an increase in income leads to an increase in quantity demanded, so the income effect is positive (reinforces the substitution effect).

71. A curve showing the relationship between a consumer's income and the quantity of a good they demand is called a/an _______.
    A) Indifference curve
    B) Engel curve
    C) Demand curve
    D) Budget line

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** An Engel curve specifically illustrates how the quantity consumed of a good changes as a consumer's income changes, ceteris paribus.

72. Indifference curves cannot intersect because it would violate the assumption of _______.
    A) diminishing MRS
    B) transitivity of preferences
    C) cardinal utility
    D) a fixed budget

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** If two indifference curves representing different utility levels were to intersect, it would imply that the consumer is simultaneously indifferent to a bundle on the lower curve and prefers a bundle on the higher curve, which is a logical contradiction and violates the assumption of transitivity.

73. The price consumption curve (PCC) can be used to derive a consumer's _______.
    A) supply curve
    B) Engel curve
    C) demand curve
    D) production function

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The price consumption curve traces the utility-maximizing bundles as the price of one good changes. By plotting these price-quantity combinations, one can derive the individual's demand curve for that good.

74. If a good is a Giffen good, its demand curve will be _______.
    A) vertical
    B) horizontal
    C) downward sloping
    D) upward sloping

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** A Giffen good is a rare type of inferior good where the negative income effect of a price change is so strong that it outweighs the substitution effect, leading to the paradoxical result that a decrease in price causes a decrease in quantity demanded.

75. The revealed preference theory attempts to understand consumer preferences based on their _______.
    A) stated intentions
    B) psychological profiles
    C) observed purchasing behavior
    D) marginal utility schedules

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The revealed preference theory, developed by Samuelson, infers preferences from actual choices consumers make in the market, rather than from unobservable concepts like utility.

### **Chapter 6: Producer Theory (Questions 76-90)**

76. In the short run, at least one factor of production is considered to be _______.
    A) free
    B) variable
    C) fixed
    D) abundant

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The short run in production is defined as a period where at least one input (typically capital like plant and machinery) is fixed and cannot be changed.

77. Costs that do not vary with the level of output are called _______.
    A) variable costs
    B) total costs
    C) marginal costs
    D) fixed costs

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Fixed costs, such as rent and insurance, remain constant regardless of the quantity of output produced in the short run.

78. The Marginal Cost (MC) curve intersects the Average Total Cost (ATC) curve at the _______.
    A) maximum point of the ATC curve
    B) minimum point of the ATC curve
    C) maximum point of the MC curve
    D) minimum point of the MC curve

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A key relationship between cost curves is that the MC curve must intersect both the AVC and ATC curves at their respective minimum points.

79. The shape of the short-run average cost curve is determined by the law of _______.
    A) diminishing marginal utility
    B) demand and supply
    C) diminishing returns
    D) comparative advantage

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The U-shape of the SRAC (and AVC, MC) curves is due to the law of diminishing marginal returns. Initially, as variable factors are added, returns increase and costs per unit fall, but eventually diminishing returns set in, causing costs per unit to rise.

80. A firm breaks even when its Total Revenue (TR) equals its _______.
    A) Total Fixed Cost (TFC)
    B) Total Variable Cost (TVC)
    C) Total Cost (TC)
    D) Marginal Cost (MC)

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The break-even point is the level of output where total revenue exactly covers total costs (TR = TC), resulting in zero economic profit.

81. A firm's Average Revenue (AR) curve is the same as its _______ curve.
    A) supply
    B) demand
    C) total cost
    D) marginal cost

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Average Revenue (AR = TR/Q = (P x Q)/Q = P) is the price per unit. The demand curve shows the price consumers are willing to pay for each quantity, so the AR curve is identical to the demand curve.

82. In long-run production, a firm experiences diseconomies of scale when its long-run average cost _______.
    A) rises as output increases
    B) falls as output increases
    C) remains constant as output increases
    D) equals marginal cost

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Diseconomies of scale occur when a firm grows too large, leading to managerial and coordination problems that cause long-run average costs to rise as output expands further.

83. Any profit earned by a firm in excess of normal profit is called _______.
    A) accounting profit
    B) break-even profit
    C) supernormal profit
    D) implicit profit

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Supernormal profit, or economic profit, is the profit earned above and beyond what is necessary to keep the firm in business (i.e., above the opportunity cost of all resources, which is normal profit).

84. A technical relationship showing the maximum output that can be produced from a given combination of inputs is called a/an _______.
    A) isoquant
    B) production function
    C) isocost line
    D) cost curve

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A production function, mathematically expressed as Q = f(L, K, ...), describes the technological relationship between inputs and the maximum possible output.

85. A curve showing all combinations of two inputs that produce the same level of output is called a/an _______.
    A) isocost line
    B) production possibility curve
    C) indifference curve
    D) isoquant

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** An isoquant (or equal product curve) is the producer's equivalent of an indifference curve, showing various combinations of, for example, labor and capital that can produce a constant quantity of output.

86. A firm's explicit costs would include all of the following EXCEPT _______.
    A) wages paid to workers
    B) the salary the owner could have earned working elsewhere
    C) rent paid for the factory
    D) cost of raw materials

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The salary the owner forgoes by running their own business is an implicit cost (an opportunity cost), not an explicit, out-of-pocket payment. The others are all explicit costs.

87. Specialization and division of labour are key reasons for _______.
    A) diseconomies of scale
    B) economies of scale
    C) diminishing returns
    D) increasing opportunity cost

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Technical economies of scale arise as a firm grows larger, allowing it to use greater specialization and division of labour, which increases efficiency and lowers long-run average costs.

88. A producer achieves the least-cost combination of inputs when the isocost line is _______.
    A) tangent to the isoquant
    B) above the isoquant
    C) below the isoquant
    D) parallel to the isoquant

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The optimal, least-cost input mix to produce a given output occurs at the point of tangency between the relevant isoquant and the lowest possible isocost line.

89. Normal profit is considered a/an _______.
    A) explicit cost
    B) supernormal profit
    C) implicit cost
    D) accounting loss

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Economists treat normal profit as an implicit cost because it represents the opportunity cost of the entrepreneur's own resources (time and capital). It's the minimum return needed to keep them in the business.

90. In a Cobb-Douglas production function Q = A * L^α * K^β, if α + β > 1, the firm exhibits _______.
    A) decreasing returns to scale
    B) constant returns to scale
    C) increasing returns to scale
    D) diminishing marginal returns

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The sum of the output elasticities (α + β) indicates the returns to scale. If the sum is greater than 1, it means that doubling all inputs will more than double the output, which is increasing returns to scale.

### **Chapter 7: National Income Accounting (Questions 91-105)**

91. Gross Domestic Product (GDP) is the total market value of all _______ goods and services produced within a country in a year.
    A) intermediate
    B) primary
    C) final
    D) second-hand

    **Answer: C**
