    **Explanation:** GDP only includes final goods and services to avoid double-counting the value of intermediate goods that are used up in the production process.

92. The formula for the expenditure approach to calculating GDP is _______.
    A) Y = C + I + G - (X-M)
    B) Y = C + S + G + (X-M)
    C) Y = C + I + G + (X-M)
    D) Y = C + I + T + (X-M)

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** GDP (Y) is the sum of Consumption (C), Investment (I), Government Spending (G), and Net Exports (Exports (X) - Imports (M)).

93. The difference between GNP and NNP is _______.
    A) net factor income from abroad
    B) indirect business taxes
    C) depreciation
    D) subsidies

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Net National Product (NNP) is calculated by subtracting depreciation (also known as capital consumption allowance) from Gross National Product (GNP) to account for the capital used up in production.

94. The income that households have available for spending or saving after paying personal taxes is known as _______.
    A) Personal Income
    B) National Income
    C) Disposable Personal Income
    D) Gross Domestic Product

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Disposable Personal Income (DI) is the income remaining after direct personal taxes are paid, which is then allocated between consumption and saving.

95. To measure real economic growth, it is best to use _______.
    A) Nominal GDP
    B) Real GDP
    C) GNP per capita
    D) Disposable Income

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Real GDP is adjusted for inflation (measured using constant base-year prices), so it reflects the actual change in the physical volume of output, which is a true measure of economic growth.

96. Which of the following would be included in the calculation of Ghana's GDP?
    A) The sale of a used car
    B) The value of work done by a stay-at-home parent
    C) The value of computers produced by a foreign-owned factory in Accra
    D) The profits earned by a Ghanaian company operating in Nigeria

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** GDP measures production within a country's geographical borders. The computers are produced within Ghana, so they are part of Ghana's GDP, regardless of the factory's foreign ownership. The Ghanaian company's profit in Nigeria would be part of Ghana's GNP.

97. If Nominal GDP is GHS 500 billion and Real GDP is GHS 400 billion, the GDP deflator is _______.
    A) 80
    B) 125
    C) 100
    D) 25

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** GDP Deflator = (Nominal GDP / Real GDP) x 100 = (500 / 400) x 100 = 1.25 x 100 = 125.

98. A major limitation of GDP as a measure of welfare is that it excludes _______.
    A) government spending
    B) non-market transactions
    C) exports of goods
    D) investment by firms

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** GDP does not account for the value of non-market activities like household production (housework, childcare) and volunteer work, even though these activities contribute significantly to human welfare.

99. In the income approach to measuring GDP, which of the following is NOT included?
    A) Compensation of employees
    B) Transfer payments
    C) Rental income
    D) Profits of companies

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Transfer payments, such as pensions or unemployment benefits, are excluded from the income approach because they are not payments for current production of goods and services.

100. If a country's nationals earn more abroad than foreigners earn within the country, then _______.
    A) GDP = GNP
    B) GDP > GNP
    C) GDP < GNP
    D) NNP > GNP

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** If Net Factor Income from Abroad (NFIA) is positive, then GNP (which equals GDP + NFIA) will be greater than GDP.

101. The Human Development Index (HDI) measures all the following EXCEPT _______.
    A) life expectancy at birth
    B) income inequality
    C) mean years of schooling
    D) gross national income per capita

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The HDI is a composite index of three dimensions: a long and healthy life (life expectancy), knowledge (education levels), and a decent standard of living (GNI per capita). It does not directly incorporate a measure of income inequality.

102. The sum of value added at each stage of production is equal to the _______.
    A) total profit of all firms
    B) total cost of intermediate goods
    C) value of the final good
    D) total wages paid in the economy

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The value-added approach avoids double counting by summing the value added at each stage, which precisely equals the market value of the final good or service.

103. Which price index measures the average change in prices of a fixed basket of consumer goods?
    A) Producer Price Index (PPI)
    B) GDP Deflator
    C) Wholesale Price Index (WPI)
    D) Consumer Price Index (CPI)

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The CPI is specifically designed to track the cost of living for a typical household by measuring price changes in a representative basket of consumer goods and services.

104. Subtracting undistributed corporate profits and corporate taxes from National Income is a step towards calculating _______.
    A) Gross National Product
    B) Disposable Personal Income
    C) Personal Income
    D) Net National Product

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** To get from National Income (income earned) to Personal Income (income received by households), one must subtract incomes that are earned but not received, such as retained profits and corporate taxes.

105. GDP measured at current market prices is known as _______.
    A) Real GDP
    B) Potential GDP
    C) Nominal GDP
    D) Green GDP

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Nominal GDP is calculated using the prices prevailing in the year of production (current prices) and is therefore not adjusted for inflation.

### **Chapter 8: Money, Banking, and Fiscal Policy (Questions 106-121)**

106. The primary function of money is to serve as a _______.
    A) store of value
    B) unit of account
    C) medium of exchange
    D) standard of deferred payment

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The most important function of money is its role as a medium of exchange, which eliminates the need for a double coincidence of wants inherent in a barter system.

107. Money that is not backed by any physical commodity and is legal tender by government decree is known as _______.
    A) Commodity money
    B) Fiat money
    C) Full-bodied money
    D) Representative money

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Fiat money, which includes most modern currencies, derives its value from government backing and public acceptance, not from its intrinsic worth or convertibility to a commodity like gold.

108. Which measure of money supply is the narrowest and includes currency in circulation and demand deposits?
    A) M3
    B) M2
    C) M1
    D) M0

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** M1 (Narrow Money) represents the most liquid forms of money readily available for transactions, typically defined as currency with the public plus checkable demand deposits.

109. To reduce the money supply, the Central Bank would _______.
    A) sell government securities in the open market
    B) buy government securities in the open market
    C) decrease the discount rate
    D) decrease the reserve requirement

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** By selling government securities through Open Market Operations (OMO), the central bank receives payment from commercial banks, which reduces their reserves and thus their ability to lend, contracting the money supply.

110. The interest rate at which commercial banks can borrow from the Central Bank is called the _______.
    A) prime rate
    B) interbank rate
    C) market rate
    D) discount rate

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The discount rate (or bank rate/policy rate) is the rate charged by the central bank for direct loans to commercial banks, serving as a key tool of monetary policy.

111. The ability of commercial banks to increase the money supply through lending is known as _______.
    A) credit control
    B) credit creation
    C) monetary policy
    D) open market operations

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Credit creation is the process by which commercial banks create new deposits (money) by making loans from their excess reserves.

112. If the required reserve ratio is 20%, the maximum credit multiplier is _______.
    A) 4
    B) 5
    C) 10
    D) 20

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The credit multiplier is calculated as 1 / RRR. So, 1 / 0.20 = 5.

113. An increase in personal income tax is an example of a/an _______.
    A) expansionary monetary policy
    B) contractionary monetary policy
    C) expansionary fiscal policy
    D) contractionary fiscal policy

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Fiscal policy involves government spending and taxation. Increasing taxes reduces disposable income and aggregate demand, which is a contractionary fiscal measure aimed at curbing inflation.

114. A budget deficit occurs when government expenditure is _______ government revenue.
    A) equal to
    B) less than
    C) greater than
    D) a multiple of

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A budget deficit is a situation where total government spending exceeds its total revenue (excluding borrowing) in a fiscal year.

115. A tax where the tax rate increases as the taxpayer's income increases is called a _______.
    A) regressive tax
    B) progressive tax
    C) proportional tax
    D) direct tax

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A progressive tax system is designed to take a larger percentage of income from high-income earners than from low-income earners, based on the ability-to-pay principle.

116. A loan that has been overdue for more than 90 days is typically classified as a _______.
    A) bad debt
    B) performing asset
    C) non-performing asset
    D) long-term loan

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A non-performing asset (NPA) or non-performing loan (NPL) is a loan for which principal or interest payments have remained overdue for a specified period, usually 90 days.

117. Which of the following is an example of capital expenditure by the government?
    A) Payment of salaries to civil servants
    B) Payment of interest on public debt
    C) Construction of a new highway
    D) Subsidies given to farmers

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Capital expenditure results in the creation of long-term assets. Building a highway is an investment in infrastructure, which is a capital expenditure. The others are recurrent expenditures.

1T18. The total accumulated amount of money owed by the government is known as _______.
    A) budget deficit
    B) fiscal deficit
    C) public debt
    D) foreign debt

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Public debt is the total stock of outstanding government borrowing resulting from past budget deficits.

119. The repo rate is the rate at which the central bank _______.
    A) borrows from commercial banks
    B) lends to the government
    C) lends to commercial banks against securities
    D) sets the minimum wage

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The repo rate is the interest rate for short-term loans where the central bank lends money to commercial banks, accepting government securities as collateral.

120. Which of the following is a direct tax?
    A) Value Added Tax (VAT)
    B) Customs Duty
    C) Corporate Income Tax
    D) Excise Duty

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A direct tax is levied on the income or wealth of a person or company. Corporate Income Tax is levied directly on a company's profits. The others are indirect taxes on goods and services.

121. Moral suasion as a tool of monetary policy involves the central bank _______.
    A) selling securities
    B) setting reserve ratios
    C) changing interest rates
    D) persuading banks to follow certain policies

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Moral suasion is a qualitative credit control tool where the central bank uses persuasion, advice, and informal requests to convince commercial banks to adopt specific lending practices aligned with its policy goals.

### **Chapter 9: Classical and Keynesian Models (Questions 122-135)**

122. Say's Law of Markets is often summarized as _______.
    A) Demand creates its own supply
    B) Supply creates its own demand
    C) Government should manage demand
    D) Saving is always bad for the economy

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Say's Law suggests that the act of production generates enough income to purchase all the output produced, meaning a general glut of goods is impossible in the long run.

123. The classical economic model assumes that wages and prices are _______.
    A) flexible
    B) sticky
    C) fixed by the government
    D) irrelevant

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A core tenet of classical economics is that prices and wages are fully flexible and will adjust quickly to clear markets, ensuring the economy returns to full employment.

124. The Keynesian model emphasizes the role of _______ in determining the level of output and employment.
    A) aggregate supply
    B) technology
    C) aggregate demand
    D) flexible wages

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** John Maynard Keynes argued that in the short run, the level of economic activity is driven by the level of aggregate demand (C + I + G + (X-M)).

125. The marginal propensity to consume (MPC) is the _______.
    A) total consumption divided by total income
    B) fraction of total income that is consumed
    C) fraction of additional income that is saved
    D) fraction of additional income that is spent on consumption

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The MPC (ΔC / ΔYd) measures how much consumption changes for a given change in disposable income.

126. If the MPC is 0.75, the simple Keynesian multiplier is _______.
    A) 4
    B) 1.33
    C) 0.25
    D) 7.5

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The multiplier (k) = 1 / (1 - MPC). So, k = 1 / (1 - 0.75) = 1 / 0.25 = 4.

127. The crowding out effect suggests that an increase in government borrowing to finance spending may lead to _______.
    A) an increase in private investment
    B) a decrease in interest rates
    C) a decrease in private investment
    D) a decrease in the price level

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Crowding out occurs when government borrowing increases demand for loanable funds, pushing up interest rates and making it more expensive for private firms to borrow, thus reducing private investment.

128. In the AD/AS model, the long-run aggregate supply (LRAS) curve is _______.
    A) horizontal
    B) upward sloping
    C) downward sloping
    D) vertical

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The LRAS curve is vertical at the level of potential output (full-employment output), indicating that in the long run, output is determined by the economy's productive capacity, not by the price level.

129. A recessionary gap occurs when short-run equilibrium output is _______ potential output.
    A) greater than
    B) equal to
    C) less than
    D) fluctuating around

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A recessionary gap exists when the economy is producing below its full potential (Y_e < Y_p), resulting in unemployment above the natural rate.

130. The Classical Dichotomy is the theoretical separation of _______.
    A) demand and supply variables
    B) real and nominal variables
    C) domestic and foreign variables
    D) short-run and long-run variables

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The Classical Dichotomy posits that real variables (like output, employment) are determined by real factors, while nominal variables (like the price level) are determined by the money supply. This implies money neutrality in the long run.

131. In the Keynesian consumption function C = a + bYd, the term 'a' represents _______.
    A) marginal consumption
    B) induced consumption
    C) disposable income
    D) autonomous consumption

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** 'a' represents autonomous consumption, which is the level of consumption that occurs even when disposable income is zero, financed out of savings or borrowing.

132. The short-run aggregate supply (SRAS) curve is upward sloping because _______.
    A) some input prices, like wages, are sticky in the short run
    B) all prices are flexible in the short run
    C) technology improves quickly
    D) potential output increases with the price level

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The SRAS curve slopes up because with sticky input prices (especially nominal wages), a higher overall price level increases firms' profitability, incentivizing them to produce more output.

133. If MPC + MPS = 1, and the MPS is 0.4, then the MPC is _______.
    A) 1.4
    B) 0.6
    C) 2.5
    D) 0.4

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Since any additional dollar of disposable income is either consumed (MPC) or saved (MPS), the two must sum to 1. If MPS is 0.4, then MPC must be 1 - 0.4 = 0.6.

134. Keynes challenged the classical view by arguing that an economy could get stuck in _______.
    A) a liquidity trap
    B) an inflationary spiral
    C) an underemployment equilibrium
    D) a state of hyperinflation

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A key contribution of Keynes was to show that, due to insufficient aggregate demand and sticky wages, an economy could reach a short-run equilibrium with high levels of unemployment, with no automatic tendency to return to full employment.

135. A rightward shift in the aggregate demand curve can be caused by _______.
    A) an increase in taxes
    B) a decrease in government spending
    C) an increase in investment spending
    D) an increase in imports

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** An increase in any of the components of AD (C, I, G, or X-M) will shift the AD curve to the right. An increase in investment spending (I) is a direct increase in aggregate demand.

### **Chapter 10: Inflation, Unemployment, and Business Cycles (Questions 136-151)**

136. Inflation is best defined as a sustained increase in the _______.
    A) price of a single good
    B) general price level
    C) wages of workers
    D) money supply

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Inflation refers to a persistent rise in the average level of prices of all goods and services in an economy, not just the price of one item.

137. Inflation caused by "too much money chasing too few goods" is known as _______ inflation.
    A) cost-push
    B) demand-pull
    C) built-in
    D) hyperinflation

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Demand-pull inflation occurs when aggregate demand exceeds the economy's ability to produce goods and services, bidding up the overall price level.

138. The combination of rising inflation and falling output is known as _______.
    A) recession
    B) deflation
    C) depression
    D) stagflation

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Stagflation, a portmanteau of stagnation and inflation, describes the situation where an economy experiences both high unemployment (stagnant/falling output) and high inflation, typically caused by a negative supply shock.

139. The short-run Phillips curve shows a trade-off between _______ and _______.
    A) economic growth; unemployment
    B) inflation; unemployment
    C) economic growth; inflation
    D) interest rates; unemployment

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The original Phillips curve illustrated an inverse relationship between the rate of inflation and the rate of unemployment in the short run.

140. Unemployment caused by a downturn in the business cycle is called _______ unemployment.
    A) frictional
    B) structural
    C) cyclical
    D) seasonal

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Cyclical unemployment is directly related to the phases of the business cycle. It rises during recessions (contractions) due to a deficiency in aggregate demand and falls during expansions.

141. The Natural Rate of Unemployment (NRU) is the sum of _______.
    A) cyclical and frictional unemployment
    B) structural and cyclical unemployment
    C) frictional and structural unemployment
    D) seasonal and cyclical unemployment

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The NRU represents the level of unemployment that persists even when the economy is at its potential output. It includes inevitable frictional (job search) and structural (skills mismatch) unemployment, but excludes cyclical unemployment.

142. A continuous and upward-moving wage-price spiral is a feature of _______ inflation.
    A) cost-push
    B) demand-pull
    C) hyperinflation
    D) built-in

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Built-in (or inertial) inflation is driven by expectations. Workers expect prices to rise and demand higher wages, and firms raise prices to cover these wages, creating a self-perpetuating spiral.

143. In the long run, the Phillips curve is believed to be _______.
    A) horizontal at the inflation rate
    B) downward sloping
    C) upward sloping
    D) vertical at the natural rate of unemployment

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The modern consensus is that there is no long-run trade-off. Attempts to keep unemployment below the NRU only lead to accelerating inflation, making the Long-Run Phillips Curve (LRPC) vertical at the NRU.

144. The peak of a business cycle is followed by a period of _______.
    A) expansion
    B) trough
    C) contraction
    D) recovery

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The phases of a business cycle are expansion, peak, contraction, and trough. The contraction (recession) phase begins after the cycle reaches its peak.

145. A government's use of increased spending and tax cuts to combat a recession is an example of a/an _______.
    A) automatic stabilizer
    B) stabilization policy
    C) supply-side policy
    D) monetary policy

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** This is a discretionary fiscal policy aimed at stabilizing the economy (specifically, an expansionary fiscal policy). An automatic stabilizer would be a feature that works without new government action.

146. A recent graduate looking for their first job is an example of _______ unemployment.
    A) cyclical
    B) seasonal
    C) structural
    D) frictional

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Frictional unemployment includes people who are temporarily between jobs or are entering the labor force for the first time, like new graduates. It is a natural part of a dynamic labor market.

147. A stock market index is typically considered a _______ economic indicator.
    A) lagging
    B) leading
    C) coincident
    D) random

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Leading indicators are variables that tend to change before the overall economy changes. Stock prices often rise or fall in anticipation of future economic performance.

148. Extremely rapid and out-of-control inflation is known as _______.
    A) disinflation
    B) creeping inflation
    C) stagflation
    D) hyperinflation

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Hyperinflation is defined as a very high, accelerating rate of inflation (e.g., over 50% per month) that quickly erodes the value of money.

149. The Non-Accelerating Inflation Rate of Unemployment (NAIRU) is the unemployment rate at which inflation is _______.
    A) zero
    B) accelerating
    C) decelerating
    D) stable

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** NAIRU is the specific level of unemployment consistent with a stable rate of inflation. If unemployment falls below NAIRU, inflation tends to accelerate.

150. A negative supply shock, such as a sharp rise in oil prices, can lead to _______.
    A) cost-push inflation
    B) demand-pull inflation
    C) a fall in unemployment
    D) a rightward shift of the AD curve

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A sharp rise in the price of a key input like oil increases production costs for many firms, causing the SRAS curve to shift left. This results in cost-push inflation.

151. An automatic stabilizer is a fiscal policy tool that _______.
    A) requires new government action to take effect
    B) acts to increase the severity of business cycles
    C) acts to reduce the severity of business cycles without new legislation
    D) is controlled by the central bank

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Automatic stabilizers, such as progressive income taxes and unemployment benefits, are built-in features of the fiscal system that automatically cushion the economy against shocks.

### **Chapter 11: International Trade Theory (Questions 152-166)**

152. A country has an absolute advantage in producing a good if it _______.
    A) has a lower opportunity cost
    B) can produce it using fewer resources than another country
    C) specializes in producing that good
    D) has a high tariff on that good

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Absolute advantage, a concept by Adam Smith, refers to being more productive or efficient (using fewer inputs) in producing a good compared to another country.

153. The principle of comparative advantage was developed by _______.
    A) Adam Smith
    B) John Maynard Keynes
    C) David Ricardo
    D) Alfred Marshall

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** David Ricardo formulated the theory of comparative advantage, showing that trade can be mutually beneficial even if one country has an absolute advantage in all goods, as long as opportunity costs differ.

154. According to the Heckscher-Ohlin model, a country will export goods that are intensive in its _______ factor.
    A) relatively scarce
    B) relatively abundant
    C) most expensive
    D) least productive

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The Heckscher-Ohlin (Factor Proportions) theory states that a country has a comparative advantage in and will export goods whose production uses a large amount of the factor of production (e.g., labor or capital) that the country possesses in abundance.

155. A tax imposed on imported goods is known as a _______.
    A) quota
    B) subsidy
    C) tariff
    D) non-tariff barrier

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A tariff is a customs duty or tax levied on goods as they cross national borders, usually on imports.

156. The argument that new domestic industries should be protected from foreign competition until they are mature is called the _______ argument.
    A) national security
    B) cheap foreign labor
    C) anti-dumping
    D) infant industry

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The infant industry argument is a common justification for protectionism, suggesting that temporary trade barriers can help new industries develop the scale and experience needed to become competitive.

157. An agreement between member countries to remove all trade barriers among themselves and adopt a common external tariff is a _______.
    A) Free Trade Area
    B) Customs Union
    C) Common Market
    D) Preferential Trading Area

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A customs union involves both free trade among members (like a free trade area) and a common external tariff against non-members.

158. ECOWAS is an example of a regional trade bloc in _______.
    A) Southeast Asia
    B) North America
    C) Europe
    D) West Africa

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** ECOWAS stands for the Economic Community of West African States and includes 15 countries from the region.

159. When a country's export prices rise relative to its import prices, its terms of trade have _______.
    A) improved
    B) deteriorated
    C) remained constant
    D) become negative

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** An improvement in the terms of trade (ToT index rises) means the country can buy more imports for a given quantity of exports, which is beneficial for its welfare.

160. A quantitative limit on the amount of a good that can be imported is called a _______.
    A) tariff
    B) quota
    C) voluntary export restraint
    D) subsidy

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** An import quota is a direct physical restriction on the quantity of a specific good that can be brought into a country during a certain period.

161. One of the main gains from international trade is that it allows countries to _______.
    A) become completely self-sufficient
    B) consume beyond their production possibility frontiers
    C) increase their tariff revenues
    D) eliminate unemployment completely

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Through specialization and trade, countries can obtain goods at a lower opportunity cost than if they produced them domestically, allowing their consumption possibilities to exceed their domestic production possibilities.

162. Selling goods in a foreign market at a price below the cost of production is known as _______.
    A) comparative advantage
    B) offshoring
    C) dumping
    D) free trade

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Dumping is a pricing strategy where a firm exports a product at a price lower than the price it normally charges in its home market or below its cost of production. It is often seen as an unfair trade practice.

163. The European Union (EU) is best described as a/an _______.
    A) Economic Union
    B) Free Trade Area
    C) Customs Union
    D) Preferential Trading Area

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The EU is a highly integrated bloc that includes a common market and has harmonized economic policies and a common currency for many of its members (the Eurozone), which are characteristics of an economic union.

164. Which of the following is a non-tariff barrier to trade?
    A) A 10% tax on imported cars
    B) Complex and lengthy customs procedures
    C) An export subsidy
    D) A specific duty per ton of imported rice

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A non-tariff barrier restricts trade through means other than explicit taxes. Complex administrative procedures act as a deterrent to importers, thus limiting trade.

165. A key difference between a Free Trade Area (FTA) and a Customs Union is the _______.
    A) free movement of labor
    B) elimination of tariffs among members
    C) adoption of a common currency
    D) presence of a common external tariff

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Both FTAs and Customs Unions have free trade among members. However, a Customs Union goes further by adopting a common external tariff for non-members, while in an FTA, each country maintains its own tariffs with the rest of the world.

166. A country has a comparative advantage in a good if it has a lower _______.
    A) absolute cost
    B) selling price
    C) opportunity cost
    D) labour cost

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The foundation of Ricardo's theory is that a country should specialize in and export the good it can produce at a lower opportunity cost (i.e., by giving up less of another good).

### **Chapter 12: Balance of Payments and Foreign Exchange (Questions 167-182)**

167. The Balance of Payments is a systematic record of all economic transactions between the residents of a country and _______.
    A) its government
    B) the rest of the world
    C) its central bank
    D) its commercial banks

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The BoP is a comprehensive record of a country's international transactions, including trade, investment, and transfers, with all other countries.

168. The export of tangible goods is recorded in the _______ account of the BoP.
    A) Financial
    B) Capital
    C) Current
    D) Official Reserves

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The Current Account records flows of goods, services, and income. The trade in goods (visible trade/merchandise trade) is a major component of the Current Account.

169. In the Balance of Payments, a transaction that leads to an inflow of foreign currency is recorded as a _______.
    A) credit
    B) debit
    C) deficit
    D) liability

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Transactions that earn foreign exchange for the country, such as exports or receiving foreign investment, are recorded as credits (+).

170. Which of the following would be a debit item in Ghana's Current Account?
    A) A Ghanaian firm exporting cocoa
    B) A foreign tourist spending money in Accra
    C) A Ghanaian citizen buying a car imported from Japan
    D) A Ghanaian working in the UK sending money home

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Importing a car from Japan requires an outflow of foreign currency from Ghana to pay the Japanese exporter, so it is recorded as a debit (-) on the Current Account (under trade in goods).

171. An exchange rate system where the currency's value is determined purely by market forces is a _______ system.
    A) fixed
    B) managed float
    C) currency board
    D) floating

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** In a pure floating (or flexible) exchange rate system, the value of the currency is determined by the interaction of demand and supply in the foreign exchange market without central bank intervention.

172. If the exchange rate between the GHS and USD changes from 1 USD = GHS 12 to 1 USD = GHS 13, the GHS has _______.
    A) appreciated
    B) depreciated
    C) been devalued
    D) been revalued

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Depreciation is a fall in the value of a currency under a floating system. It now takes more GHS to buy one USD, so the GHS is weaker. Devaluation refers to an official downward adjustment in a fixed system.

173. The part of the BoP that records purchases and sales of assets like stocks and bonds is the _______ account.
    A) Current
    B) Capital
    C) Financial
    D) Transfer

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The Financial Account records transactions involving financial assets. Portfolio investment (trade in stocks and bonds) and Foreign Direct Investment are its key components.

174. If a country's government fixes its exchange rate but market forces are pushing its value down, the central bank must _______.
    A) buy its own currency using foreign reserves
    B) sell its own currency
    C) increase the money supply
    D) lower interest rates

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** To prevent depreciation below a fixed peg, the central bank must intervene by increasing the demand for its own currency. It does this by buying its own currency in the forex market, which requires selling its foreign exchange reserves.

175. The Purchasing Power Parity (PPP) theory suggests that in the long run, exchange rates adjust to equalize _______.
    A) interest rates
    B) the prices of a common basket of goods
    C) balance of payments
    D) GDP growth rates

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** PPP is based on the law of one price and argues that the exchange rate between two currencies should reflect the relative price levels of the two countries, thus equalizing the purchasing power of each currency.

176. The International Monetary Fund (IMF) primarily provides loans to countries facing _______.
    A) long-term development challenges
    B) balance of payments difficulties
    C) the need for infrastructure projects
    D) natural disasters

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The core mandate of the IMF is to ensure the stability of the international monetary system. It provides temporary financial assistance to member countries to help them address short-term balance of payments problems.

177. The World Bank Group's primary objective is _______.
    A) exchange rate stabilization
    B) poverty reduction and long-term development
    C) crisis management
    D) regulating international trade

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The World Bank's focus is on providing long-term loans, grants, and technical assistance to developing countries to support poverty reduction and economic development projects.

178. A "dirty float" is another term for a _______ exchange rate system.
    A) fixed
    B) currency board
    C) managed float
    D) floating

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A managed float system is sometimes called a "dirty float" because the central bank "dirties" the otherwise clean market determination of the exchange rate by intervening periodically.

179. If interest rates in Nigeria rise relative to those in the USA, demand for the Naira is likely to _______.
    A) increase, causing appreciation
    B) decrease, causing depreciation
    C) increase, causing depreciation
    D) decrease, causing appreciation

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Higher relative interest rates attract short-term capital inflows (hot money) from foreign investors seeking higher returns. To invest, they must buy Naira, increasing its demand and causing its value to appreciate.

180. A deliberate downward adjustment of a currency's official value in a fixed exchange rate system is called _______.
    A) depreciation
    B) appreciation
    C) revaluation
    D) devaluation

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Devaluation is a policy action by a country's monetary authority to lower the fixed value of its currency. Depreciation is the equivalent term for a market-driven fall in value under a floating system.

181. In the BoP, an increase in a country's official reserve assets is recorded as a _______.
    A) debit
    B) credit
    C) surplus
    D) statistical discrepancy

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** An increase in reserves means the central bank has used foreign exchange earned (or its own currency) to purchase foreign assets. This is an outflow from the regular accounts into the reserve account, so it is recorded as a debit (like importing an asset).

182. Remittances sent by migrant workers are recorded in which part of the BoP?
    A) Primary Income
    B) Trade in Services
    C) Secondary Income (Current Transfers)
    D) Financial Account

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Remittances are unilateral transfers where no good or service is exchanged in return. They are therefore recorded under Secondary Income (or Current Transfers) in the Current Account.

### **Chapter 13: Concepts and Indicators of Development (Questions 183-197)**

183. The main difference between economic growth and economic development is that economic development _______.
    A) is a purely quantitative measure
    B) only considers increases in GDP
    C) includes qualitative improvements in human well-being
    D) is easier to achieve

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Economic development is a broader concept than growth. It includes not just increases in income (growth) but also improvements in health, education, equity, and overall quality of life.

184. The GINI coefficient is a measure of _______.
    A) absolute poverty
    B) GDP growth
    C) income inequality
    D) literacy rates

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The GINI coefficient (ranging from 0 to 1) is the most common statistical measure of income or wealth distribution, where a higher value signifies greater inequality.

185. A GINI coefficient of 0 represents _______.
    A) perfect inequality
    B) perfect equality
    C) maximum poverty
    D) a developing economy

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A GINI coefficient of 0 indicates that income is distributed perfectly equally among all members of the population.

186. Development that meets the needs of the present without compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs is called _______.
    A) rapid development
    B) economic growth
    C) equitable development
    D) sustainable development

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** This is the classic definition of sustainable development, emphasizing the need to balance economic, social, and environmental considerations for both present and future generations.

187. The process of shifting an economy's output and employment from agriculture to industry and then to services is known as _______.
    A) structural transformation
    B) economic growth
    C) resource management
    D) globalization

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Structural transformation (or structural change) is a key feature of economic development, describing the reallocation of economic activity across the primary, secondary, and tertiary sectors.

188. Which of the following is an example of the primary sector?
    A) Manufacturing of cars
    B) Banking services
    C) Mining of bauxite
    D) Construction of roads

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The primary sector involves the extraction of raw materials from the earth. Mining falls under this category. Car manufacturing and construction are secondary sector activities, while banking is in the tertiary sector.

189. A situation where low income leads to low savings, which leads to low investment and back to low income is known as the _______.
    A) development trap
    B) vicious circle of poverty
    C) paradox of plenty
    D) poverty gap

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The vicious circle of poverty describes a set of self-reinforcing mechanisms that cause poverty to persist across generations unless there is an external intervention.

190. The "Resource Curse" or "Paradox of Plenty" refers to the observation that countries with abundant natural resources have sometimes _______.
    A) grown faster than resource-poor countries
    B) experienced slower growth and more corruption
    C) had the highest levels of education
    D) attracted the most foreign aid

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The Resource Curse describes the paradoxical finding that an abundance of valuable natural resources can sometimes hinder, rather than help, economic development due to factors like Dutch disease, volatility, and weak governance.

191. Which of the following is NOT one of the three main dimensions of the Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI)?
    A) Health
    B) Education
    C) Living Standards
    D) Income

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The MPI is designed to measure poverty beyond just income by looking at deprivations in three key dimensions: Health (nutrition, child mortality), Education (schooling years, attendance), and Living Standards (assets, sanitation, etc.).

192. A Lorenz curve that is far bowed away from the 45-degree line of perfect equality indicates _______.
    A) a high degree of equality
    B) a low degree of inequality
    C) a high degree of inequality
    D) a high level of economic development

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The greater the area between the Lorenz curve and the line of perfect equality, the more unequal the distribution of income, and the higher the corresponding GINI coefficient.

193. As a country develops, the share of the _______ sector in GDP and employment tends to decline.
    A) agricultural
    B) industrial
    C) service
    D) private

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A universal pattern of structural transformation is the declining importance of the agricultural (primary) sector as a country develops and industrializes.

194. In the three-sector model, a developed, high-income economy is typically dominated by the _______ sector.
    A) primary
    B) secondary
    C) tertiary
    D) public

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** In mature economies, the service (tertiary) sector becomes the largest contributor to GDP and employment as incomes rise and demand for services increases.

195. Absolute poverty is often defined by a/an _______.
    A) comparison with the median income
    B) international poverty line
    C) country's GINI coefficient
    D) employment rate

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Absolute poverty is measured against a fixed standard of living, often an international poverty line set by institutions like the World Bank (e.g., living on less than $2.15 per day).

196. The Environmental Kuznets Curve hypothesis suggests that environmental degradation _______ at later stages of development.
    A) always increases
    B) may decrease
    C) remains constant
    D) is unrelated to income

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The EKC hypothesis posits an inverted U-shaped relationship, where environmental pollution initially increases with income but then declines after a certain level of development is reached.

197. All the following are examples of social capital EXCEPT _______.
    A) trust within a community
    B) networks of cooperation
    C) shared social norms
    D) machinery and equipment

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Machinery and equipment represent physical capital. Social capital refers to the intangible networks and norms that facilitate cooperation in a society.

### **Chapter 14: Strategies for Economic Development (Questions 198-212)**

198. An industrialization strategy focused on replacing imports with locally produced goods is called _______.
    A) Export-Oriented Industrialization (EOI)
    B) Import Substitution Industrialization (ISI)
    C) Free Trade Industrialization (FTI)
    D) Primary Product Exportation (PPE)

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** ISI is an inward-looking strategy that uses protectionist policies to foster domestic industries capable of producing goods that were previously imported.

199. Which of the following is a common policy tool used in an ISI strategy?
    A) Low tariffs on imported consumer goods
    B) An undervalued exchange rate
    C) High tariffs on imported consumer goods
    D) Subsidies for exports

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** High tariffs are used in ISI to make imported goods more expensive, thereby protecting domestic "infant" industries from foreign competition and encouraging local consumption.

200. A key criticism of long-term ISI strategies is that they often lead to _______.
    A) efficient and globally competitive industries
    B) a strong export sector
    C) inefficient domestic industries
    D) a fall in domestic prices

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** By shielding domestic firms from international competition, ISI often resulted in industries that were inefficient, produced high-cost or low-quality goods, and could not compete in global markets.

201. The East Asian "Tiger" economies are often cited as successful examples of which development strategy?
    A) Import Substitution Industrialization
    B) Agriculture-led development
    C) Foreign aid-led growth
    D) Export-Oriented Industrialization

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Economies like South Korea, Taiwan, and Singapore achieved rapid economic growth by focusing on manufacturing goods for export, an EOI strategy.

202. Investment made by a foreign company to acquire a lasting interest and influence in a domestic enterprise is known as _______.
    A) Foreign Direct Investment (FDI)
    B) Foreign Aid
    C) Portfolio Investment
    D) Remittances

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** FDI is distinguished by its long-term nature and the intent of the investor to have a significant degree of influence over the foreign business operation.

203. Money sent home by migrant workers abroad is known as _______.
    A) foreign aid
    B) foreign direct investment
    C) remittances
    D) portfolio investment

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Remittances are personal transfers of money from migrant workers to their families in their home countries and are a major source of foreign exchange for many developing nations.

204. Which of the following is a potential benefit of FDI for a host country?
    A) Repatriation of profits
    B) Transfer of technology
    C) Weakening of domestic firms
    D) Brain drain

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Multinational corporations often bring with them advanced technology, production techniques, and managerial skills, which can be transferred to the host country's economy.

205. The stock of knowledge, skills, health, and competencies embodied in a population is referred to as _______.
    A) physical capital
    B) financial capital
    C) social capital
    D) human capital

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Human capital is the term used to describe the productive capabilities of the labor force, which can be enhanced through investment in education and health.

206. A key reason for investing in education for economic development is that it _______.
    A) increases labor productivity
    B) guarantees employment for everyone
    C) reduces the need for physical capital
    D) is a form of foreign aid

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A more educated and skilled workforce is generally more productive, innovative, and adaptable, which is a major driver of economic growth.

207. A financial service that provides small loans to low-income individuals who lack access to formal banking is _______.
    A) microfinance
    B) venture capital
    C) public debt
    D) investment banking

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** Microfinance, particularly its component of microcredit, is designed to offer small-scale financial services to the poor to help them start businesses and improve their livelihoods.

208. An enterprise that is jointly owned and democratically controlled by its members to meet common economic needs is a/an _______.
    A) multinational corporation
    B) state-owned enterprise
    C) cooperative enterprise
    D) public limited company

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The definition perfectly describes a cooperative, which operates on principles of voluntary and open membership, democratic member control, and member economic participation.

209. Foreign aid that requires the recipient country to purchase goods and services from the donor country is called _______.
    A) untied aid
    B) multilateral aid
    C) tied aid
    D) project aid

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Tied aid is a common criticism of foreign aid, as it can reduce the effectiveness and value of the aid by restricting procurement to potentially more expensive or less suitable suppliers from the donor country.

210. An overvalued exchange rate is a policy sometimes used to support _______.
    A) EOI, by making exports cheaper
    B) ISI, by making imported capital goods cheaper
    C) ISI, by making exports more expensive
    D) EOI, by making imported consumer goods cheaper

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Under an ISI strategy, a country might maintain an overvalued exchange rate to make the importation of necessary machinery, technology, and intermediate inputs cheaper for its domestic industries.

211. Which type of external financial flow is generally considered the most stable and directly beneficial to households?
    A) Foreign Direct Investment
    B) Foreign Aid
    C) Remittances
    D) Short-term portfolio flows

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Remittances tend to be more stable than other capital flows (especially during downturns) and flow directly to households for consumption, education, and health, directly alleviating poverty.

212. A major risk of an EOI strategy is _______.
    A) the small size of the domestic market
    B) the inefficiency of protected firms
    C) vulnerability to global market fluctuations
    D) a shortage of foreign exchange

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Because EOI relies on external demand, countries pursuing this strategy are vulnerable to recessions, changes in consumer tastes, or protectionist policies in their major export markets.

### **Chapter 15: Globalization and Its Impacts (Questions 213-227)**

213. The process of increasing interconnectedness and interdependence of countries is known as _______.
    A) nationalism
    B) localization
    C) globalization
    D) regionalization

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Globalization describes the growing integration of economies and societies worldwide through the increased flow of goods, services, capital, and information.

214. Which of the following is a major driver of modern globalization?
    A) Advancements in Information and Communication Technology (ICT)
    B) An increase in trade protectionism
    C) The decline of multinational corporations
    D) A decrease in capital mobility

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The ICT revolution, particularly the internet, has been a primary force accelerating globalization by drastically reducing the costs of communication and information exchange.

215. The practice of a company contracting out some of its internal functions to a third-party provider is called _______.
    A) offshoring
    B) globalization
    C) outsourcing
    D) nationalization

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Outsourcing involves hiring an external firm to perform specific business tasks, which can be done either domestically or abroad.

216. Relocating a business process from one country to another to take advantage of lower costs is known as _______.
    A) insourcing
    B) offshoring
    C) reshoring
    D) franchising

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Offshoring specifically refers to the geographic relocation of a business operation to a different country, often for cost savings. It can be done via outsourcing or by setting up a company-owned facility.

217. A potential negative impact of globalization on developed countries is _______.
    A) a decrease in the variety of goods available
    B) job losses in low-skilled manufacturing sectors
    C) an increase in consumer prices
    D) reduced access to foreign investment

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Developed countries may see a decline in employment in sectors that cannot compete with lower-cost production in developing countries, often affecting low-skilled workers.

218. The General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade (GATT) and its successor, the WTO, have promoted globalization by _______.
    A) encouraging higher tariffs
    B) restricting capital flows
    C) working to reduce trade barriers
    D) promoting centrally planned economies

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A key role of GATT and the World Trade Organization (WTO) has been to facilitate multilateral negotiations aimed at liberalizing trade and reducing tariffs and other barriers.

219. The rapid spread of the 2008 Global Financial Crisis from the US to the rest of the world highlighted the risks of _______.
    A) financial globalization and interconnectedness
    B) trade protectionism
    C) excessive government regulation
    D) fixed exchange rates

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** The crisis demonstrated how highly integrated global financial markets can quickly transmit shocks from one major economy across the globe, a phenomenon known as contagion.

220. For a developing country, a major potential benefit of globalization is _______.
    A) increased income equality
    B) a reduction in economic vulnerability
    C) access to larger export markets
    D) greater cultural diversity

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Globalization provides developing countries with the opportunity to access markets in developed nations for their goods and services, which can be a powerful engine for export-led growth.

221. Which term describes the situation where a developing country is unable to make payments on its government debt?
    A) A debt crisis
    B) A currency crisis
    C) A liquidity trap
    D) A financial surplus

    **Answer: A**
    **Explanation:** A debt crisis occurs when a country's government lacks the resources to service its debt obligations to domestic or foreign creditors.

222. An increase in income inequality within countries is often cited as a _______ of globalization.
    A) key driver
    B) main benefit
    C) potential negative consequence
    D) primary objective

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Critics argue that globalization can worsen income inequality, as the benefits may disproportionately flow to owners of capital and high-skilled workers, while low-skilled workers face increased wage pressure.

223. The growth of short-term contract work and freelance assignments facilitated by online platforms is known as the _______.
    A) traditional economy
    B) public sector economy
    C) industrial economy
    D) gig economy

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The gig economy is a key feature of the changing nature of work, characterized by flexible, temporary, or freelance jobs, often connecting clients and workers through online platforms.

224. A key reason a company might choose to outsource is to _______.
    A) increase control over all business functions
    B) focus on its core competencies
    C) hire more permanent employees
    D) reduce its access to specialized expertise

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** By outsourcing non-essential functions, a company can free up resources and management attention to concentrate on the core activities that give it a competitive advantage.

225. A rise in global interest rates can increase the risk of a debt crisis for a developing country because it _______.
    A) reduces the value of its exports
    B) increases the cost of servicing its existing debt
    C) decreases its GDP
    D) encourages foreign investment

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** If a country has debt with variable interest rates or needs to issue new debt, a rise in global interest rates increases the amount it must pay in interest, straining its finances.

226. If the domestic currency depreciates sharply, the real burden of foreign-currency-denominated debt _______.
    A) decreases
    B) remains the same
    C) increases
    D) becomes zero

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** When the domestic currency depreciates, it takes more units of the local currency to pay back a debt that is fixed in a foreign currency (e.g., US dollars), thereby increasing the real cost and burden of the debt.

227. A primary cause of the 2008 Global Financial Crisis was the _______.
    A) collapse of the dot-com bubble
    B) US subprime mortgage crisis
    C) Asian financial crisis
    D) increase in oil prices

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The GFC originated with widespread defaults on subprime mortgages in the US housing market, which then spread through the global financial system via complex securities.

### **Chapter 16: Environmental Economics and Resource Management (Questions 228-250)**

228. A situation where the free market fails to allocate resources efficiently is known as _______.
    A) market equilibrium
    B) perfect competition
    C) market failure
    D) consumer sovereignty

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Market failure occurs when the pursuit of individual self-interest leads to a collectively inefficient outcome. Environmental problems are a classic example.

229. Pollution from a factory that harms a nearby community is an example of a _______.
    A) positive externality
    B) negative externality
    C) public good
    D) private good

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** A negative externality is an uncompensated cost imposed on a third party. The pollution imposes costs (e.g., health problems, cleanup costs) on the community that are not reflected in the factory's market price.

230. A good that is both non-rivalrous in consumption and non-excludable is a _______.
    A) private good
    B) public good
    C) common resource
    D) club good

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** These two characteristics—non-rivalry (one person's use doesn't prevent another's) and non-excludability (it's hard to stop people from using it)—define a public good.

231. The overfishing of open oceans is a classic example of the _______.
    A) free-rider problem
    B) tragedy of the commons
    C) principle of comparative advantage
    D) paradox of value

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The tragedy of the commons describes the over-exploitation of a shared resource that is rivalrous but non-excludable. Each individual has an incentive to take as much as they can, leading to the depletion of the resource (the fish stock).

232. Because of the free-rider problem, public goods are often _______.
    A) overproduced by the private market
    B) underproduced or not produced by the private market
    C) produced only by monopolies
    D) sold at very high prices

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** The free-rider problem makes it unprofitable for private firms to supply public goods, as they cannot easily charge consumers for them. This leads to underprovision by the market, justifying government provision.

233. A tax placed on a polluting activity to make the polluter pay the full social cost is called a/an _______.
    A) income tax
    B) ad valorem tax
    C) Pigouvian tax
    D) sales tax

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A Pigouvian tax is a tax on any market activity that generates negative externalities (e.g., a carbon tax). The tax is intended to correct the inefficient market outcome by "internalizing" the externality.

234. In the case of a positive externality, the marginal social benefit is _______ the marginal private benefit.
    A) less than
    B) equal to
    C) greater than
    D) unrelated to

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** For a positive externality like education or vaccination, the benefits to society as a whole (social benefit) are greater than the benefits received by the individual consumer (private benefit).

235. The environmental valuation method that estimates value based on what people spend to visit a recreational site is the _______.
    A) hedonic pricing method
    B) contingent valuation method
    C) travel cost method
    D) defensive expenditure method

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The travel cost method uses the costs incurred by visitors (travel time, fuel, accommodation) as a proxy for the price of accessing a recreational environmental good.

236. A system where the government sets a limit on total pollution and allows firms to trade pollution permits is known as _______.
    A) a carbon tax
    B) a subsidy system
    C) cap-and-trade
    D) environmental regulation

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Cap-and-trade (or an Emissions Trading System) is a market-based approach that sets a cap on emissions and creates a market for tradable allowances, providing an incentive for firms to reduce pollution at the lowest cost.

237. Cost-Benefit Analysis (CBA) is a tool used to _______.
    A) calculate a firm's profit
    B) compare the total social costs and benefits of a project
    C) set interest rates
    D) measure GDP

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** CBA provides a systematic framework for evaluating public projects or policies by identifying, monetizing, and comparing all associated costs and benefits to society.

238. From an economic perspective, climate change is a market failure because greenhouse gas emissions are a _______.
    A) positive externality
    B) club good
    C) private good
    D) global negative externality

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** GHG emissions create costs (climate damages) that are felt globally and by future generations, while the emitters do not bear these costs, making it a perfect example of a global negative externality.

239. An example of a non-renewable resource is _______.
    A) forests
    B) fish stocks
    C) coal
    D) solar energy

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Coal is a fossil fuel that exists in a finite stock and cannot be replenished on a human timescale. Forests and fish are renewable (if managed sustainably), and solar energy is effectively inexhaustible.

240. The harvest of a renewable resource at a rate that does not exceed its natural rate of regeneration is called _______.
    A) maximum yield
    B) economic yield
    C) sustainable yield
    D) primary yield

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Sustainable yield is the key principle for managing renewable resources. Harvesting at or below this level ensures the resource stock can be maintained for future use.

241. A subsidy given to a university to encourage higher education is a policy response to a _______.
    A) negative externality
    B) positive externality
    C) public good
    D) tragedy of the commons

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Education generates positive externalities (benefits to society beyond the individual student). A subsidy can help align the private incentive with the social benefit, encouraging more people to pursue education, moving consumption closer to the socially optimal level.

242. A major challenge in using Cost-Benefit Analysis for environmental projects is _______.
    A) calculating a firm's revenue
    B) the ease of identifying all costs
    C) the difficulty in placing a monetary value on non-market goods
    D) the lack of government interest

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** A key difficulty of CBA is accurately monetizing environmental benefits (like clean air or biodiversity) and costs (like the loss of an ecosystem), which requires controversial valuation methods.

243. Fish stocks in the open ocean are an example of a common resource because they are _______ and _______.
    A) rivalrous; excludable
    B) non-rivalrous; non-excludable
    C) non-rivalrous; excludable
    D) rivalrous; non-excludable

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** A fish caught by one person is no longer available for another (rivalrous), but it is difficult to prevent anyone from fishing in the open ocean (non-excludable). These two characteristics define a common resource and lead to the tragedy of the commons.

244. The Contingent Valuation Method asks people their _______ for an environmental good.
    A) actual expenditure
    B) travel cost
    C) willingness to pay
    D) marginal utility

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Contingent valuation is a survey-based method that creates a hypothetical market and directly asks respondents their "willingness to pay" (WTP) for an environmental benefit or "willingness to accept" (WTA) compensation for a loss.

245. According to the Coase theorem, private bargaining can solve externality problems if property rights are well-defined and _______.
    A) government intervenes
    B) transaction costs are high
    C) the externality is large
    D) transaction costs are low

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The Coase theorem suggests that as long as property rights are clear and transaction costs are negligible, affected parties can bargain to an efficient solution without government intervention.

246. National defense is often cited as a pure public good because it is _______.
    A) very expensive
    B) provided by the government
    C) non-rivalrous and non-excludable
    D) a response to a negative externality

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** The protection of one citizen by the national defense system does not reduce the protection available to others (non-rivalrous), and it is impossible to exclude any single citizen from this protection (non-excludable).

247. The Paris Agreement is an international accord aimed at addressing _______.
    A) the tragedy of the commons in fisheries
    B) global debt crises
    C) trade protectionism
    D) climate change

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** The Paris Agreement, under the UNFCCC, is a landmark international treaty on climate change, with the goal of limiting global warming and providing a framework for countries to reduce their GHG emissions.

248. Hotelling's Rule is a principle that applies to the management of _______.
    A) renewable resources
    B) non-renewable resources
    C) public goods
    D) human capital

    **Answer: B**
    **Explanation:** Hotelling's Rule concerns the efficient depletion of a non-renewable resource, suggesting that its price (net of extraction cost) should rise over time at the rate of interest.

249. Which of the following best describes the Social Cost of a good?
    A) The cost paid by the producer
    B) The price paid by the consumer
    C) Private cost plus external cost
    D) Private cost minus external cost

    **Answer: C**
    **Explanation:** Social cost is the total cost to society of producing a good. It is calculated by adding the private costs borne by the producer to any external costs imposed on third parties.

250. Promoting the use of solar and wind energy is a strategy aimed at achieving _______.
    A) greater dependence on fossil fuels
    B) short-term economic growth
    C) economic self-sufficiency
    D) environmental sustainability

    **Answer: D**
    **Explanation:** Shifting to renewable energy sources like solar and wind reduces greenhouse gas emissions and reliance on depletable fossil fuels, which are core objectives of environmental sustainability.

**Explanation:** While human wants in general are unlimited, a specific want (like hunger) can be satisfied for a certain period, which is the characteristic of being satiable.

Chapter 3: Perfect Competition and Monopoly (Questions 283-297)

In a perfectly competitive market, the relationship between price (P), average revenue (AR), and marginal revenue (MR) for an individual firm is _______.
A) P > AR > MR
B) P = AR = MR
C) P = AR > MR
D) P < AR < MR

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Since a perfectly competitive firm is a price taker and can sell any quantity at the constant market price, the price, the average revenue per unit, and the marginal revenue from an extra unit are all equal.

**Explanation:** X-inefficiency refers to organizational slack and higher-than-necessary costs due to a lack of competitive pressure. A monopolist, sheltered by barriers to entry, is most susceptible to this.

**Explanation:** The "kink" in the demand curve of the kinked-demand curve model creates a vertical gap or discontinuity in the corresponding marginal revenue curve. As long as MC intersects MR in this gap, price remains stable.

The ordinal utility approach assumes that utility can be _______.

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The ordinal approach, which uses indifference curves, is considered more realistic because it only requires consumers to be able to rank their preferences (e.g., prefer A to B, or be indifferent) without needing to assign specific numerical values ("utils") to them.

If a consumer's MRS_xy = 3 and the price ratio (P_x / P_y) = 2, the consumer should _______ to maximize utility.
A) buy more of Y and less of X
B) buy more of X and less of Y
C) not change their consumption
D) buy only good X

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Here, the rate at which the consumer is *willing* to trade Y for X (3) is greater than the rate at which the market *requires* them to (2). This means they value an extra unit of X more than it costs in terms of Y, so they should consume more X and less Y until MRS = P_x / P_y.

content_copy
download
Use code with caution.
IGNORE_WHEN_COPYING_END

For an inferior good, the income effect of a price rise is _______.
A) positive
B) negative
C) zero
D) ambiguous

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A price rise decreases real income. For an inferior good, a decrease in income leads to an *increase* in quantity demanded. Therefore, the income effect of a price rise is negative (i.e., it works to increase quantity, opposing the substitution effect). Note: Some texts define a negative effect as one that moves quantity in the opposite direction of the income change, making this confusing. The key is it works in the opposite direction to the income effect for a *normal* good. A price rise (which reduces real income) makes the consumer buy *more* of an inferior good due to the income effect.

**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** For a necessity, as income rises, consumption increases, but by a smaller and smaller proportion (YED < 1). This is represented by an Engel curve that slopes upwards but gets flatter.

A) total fixed cost is falling
B) total fixed cost is being spread over more units of output

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** AFC = TFC / Q. Since Total Fixed Cost (TFC) is constant, dividing it by an increasing quantity (Q) results in a continuously decreasing value.

B) 8
C) 5
D) 5.08

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Marginal Cost = ΔTC / ΔQ. The change in total cost is 508 - 500 = 8. The change in quantity is 101 - 100 = 1. So, MC = 8/1 = 8.

D) none of the monetary aggregates

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The definition of M2 is M1 plus "near money" assets like savings deposits and small-denomination time deposits, which are slightly less liquid than the components of M1.

Which tax is regressive in its impact?
A) A progressive income tax
B) A flat tax of 20% on all income
C) A specific tax of GHS 5 on every loaf of bread sold
D) A corporate profit tax

**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** A specific tax on a basic commodity like bread takes a larger proportion of income from a low-income person than from a high-income person, making its impact regressive.

A government is running a revenue deficit if its _______.
A) total expenditure exceeds its total receipts
B) revenue expenditure exceeds its revenue receipts
C) capital expenditure exceeds its capital receipts
D) total borrowing exceeds its interest payments

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A revenue deficit specifically means the government's day-to-day (recurrent) spending is greater than its regular income (revenue receipts). This implies the government is borrowing to finance current consumption, which is generally considered undesirable.

The primary deficit is calculated as the _______.
A) Fiscal Deficit - Interest Payments
B) Fiscal Deficit + Interest Payments
C) Revenue Deficit - Fiscal Deficit
D) Total Debt - External Debt

**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The primary deficit shows the borrowing requirement of the government, excluding the interest payments on past debts. It is a measure of the current year's fiscal imbalance.

All of the following are functions of money EXCEPT _______.
A) generating unlimited profit
B) being a medium of exchange
C) being a store of value
D) being a unit of account

**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** Generating profit is a goal of a business, not a function of money itself. The other three options are the core economic functions of money.

Raising margin requirements on loans for stock purchases is an example of _______.
A) expansionary fiscal policy
B) quantitative monetary control
C) contractionary fiscal policy
D) qualitative/selective credit control

**Answer: D**
**Explanation:** Qualitative or selective credit controls aim to influence the direction of credit rather than its overall volume. Raising margin requirements makes it harder to borrow for a specific purpose (speculating on stocks), thus channeling credit away from that area.

In modern banking, the rate at which the central bank absorbs liquidity from commercial banks is the _______.
A) Reverse Repo Rate
B) Repo Rate
C) Discount Rate
D) Bank Rate

**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** The Reverse Repo Rate is the rate at which commercial banks can park their excess funds with the central bank. The central bank uses it as a tool to absorb excess liquidity from the system.
